(GetName/GetArgCount/GetParamType in one shared 2869-slot vtable) + a generic
dispatcher; some API names (Vessel_Attack, BindEasy) are Lua helpers, not C.

float, 0xc string, 0xd name/key-string) the dispatcher uses to marshal args.
Section 22 = a NEGATIVE RESULT: a naive full 398-signature auto-dump is unreliable

Section 23 = REFINEMENT: the 398 fns are methods of ONE big class (vtable
@0x5f70c8, 2869 slots ~= 398x7); registration ctor @0x4a8aa0 -- entry points for next session.

string/base) -> PROVEN the game API is not luaL_reg-registered; ctor 0x4a8aa0 /
dtor 0x4a8c60; GetName sits at vtable index 1864, reached only indirectly.

1. PROJECT GOAL & CONSTRAINTS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Goal: Build modding tools for AquaNox 1 (KRASS engine, Massive Development,
2001) from scratch (none exist publicly for scripting), and create a new
single-player "Instant Fight" level on the multiplayer map mp_06_dm
("Narrow"), with player ship, weapons, music, and enemies with REAL AI.

Explicit user decision ("Weg A" vs "Weg B"):
  - Weg B (transplant the whole fightclub script graph wholesale) = REJECTED.
  - Weg A (understand the framework, write/graft only the minimal necessary
    logic) = CHOSEN. Keep everything minimal and understandable.

Workflow: The user uploads the game's PAK files to the chat sandbox
(/mnt/user-data/uploads/aquanox0.pak .. aquanox6.pak, plus Aqua.exe).
Claude builds Python tools in /home/claude, produces a MODIFIED aquanox6.pak
(higher-numbered PAKs override lower ones), and the user playtests on a real
game install and reports back. Debug: run `Aqua.exe -logfile`.

IMPORTANT: /home/claude is wiped between sessions. All tools must be
recreated from the sources embedded in section 8 of this document. The
uploaded PAKs must be re-extracted (takes ~1 minute, ~900 MB total).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. FILE FORMATS (ALL FULLY REVERSE-ENGINEERED AND ROUND-TRIP VERIFIED)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2.1 PAK ("MASSIVEFILE") container
  Header: magic "MASSIVEFILE\0" (12 B) | uint32 version (=3) |
          uint32 file_count | 60 B copyright (zero-padded) | "LPT\0".
  Directory: file_count entries of 0x84 bytes each:
          128 B encrypted filename + 4 B encrypted size.
  Data:   all file bodies concatenated sequentially right after directory.
  Filename decryption (j = entry index): dec[i] = enc[i] - KEY[(j+i) % 64].
  Size decryption: size = abs( LE_uint32(KEY[j % 60 .. +3]) - size_enc ).
  Encryption for packing is the exact inverse (add instead of subtract;
  size_enc = (key_uint + size) & 0xFFFFFFFF).
  64-byte XOR/ADD KEY (hex):
    683c61374c6cc44f6f727848334a2b78dcdf61624b6e296a736c6e446f4a4466
    684433376655674f6fd67848335832783541613551376e2af66c2bfc6f4a2340
  Verified: repacking pak6 from extracted files is BYTE-IDENTICAL.
  Precedence: higher-numbered PAKs override lower ones -> mods ship as a
  modified aquanox6.pak (original pak6 contains only 10 files).

2.2 Cooked DES/OSD ("~.des", "~.osd") config tree
  Binary pre-order tree of Header and Entry nodes, linked by absolute file
  offsets (int32, -1 = end of chain).
  Header: uint16 key_len | 6 B unknown (preserve verbatim) |
          int32 first_child_off | int32 next_sibling_off |
          int32 first_entry_off | key bytes (incl. NUL).
  Entry:  4 B (vtype, key_len, val_len, pad) | int32 next_entry_off |
          key bytes | value bytes (verbatim; strings incl. NUL terminator,
          ints stored as raw little-endian int32 bytes!).
  Round-trip verified on all 1367 cooked files (byte-identical).
  New nodes: set orig_off=None -> serializer appends them at file end and
  rewires all offset chains. Keys/values must be BYTES with \x00 terminator
  for strings; e.g. numofentry value = (5).to_bytes(4,'little').

2.3 Compiled Lua scripts (".sco")
  AquaNox scripting = Lua 4.0 PRECOMPILED chunks (lundump format).
  Header (16 B): "\x1bLua\x40" + bytes(1,4,4,4,0x20,6,9,8) + test number
  3.14159265358979323846e8 as little-endian double.
  Function record (recursive): source string | int32 line | int32 numparams |
  byte is_vararg | int32 maxstack | locals (n * [string,int32,int32]) |
  lineinfo (int32 count + raw) | kstr (string constants) | knum (doubles) |
  kproto (child functions, recursive) | code (int32 count + uint32 words).
  Strings serialize as uint32 length + bytes INCLUDING trailing NUL
  (length 0 = null string).
  Instruction encoding (32-bit): opcode = bits [0..5];
  U-arg = bits [6..31]; A = bits [15..31] (POS_A=15); B = bits [6..14]
  (POS_B=6, 9 bits); signed S-arg = U - MAXARG_S (MAXARG_S = (2^26-1)>>1).
  Opcode list (index = opcode number) is in the lua4dis.py source below.
  Key opcode indices used when emitting code by hand:
    CALL=2 PUSHNIL=4 PUSHINT=6 PUSHSTRING=7 PUSHNUM=8 PUSHNEGNUM=9
    GETGLOBAL=12 GETDOTTED=14 CREATETABLE=17 SETGLOBAL=19 SETTABLE=20
    SETMAP=22 CLOSURE=48
  "t.key = value" statement pattern: GETGLOBAL t; PUSHSTRING key;
  <push value>; SETTABLE A=3 B=3 (raw word 98516).
  Round-trip verified on all 97 .sco files (byte-identical).
  Reference: Lua 4.0 C source cloned to /home/claude/lua-4.0
  (github.com/lua/lua tag v4.0) — clone again if needed.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. GAME SCRIPTING ARCHITECTURE (VERIFIED FINDINGS)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3.1 Mission script layout
  Each map has one .sco, e.g. ext/pak4/map/mp_06_dm/script/dm_narrow.sco.
  - main chunk: defines constants True/False and IFF constants F/E/N/U,
    assigns library tables (VesselAttackLib, DipolLib, MiscLib, ...; these
    globals are set by every mission's main), defines behavior-object
    templates (S_oXXXX tables of closures) and instance tables (oXXXX),
    and defines the functions __InitLayout (= kproto[1] world build, huge
    linear function) and __StartUp.
  - The engine runs main, then calls __StartUp.
  - __StartUp: calls __InitLayout(), binds the script timer
    (G_ScriptTimerThread = BindEasy(Node_Find("/"), "/script",
    C_ScriptTimer())), then BindEasy's every scripted node, then for some
    objects calls oXXXX.Start() (these are auxiliary condition objects,
    NOT the bound wrappers).
  dm_narrow.sco: 61 kprotos. kproto[1] = world build (f1). kproto[60] =
  __StartUp (params=0, locals=0 -> statement CALLs use stack base 0,
  i.e. "GETGLOBAL fn; <args>; CALL 0 0"). dm_narrow defines True/False/
  F/E/N/U and all Lib globals, and its __StartUp has the C_ScriptTimer /
  G_ScriptTimerThread bind. Scene tree globals in f1:
    Scenario_Dynamic = node69; its Navigation = node71; its Object = node90.

3.2 glue.sco library (ext/pak3/dat/script/lib/glue.sco) — key functions
  BindEasy(scenarioNode, name, stateTable):
    creates a NEW wrapper table t; BindThread(scenario, name, t) sets
    t.State=nil, t.TypeId=666, t.__UniqueID=Script_BindThread(node),
    t.Node=node, registers t in __ThreadTable; then t.CommandTable =
    stateTable; returns t. NOTE: BindThread does NOT call Start in Lua —
    the ENGINE calls the object's Start automatically after
    Script_BindThread (empirically proven: V6 toiler despawned via Code0
    without any explicit Start call).
  CallFunction(objOrThread, fname, param): resolves the function through
    the wrapper's State/CommandTable at runtime and calls it.
  SetEnemyMatrixElement(a, b, const) -> Game_SetEnemyMatrixElement(
    GetGameNode(), a, b, const). THE IFF MECHANISM, see 3.4.
  StartEnemyPatrol(o, ...) -> Body_InformEnemyPatrol(o.Node, ...).

3.3 Enemy AI = behavior objects (the heart of the matter)
  Per scripted vessel there is a template table S_oXXXX with closures:
  Start, Code0..Code4, SetStateValue_Destroyed, CheckCondition,
  ProcesseStateChange. The engine drives it via the bound wrapper.
  Fightclub toiler chain (inst_fightclub.sco, 362 top-level protos;
  the toiler subsystem = protos 151..165, ZERO internal closures/children,
  cleanly transplantable):
    Start (f151): CodeIndex=0; CallFunction(self.Thread,"Code0")
    Code0 (f152): Node_LeaveSimulation(self.Node); chain Code1
      -> vessels DESPAWN first by design (staging), then re-enter later!
    Code1 (f153): proceeds to Code2 only if CheckCondition==1
    CheckCondition (f158): returns 0 IFF CodeIndex in [1,2) AND
      o1761.Value ~= True; otherwise always returns 1. o1761 is a small
      "calculator" object (protos 160..163) whose Value is set by OTHER
      subsystems in fightclub (a web of cross-references).
    Code2 (f154): DipolLib.InUtero_DipolIn(self,"Code3") — the teleport
      spawn FX, then chains Code3.
    Code3 (f155): VesselAttackLib.GuardArea(self, 0,
      "/Scenario_Dynamic/Navigation/PatrolArea_1" (x3), "E", "", "", "",
      1, "Code4")  — the actual combat behavior.
  o1764 (protos 164..165) is an auxiliary starter that references FOREIGN
  objects o1809/o2018/o2025 (other vessels' calculators) — must NOT be
  started when transplanting just the toiler (nil deref crash).
  GuardArea implementation (vesselattack.sco): a state machine using
  StoreInnerValue/ApplyModification(LVA_ClearArea / LVA_GotoAttack) +
  CallFunction(self.Thread,"LVA_Activate"); bottoms out in engine calls
  Vessel_Patrol / StartEnemyPatrol / Vessel_AttackPatrol /
  Vessel_AttackTurrets. There is NO simple engine one-liner like
  "make X attack Y" — the behavior object IS the AI.

3.4 IFF / EnemyMatrix (CRITICAL, was mis-analyzed twice)
  Every mission main sets a 16x16 matrix via SetEnemyMatrixElement(a,b,C)
  where C is one of the GLOBAL constants F (friend), E (enemy), N (neutral).
  Fightclub sets among others: (2,4,E), (4,2,E), (2,2,F), (1,4,E), (3,4,E).
  Player FoF group = 2 (Body_SetFriendOrFoeID(playerNode, 2)), scripted
  enemies = 4. WITHOUT matrix E entries: enemies show as blue "???", never
  attack, and killing them can trigger mission-fail (neutral-kill penalty).
  TWO earlier scanner bugs to beware of: (1) first scan drowned in output;
  (2) second scan's argument collector SKIPPED GETGLOBAL operands, so the
  E/F/N constants (passed as globals!) were dropped -> false conclusion
  "everything Neutral". A correct collector must handle PUSHINT,
  PUSHSTRING, AND GETGLOBAL.

3.5 Music
  Game_LoadMusic(node0, "sfx/sample/....sam", slotIndex) loads a track.
  Game_PlayMusic(GetGameNode(), SLOTNAME) plays it — the second argument
  is a SLOT NAME string, not a file path! Names used across all scripts:
  "track3" (63x), "agressive" (60x), "atmo" (48x), "track4", "track5".
   Passing the file path fails silently (this was the no-music bug V3-V7).
  SLOT NAMES ARE ENGINE-FIXED to indices: 0<->"atmo", 1<->"agressive",
  3<->"track3" (also "track4"/"track5"). LoadMusic(node0,file,INDEX) loads a
  file into a slot; PlayMusic(GetGameNode(),NAME) plays the file in that slot.
  CRUCIAL PLACEMENT (root cause of V8 silence): LoadMusic goes in the world
  build (f1) like every map does, but PlayMusic must run at GAMEPLAY time, NOT
  during world build. V8 called PlayMusic at the end of f1 -> silent. V9 FIX
  (verified working): keep LoadMusic in f1 (slot 1, sfx/sample/music/
  music_track04.sam), and append PlayMusic(GetGameNode(),"agressive") at the
  END of __StartUp (after all binding = the moment the level goes live). This
  is the proven inst_succubus/dogfight pairing. NB: campaign maps trigger
  PlayMusic from auto-started director objects; __StartUp-end also works.
  Music files on disk are cooked as "...~.sam"; scripts reference them WITHOUT
  the ~ (engine adds it). Both sfx/sample/music/<f>~.sam and some
  sfx/sample/<f>~.sam exist.
  V8 uses: LoadMusic(node0, "sfx/sample/music_track02.sam", 3) +
  PlayMusic(GetGameNode(), "track3").

3.6 Menu integration
  dat/menu/instantfightlist~.des (from pak3) holds the Instant Fight menu:
  [HEAD][instantfight] numofentry=<int32-bytes> + entryN {fightname, path}.
  Our mod adds entry4: fightname "< Narrow >", path
  "map\mp_06_dm\script\sp_narrow" (backslashes, no .sco extension),
  numofentry -> 5. Ship the modified file inside aquanox6.pak at
  dat\menu\instantfightlist~.des.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4. THE CROSS-CHUNK GRAFT TECHNIQUE (CODE TRANSPLANT BETWEEN .sco FILES)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
To copy an instruction slice from fightclub into dm_narrow you must remap
constants and closure indices:
  graft_slice(srcProtoOrChunk, lo, hi, targetProtoOrChunk, pmap):
    for each instruction word i in src.code[lo:hi]:
      op in {GETGLOBAL, SETGLOBAL, PUSHSTRING, GETDOTTED, PUSHSELF,
             SETDOTTED}: re-index the string constant into the TARGET's
             own kstr pool (append if missing; also append raw bytes with
             NUL to kstr_raw!).
      op in {PUSHNUM, PUSHNEGNUM}: likewise into target.knum.
      op == CLOSURE: new_word = (i & 0x3F) | (pmap[old_proto_idx] << POS_A)
             (upvalue count B is 0 for these standalone protos).
      else: copy verbatim.
  Transplanted protos are appended via copy.deepcopy to target.kproto.
  CRITICAL BUG CLASS (hit once): the TARGET whose kstr pool you write into
  must be the SAME proto whose .code receives the instructions. A slice
  grafted into __StartUp (kproto[60]) must remap into kproto[60]'s pools,
  NOT into the main chunk's pools — otherwise indices point at unrelated
  strings and the code silently does nonsense.
  Toiler transplant mapping used: fightclub protos 151..165 -> dm protos
  61..75 (BASE=61, PMAP = {151+k: 61+k}).
  Template/instance build slice in fightclub main: code[1819:1858]
  (CREATETABLE + PUSHSTRING/CLOSURE pairs + SETMAP for S_o1759, o1761,
  o1764). __StartUp bind slice: fc.kproto[361].code[429:437] =
  BindEasy(Node_Find("/Scenario_Dynamic/Object"), "gen_toiler_inst_1",
  S_o1759) -> SETGLOBAL o1759.  (fc __StartUp = fc.kproto[361].)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5. VERSION HISTORY (WHAT WAS TRIED, WHAT EACH TEST SHOWED)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
V1/V2: menu entry works, empty map + player spawn. Player flies. OK.
V3: weapons OK (Body_AddItem list below), music silent (wrong PlayMusic
    arg, later understood), enemies missing.
V4/V5: enemies spawned as plain nodes (Node_CreateNode nod_vessel + OSD +
    FoF 4 + EnterSimulation) -> visible but STATIC, blue "???", and
    killing one triggered instant game-over. Lesson: without a bound
    behavior object there is NO AI at all; FoF alone does nothing.
V6: grafted the toiler AI subsystem (protos 151..165 + main template
    slice + __StartUp bind + o1764.Start()). Result: toiler VANISHED
    entirely. Diagnosis: engine auto-calls Start after Script_BindThread;
    Code0 ran LeaveSimulation; Code1 blocked forever because
    o1761.Value != True (the releasing objects weren't transplanted);
    additionally o1764.Start() would crash on nil o1809.
V7: set o1761.Value = True in main graft; REMOVED o1764.Start() from the
    __StartUp graft. Result (user-confirmed): toiler spawns and PATROLS
    (GuardArea alive!) but still blue "???", no attack, still no music.
V8 (VERIFIED WORKING, user-playtested 2026-07-06): added to main graft:
    SetEnemyMatrixElement(2,4,E); (4,2,E); (2,2,F)   [5-instr pattern
    each: GETGLOBAL wrapper; PUSHINT a; PUSHINT b; GETGLOBAL const;
    CALL 0 0], and set music to slot 3 + PlayMusic("track3").
    CONFIRMED RESULT: toiler is generated, patrols, is HOSTILE (shown RED /
    "three red question marks", not blue). Player can fight and destroy it;
    it explodes. NO game-over animation on kill; player keeps moving after.
    => full stack works: menu -> level -> player -> weapons -> enemy spawn
    (dipol FX) -> GuardArea patrol -> IFF hostile -> combat/kill. The core
    problem (enemies with real, hostile, killable AI) is SOLVED.
    Music: the slot-3 / "track3" approach was shipped; user reported no
    music through V7 and did not separately confirm audio in V8 -- if music

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6b. WAVE MODE (V10-V16) -- ARCHITECTURE & THE THREE HARD-WON MECHANISMS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Builder: build_narrow_waves.py (supersedes build_sp_narrow_v6.py for the wave
level; the V9 single-toiler builder remains the minimal reference).

Design (V16, VERIFIED):
- 4 template copies of fightclub protos 151..159 grafted (tags 1..4); the
  per-instance global o1759 is RENAMED to o1759_<tag> inside each copy's kstr
  (Code0-3 reference it; without rename, multiple vessels steer each other).
- Shared gate o1761={Value=True}; shared counter object o1764={SetStateValue=
  <hand-built wave closure>}. G_Kills=0 in main.
- f1 builds player (+weapons incl. device_repair), PatrolArea_1, THREE entered
  toilers. NO un-entered ("dangling") vessel nodes in f1 -- a created-but-not-
  entered nod_vessel in the world build CRASHES THE LEVEL LOAD (V10 lesson).
- __StartUp: BindEasy each toiler -> SETGLOBAL o1759_N -> SetEngineValue(
  o1759_N, "Destroyed", 1); then PlayMusic("agressive").
- Wave closure (o1764.SetStateValue, params=1): G_Kills=G_Kills+1;
  if ==3: create phobocaster ENTIRELY at runtime (Node_CreateNode nod_vessel,
  AddSon, Body_SetCS with TWO MAT_Vector3 (CALL a,1 / a+1,255 / base,0 --
  copy this encoding EXACTLY; a hand-rolled Body_SetPosition variant crashed),
  ParseIniFile osd/gen/gen_phobocaster_inst.osd, FoF 4, NameKey/CargoKey -1,
  EnterSimulation, BindEasy -> o1759_4, SetEngineValue Destroyed);
  if ==4: PlayMusic("track3") + Game_MissionEnd(GetGameNode(), 1).
- Victory fanfare: f1 loads music_fightclub_won.sam into slot 3 ("track3").
- Runtime spawning inside a state callback WORKS (engine auto-starts the
  bound object on the next tick; the despawn->dipol-in chain runs fine).

THE THREE MECHANISMS THAT COST V10-V15 (memorize these):
(A) DEATH EVENTS NEED REGISTRATION. The engine only calls a bound object's
    SetStateValue_Destroyed if the object was registered via
    SetEngineValue(boundObj, "Destroyed", 1) (pattern from sensor.sco f7;
    glue/dipol/misc register other events the same way: Born, Entered,
    UnderAttack, Triggered, ...). Without it, kills are silently ignored.
(B) CALL BASE = numparams. In Lua 4.0 bytecode, params occupy stack slots
    0..n-1; a statement call in a proto with 1 param must be CALL A=1
    (proof: original o1764.SetStateValue uses CALL 1 0 throughout). Using
    base 0 calls the proto's own PARAMETER -> crash or silent script abort.
    Same class of bug hit twice (v12 SetEngineValue in __StartUp had the
    inverse mistake). build_narrow_waves.py now contains a STACK SIMULATOR
    that verifies every CALL's A against simulated stack depth -- run it
    after any hand-emitted bytecode change.
(C) Game_MissionEnd(GetGameNode(), 1) = SUCCESS; 0 = FAILURE ("Mission
    gescheitert"). Empirically proven in-game; all four instant-fight maps
    end with MissionEnd(1). (Campaign f10-style handlers with parameter 0 +
    "track3" music are the FAIL path; note fightclub loads music_badend
    into its "agressive" slot -- slot names say nothing about content.)

Item name for the repair kit: "device_repair" (tiers device_repair1..3 exist);
full Body_AddItem item-name list can be regenerated by scanning for
Body_AddItem's first PUSHSTRING argument across all .sco files.

Version history (ZIPs aquanox6_v<N>.zip in outputs; see also
aquanox_narrow_versionen.txt): v10 load-crash (dangling node) -> v11 IFF
overrides restored -> v12 Destroyed-registration added but broke __StartUp
(CALL base) -> v13 fixed that, crash at kill 3 -> v14 Body_SetCS pattern,
silent fail -> v15 closure CALL bases fixed, phobo appears -> v16 SUCCESS
(MissionEnd 1 + repair kit + victory fanfare). V16 pak = 1,206,647 bytes.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6b-1. CAMPAIGN LEVEL AS INSTANT-FIGHT ARENA (sp_1n1) -- VERIFIED WORKING
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Goal: reuse a CAMPAIGN level's scene (terrain/structures/atmosphere) but replace
its mission with our wave combat -- ONLY in Instant Fight, campaign untouched.
Tool: build_sp_1n1.py (adaptation of build_narrow_waves.py). Menu entry
"< 1N1: Kampf >" -> map\1n1\script\sp_1n1 (a SEPARATE file in the same folder;
the campaign's 1n1.sco is never touched / never in our pak).

METHOD (per campaign level):
  * __InitLayout = proto[1]: the scene builder. It creates EVERYTHING incl. the
    campaign enemies + player. The combat objects (7 vessels + player in 1n1) are
    a CONTIGUOUS instruction block (1n1: PC [2889,3310), 421 instr) -> excise it,
    keep terrain/structures/atmosphere/nav, then append OUR player+toilers+patrol
    +music (using the level's OWN coordinates, read from its nod_player/nod_vessel
    Body_SetCS calls -- guaranteed valid positions).
  * __StartUp = proto[186] (last proto, "CLOSURE ... SETGLOBAL __StartUp"): rebuild
    it, but PRESERVE the essential engine bootstrap (see 4 lessons below), then
    bind only our 3 toilers.
  * Then graft the wave templates + wave closure exactly like build_narrow_waves.

THE 4 DEBUGGING LESSONS (a black screen showing only a small character portrait =
"loading screen never dismissed"; ESC returns to menu = engine alive, SCRIPT dead):

  (1) PROGRESS BOOTSTRAP (campaign-only!). Campaign __StartUp runs:
        Game_LoadProgress_Enter(Node_Find("/"), N)   -- N = # of Advance calls
        __InitLayout()                                -- calls Advance N times
        __InitLayout = nil
        G_ScriptTimerThread = BindEasy(Node_Find("/"), "/script", C_ScriptTimer)
        ... binds ...
        Game_LoadProgress_Leave(Node_Find("/"))       -- DISMISSES the load screen
      Each created object calls Game_LoadProgress_Advance once. If you REMOVE
      objects, Enter's N must be reduced to match the new Advance count (1n1:
      94 -> 86) AND Game_LoadProgress_Leave MUST be called at the end, else the
      loading portrait stays on a black screen forever. MP maps (dm_narrow) have
      NO progress system -- that's why Narrow never needed this.
  (2) CALL A/B ENCODING: POS_A=15 (base = function slot), POS_B=6 (nresults).
      Statement call = CALL(0,0). Node_Find as a nested arg = CALL(1,1). BindEasy
      right after (consuming that result) = CALL(0,1). Swapping A/B makes CALL
      invoke a NON-function stack slot -> [SCRIPTERROR] -> script thread dies ->
      same black-portrait screen. (Don't hand-decode A as i>>6; that's argU, not A.)
  (3) DIRECTORY PATHS ARE PER-LEVEL. Objects must be added (Node_AddSon) to the
      directory whose PATH matches the BindEasy Node_Find() path. NODE NUMBERS
      DIFFER PER LEVEL -- do NOT assume dm_narrow's node90/node71. Map the level's
      own tree by walking NOD_Directory/NOD_Scenario create+AddSon:
        1n1:  /Scenario_Dynamic/Object     = node70   (put vessels+player here)
              /Scenario_Dynamic/Navigation  = node58   (put PatrolArea_1 here; the
                                                        toiler AI looks for it at
                                                        /Scenario_Dynamic/Navigation/PatrolArea_1)
              /Scenario_Dynamic/Object/TRAFFIC = node71 (a SUB-dir! campaign put its
                                                         combat vessels here)
      Adding toilers to node71 (TRAFFIC) but binding via "/Scenario_Dynamic/Object"
      -> Node_Find misses -> BindEasy returns nil -> SetEngineValue(nil,...) ->
      log: "NOD_Script::PM_BindThread() node is NULL" + "[SCRIPTERROR] [Lua
      function: SetEngineValue]". Fix: parent = the "Object" dir (node70), bind via
      "/Scenario_Dynamic/Object".
  (4) DEBUG LOGGING: the game writes log.txt in the game dir ONLY when launched
      with -logfile AND -logunknown together (with just -logfile the user got no
      file). Script errors appear as: "[SCRIPTERROR] (none):-1 [Lua function: X]".
      -logunknown also dumps the live node tree ([NOD_Generic]: '/path'), handy for
      confirming actual object paths.

NICE-TO-KNOW: Body_SetNameKey(node, -1) = engine assigns a RANDOM generated pilot
name from its name pool (e.g. "Jeff the Ripper"), so wave enemies get flavour names
for free.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6c. THE TWO Aqua.exe VERSIONS: STEAM (DRM) vs GOG (DRM-FREE) -- CRITICAL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The user uploaded TWO executables. They are radically different for modding:

STEAM Aqua.exe (2,850,816 B):
  - .text entropy 8.00 (AES-256 ENCRYPTED), entry point in a ".bind" section.
  - ".bind" + entry-point-in-it + encrypted .text = STEAM DRM (SteamStub).
    (NOT SecuROM -- an earlier guess of "SecuROM" was WRONG; corrected. The
    original 2001 disc release used SafeDisc, which Steam stripped and replaced
    with its own SteamStub wrapper.)
  - steam_api.dll is NOT in the normal import table because SteamStub copies
    the import directory into .bind and appends what it needs.
  - Consequence: the real engine CODE is only decrypted in RAM at runtime.
    Static analysis / code reference-finding / binary patching are ALL BLOCKED.
  - Removable in principle with the open-source tool "Steamless".

GOG Aqua.exe (2,502,656 B, uploaded as 1783412860968_Aqua.exe):
  - .text entropy 6.40 (PLAIN, unencrypted), entry point in .text (RVA 0x2a791,
    VA 0x42a791), NO .bind section. Disassembles cleanly (standard MSVC startup
    with SEH via fs:[0]). ImageBase = 0x400000.
  - FULLY analyzable and patchable. This is THE version to use for any engine-
    level work. GOG is DRM-free by policy; this confirms it.
  - Verified by reference-finding actually working (see 6d). On Steam every
    string ref search returned 0 (encrypted); on GOG they resolve correctly.

RULE: For engine analysis/patching, ALWAYS use the GOG exe. The Steam exe is
only good for extracting readable .data/.rdata (strings, embedded shaders),
NOT code.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6d. ENGINE INTERNALS FROM THE GOG EXE (all statically verified)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Analysis stack (rebuild): pip install pefile capstone --break-system-packages.
  import pefile; pe=pefile.PE(path); base=pe.OPTIONAL_HEADER.ImageBase
  img=pe.get_memory_mapped_image()   # RVA-indexed! offset = VA - base
  text=pe.sections[0]; tb=img[text.VirtualAddress:...]   # .text bytes
Reference finder (works on GOG, not Steam): search tb for struct.pack('<I',VA).
find_str(sub): locate a substring, walk back to the preceding NUL for the
string start VA, then refs() finds absolute 4-byte refs in .text.

FILE SYSTEM (confirms our PAK reverse-engineering from the OTHER side!):
  - MOS_PackFile::OpenDepots @ 0x63c46c (ref 0x5228f2) opens the PAKs. In code
    it pushes the magic "MASSIVEFILE" and checks version == 3 -- EXACTLY the
    format we reverse-engineered (section 2). PAKs are called "depots".
  - PAK path format strings: "%s%d.pak" and "dat\pak\aquanox"  ->  the engine
    loads dat\pak\aquanox0.pak .. aquanox6.pak. Higher number overrides lower.
  - CON_Cache<T> = the resource cache. "CON_Cache::Allocate() file not found"
    @ 0x6045e8 (7 refs) -- THIS is the exact error box the user saw for the
    missing brainfire mesh. Has SetAlternativeExtension() (the "~" cooked-file
    handling), Lock/Unlock.
  - DES_File::Open() tries "load compiled" then "load uncompiled" (a fallback:
    cooked ~ file first, plain text second).

CVAR SYSTEM (MOS_CVar) -- console variables, Quake-style:
  - MOS_CVar::Find(name, warnflag) @ 0x521520. Cvar list head @ [0x68ac48],
    singly-linked, strcmp-compared. "MOS_CVar::Find() - cvar not found".
  - Types: S32/F32/CString with _Get/_Set (typed). MOS_CVar::Load() reads cvar
    values FROM A DES FILE ("syntax error in des file"); MOS_CVar::Save() writes.
  - A "config.txt" string exists @ 0x609d7c (refs 0x428522 etc.), path built
    with a "%s_%d_%d" format (likely per-resolution config).
  - There is a built-in in-game CONSOLE: NOD_Console, Console_Activate/Input,
    bound via input action "menu_toggleconsole".
  - COMPLETE cvar list (35), all essentially the graphics/sound/gameplay menu:
    dsp_: anisotropyquality, avoidframebuffering, depth, display, forcealphatest,
      fsaa, gammacorrection, height, pixelshader, texturecachesize, trilinear,
      triplebuffer, usecompressedtextures, usemipmap, vsync, width
    ren_: object_dlight, objectprelit_bumpmap, plankton_detail, plant_detail,
      radar_detail, rays_enable, terrain_bumpmap, terrain_dlight,
      terrain_dshadow, terrain_lod, terrain_singlepass
    g_: autochangegun, cdkey, identify_target, showgun ; net_bandwidth ;
    snd_: fx_volume, music_volume, sounddevice3d
  - IMPORTANT REALITY CHECK: these cvars ARE the in-game menu settings. There is
    NO hidden graphics-boost cvar. A few MIGHT accept out-of-menu values
    (dsp_pixelshader, dsp_texturecachesize, ren_terrain_lod, maybe
    dsp_anisotropyquality beyond the menu step) but gains are marginal. The
    cvar/console path works even on the Steam build (it's runtime, DRM doesn't
    touch it); GOG was only needed to DISCOVER it.

RENDERER (DSP_Display, DirectX 8 hybrid):
  - Uses BOTH fixed-function (SetTextureStageState) AND programmable shaders
    (VertexShader_CreateFromFile, PixelShader_CreateFromMemory). This hybrid
    nature is why RTX Remix is a poor fit (Remix needs pure fixed-function).
  - VertexShader_Set (~0x50b920): takes a shader index (1..256), indexes a
    table at [display+0x6d98] with stride 12 bytes/entry ([+0]=handle), then
    calls D3D device vtable [ecx+0x138] (SetVertexShader). Errors "vertex
    shader not created" / "ID is out of range".
  - ~80 VERTEX SHADERS are EMBEDDED in the exe as .vsa blobs in an internal
    "rep_" (repository) region (Steam: ~0x61a168-0x6446a8; 158 FILE-tagged
    entries). Each is a real vs.1.0 DX8 shader: version token 0xFFFE0100, a
    comment token "FILErep_<name>.vsa", "LINE n" debug comments, real
    instructions (m4x4/m4x3/sub/mul/mov), end token 0x0000FFFF. Named by
    material function: object_vs_cmdm (colormap+detailmap), object_vs_dmbm
    (detail+bump), objectprelit_vs_lmcs (lightmap), terrainprelit_vs_*,
    radar_vs_*, clear_vs_*, halo_vs_*, standard_vs_eoap*, etc.
    Suffix codes: cm=colormap dm=detailmap bm=bumpmap lm=lightmap fm=? cs=?
    eoa=? These live in DATA (readable even on Steam) but modifying them means
    binary-patching the token stream. Pixel shaders are CreateFromMemory.
  - Render feature cvars default mostly to 1 (ON): bumpmap, dlight, singlepass
    are already enabled in normal play. ren_terrain_dshadow default was
    variable (register push ebx, not push 1) -> possibly hardware-gated.

WHAT ENGINE-LEVEL MODDING IS NOW POSSIBLE (with GOG exe):
  (1) Binary patches for hard limits / gates the menu+cvars cannot reach
      (texture-size caps, LOD/draw distance, shadow res, a hardware check that
      wrongly disables a feature). Real RE work per change, but now UNBLOCKED.
  (2) Shader edits (embedded .vsa are locatable + decodable; vs.1.0 limits).
  (3) New features via an INJECTED/PROXY DLL (wrap a loaded DLL e.g. dinput8.dll,
      hook engine funcs, add behaviour in C++). This is how SilentPatch (which
      EXISTS for AquaNox) works. Claude can do static analysis + design the
      hook; the USER must compile+test on Windows (no Win build/test in sandbox).
  RT / VR remain impractical (hybrid shaders block Remix; VR = external wrapper).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6e. .msb MESH FORMAT -- FULLY CRACKED (read; write not yet implemented)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
KRASS binary mesh. Used for SHIP models AND terrain tiles (lmsh/*.msb). Layout:
  - 8-byte file header, then a sequence of submeshes.
  - Submesh nominal header: uint32 vcount, uint32 icount (but robust parsing
    below does not rely on these).
  - VERTEX = 40 bytes: pos(3f) + normal(3f) + color(uint32, almost always
    0xFFFFFFFF white) + UV(2f) + extra(4 bytes). The 0xFFFFFFFF at local +24
    is the reliable stride marker.
  - FACE = 8 bytes: 3x uint16 vertex index + uint16 material/group ID (varies,
    e.g. 0x0701, 0x0401 at material boundaries -- do NOT treat it as a fixed
    marker; validate faces by indices < vcount instead).
  - UV V-coord is flipped on export (1-v) to match OBJ/Blender.
ROBUST content-based parser (works regardless of the count headers): scan for
runs of 40-byte vertex records (color==0xFFFFFFFF, |normal|~1), then the
following run of 8-byte face records (3 indices < that run's vcount). Each
(vertex-run, face-run) pair is a submesh; face indices are LOCAL to the run.
Phobocaster: 934 verts / 682 faces / 16 submeshes -> extracted to OBJ, looks
correct (symmetric sub hull). Textures: DDS -> PNG via Pillow (DXT1/DXT3).
Delivered phobocaster_model.zip (OBJ+MTL+PNG+preview+raw). Parser code lives in
aqua_level.py::load_msb (see section 8 additions / 6h).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6f. BASE / STATION SYSTEM (sta_*.sco) + CAMPAIGN DATA
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
A "base" is not special engine code -- it REUSES known systems, reconfigured:
  1. 3D scene: sta_<name>.sco (e.g. sta_neopolis in map/4n2/) builds a scene
     graph of dock geometry (atl_dock*, gen_dock) + a Director + camera paths
     (Navigation/LookFrom/LookAt/MoveFrom) driven by CameraLib -> the gliding
     ambient camera. Same camera system as mission cutscenes, no combat.
  2. Docking: DockLib = dat/script/lib/dock.sco (CreateDockMaster, PlaceInDock,
     LaunchVessel, UndockOwn, LD_ClearForDocking) = physical dock behaviour
     objects (same BindEasy framework as enemy AI).
  3. Data (dat/sty/, PLAIN TEXT .des, EDITABLE): item_d.des = the SHOP (33 items,
     each Key/Class(gun|device|torpedo)/Name/Image/Info/Price); gun_d.des =
     weapon names; briefing_d.des; mood_d.des; movie_d.des (cutscenes);
     mission_d.des = campaign spine (30 missions: Key/Name/EngineScript path).
  4. Dialogue: mtake_*.des tables (Key -> Text + Person + .wav + Mood), same
     radio-message system as in-mission comms, reused for base conversations.
The base UI itself (buy/sell mechanic, upgrade, save, layout) is ENGINE-
HARDWIRED (no menu .des, no .sco for it) -- it reads the sty databases and is
in the (DRM'd) engine code. So: DATA is moddable (shop items, prices, dialogue,
campaign order, even inserting missions via mission_d), UI LOGIC is not (without
the GOG-exe engine route).

CUT TRADE ECONOMY (nice archaeology; user spotted it):
  cargo_d.des lists 104 trade goods incl. "Standard Handelsgueter" (Manganknollen,
  Methanhydrat, Sulfide, Titan, Palladium, Uran...). It is a NAME table only
  (Key->Text, NO prices), with dev working-section comments ("NEW CARGO",
  "Thorstens Stuff", "Christophs Stuff", "Philipp", "Senol", "Fishtank",
  "Massive Names"). Of the 24 standard goods, 20 are GHOSTS (never assigned to
  any vessel anywhere); only 4 (Medikamente, Synthohol, Lightwater, Carfentanyl)
  appear as vessel loot via Body_SetCargoKey. There are NO buy/sell/price/money
  APIs anywhere (scripts or exe). item_d.des (equipment shop) HAS prices and
  survived; cargo (the ECONOMY) was cut in AquaNox's simplification from
  Archimedean Dynasty. NOT resurrectable (no underlying mechanic to re-enable) --
  unlike the cut Vulcano map, which just needed a menu entry.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6g. KRASS ENGINE FAMILY & ASSET PORTING
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
KRASS games: AquaNox(2001, our base), AquaNox 2: Revelation(2003, same studio/
genre, evolved KRASS), SpellForce trilogy(2003-05, heavily modified for fantasy
RTS/RPG), Legend of Kay(2005, middleware). Predecessor Archimedean Dynasty(1996)
is pre-KRASS.
  - SpellForce as an asset source = DEAD END (fantasy theme useless underwater;
    formats diverged for the genre change).
  - AquaNox 2 = the viable candidate (same theme) -- and it turned out to be
    HIGHLY compatible, see section 11. CORRECTION: an earlier note here claimed
    AN1/AN2 need "separate ciphers" and formats "diverged". That was WRONG. AN2
    uses the SAME 64-byte PAK key (verified: the key bytes are literally present
    in AN2.dat) with only a tiny header/cipher tweak, the SAME .msb mesh version
    (0x0200), and the SAME .dds/.osd/.col/.sco formats. Our AN1 tools read AN2
    assets directly. (AM3 = AquaMark3 may still differ; not tested.)
  - GRAPHICS/engine improvements are NOT portable as data -- they live in each
    game's compiled (DRM'd) exe. Cannot "copy SpellForce's shaders into AN1".
If the user provides an AN2 PAK, analyze its format first (magic/version/cipher).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6h. LEVEL VIEWER + EXPORT PIPELINE (built, works) -- BASIS FOR A LEVEL EDITOR
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Every piece needed for a level viewer is already cracked; a viewer needs NO game
engine. Built two deliverables from map mp_08 "Vertical" (dm_vertical.sco):
  - aquanox_level_viewer.html: standalone interactive three.js viewer (embedded
    geometry as JSON, ~270KB). Orbit camera (theta/phi/rad -- same spherical
    model the engine itself uses, see 6i), object list grouped by class,
    click-to-select (3D raycast + list), wireframe/grid toggles, per-object info
    (name/class/position/faces). 66 objects, 8956 verts, 6298 faces.
  - aquanox_level_dm_vertical_obj.zip: same level as OBJ+MTL+PNG, opens in
    Blender = instant full editor (each object separately selectable).

PIPELINE (tools: aqua_level.py + export_level.py, both in section 8 additions):
  1. extract_scene(sco): parse f1 (the __InitLayout / world-build proto). For
     each Node_CreateNode(class,name)->SETGLOBAL gvar, capture class+name; then
     Node_ParseIniFile(gvar, osd) gives the OSD; Body_SetCS(gvar, MAT_Vector3
     pos, MAT_Vector3 rot) gives world position + rotation (DEGREES, first
     component = heading around Z; e.g. arena walls at 0/90/180/90).
  2. osd_info(osd): parse the OSD (vfx/<osd>, ~ fallback) -> mesh name (visual
     m0/namemesh) + texture refs.
  3. find_file(rel): resolve across all 7 PAKs with ~ fallback (and osd/ ->
     vfx/osd/ mapping for OSDs; meshes at vfx/msh/<stem>.msb).
  4. load_msb: geometry (see 6e).
  5. export_level.py: for each placed object, rotate (rot_matrix: Rz(h)*Rx(p)*
     Ry(r), degrees) + translate to world, write named OBJ 'o <name>' group,
     one material per OSD (Kd + map_Kd PNG). Skip NOD_Position/Camera/Directory/
     Scenario (non-visual).
Terrain: stored as .msb tiles in map/<m>/lmsh/ + lightmap DDS in ltex/ +
terrain.des config + fog/*.fog (64x64 fog grid). The tz_element*_ws tiles also
appear as placed objects in the scene graph, so the current exporter already
includes terrain geometry that is script-placed.
NEXT for a real editor: the WRITE half -- a .sco writer that edits the Body_SetCS
calls for moved objects (we CAN already write .sco: the wave mission proves it;
it is "just" targeted edits of position constants). Then edit externally, reload
in engine to preview.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6i. IN-ENGINE VIEWER FEASIBILITY (camera system)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Showing a level IN the real engine is feasible NOW with data mods (no exe edit).
Our sp_vulcano/sp_vertical mods ALREADY are "the engine displaying a level".
The engine has a full camera system usable for a free/orbit viewer:
  - INPUT ACTIONS: camera_alphaadd/sub, camera_betaadd/sub, camera_distanceadd/
    sub, camera_toggle -> a spherical ORBIT CAMERA (alpha/beta angles + distance),
    identical model to our viewer's theta/phi/rad. So we do NOT need to write
    camera code -- the external chase camera is already there.
  - PROGRAMMATIC: Camera_MoveTo, Camera_MoveToAnglesAbs/Rel, Camera_LookAt,
    Camera_ZoomTo, Camera_RollTo, Camera_Cut; NOD_Camera::GetVisibleObjects()
    (culling), GetModeInfo() (camera modes). Station scenes (sta_*) are the
    template: display geometry + camera, no combat.
Plan for an in-engine level VIEWER: a "viewer level" script that loads target
geometry (like the cut-map mods) but instead of a combat player, runs the
existing orbit camera, no enemies -> free fly-through with full engine fidelity
(real water/fog/shaders/lighting). An in-engine EDITOR (move objects live) needs
either a reload workflow (edit externally -> reload) or a code hook via injected
DLL (GOG exe makes this possible). Elegant architecture = external editor for
editing + engine for full-fidelity preview (the professional/dev workflow).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
7. GOTCHAS / LESSONS LEARNED (READ BEFORE TOUCHING ANYTHING)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
- ALWAYS remap grafted constants into the pool of the proto that receives
  the code (see section 4 bug class).
- The engine auto-starts bound behavior objects; never assume you control
  the first call.
- o1764-style starter objects reference foreign globals; transplant only
  self-contained proto sets (verify: no internal CLOSUREs, no oNNNN
  globals outside your set except the ones you bind).
- Statement CALL encoding depends on active locals: in a proto with
  params=0/locals=0 use CALL 0 0; inside f155-style handlers with a
  param, calls are CALL 1 0. Copy raw patterns from originals when unsure.
- DES values: ints are raw LE-int32 bytes; strings carry trailing NUL;
  keys are bytes with NUL. New DES nodes need orig_off=None on the node
  AND its entries/children.
- Game_PlayMusic takes a slot NAME ("track3"), not a path.
- E/F/N/True/False are per-mission globals set in main — dm_narrow has
  them all (verified), so grafted code can reference them.
- The .sco filename in the menu path has NO extension and uses
  backslashes: map\mp_06_dm\script\sp_narrow.
- Scanner discipline: when collecting call arguments from bytecode,
  handle PUSHINT + PUSHSTRING + GETGLOBAL (+ PUSHNUM/PUSHNEGNUM) or you
  WILL draw wrong conclusions (this cost us the matrix insight twice).
- Repack sanity: after building a PAK, re-read its directory, extract
  sp_narrow.sco from it and lua-load it as a smoke test.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
8. COMPLETE TOOL SOURCES (RECREATE THESE IN /home/claude VERBATIM)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Recreation order: pak_tool.py, pak_pack.py, descook.py, lua4dis.py, then
the level builder build_sp_narrow_v6.py (which produces mod_sp_narrow.sco
at V8 state). Extraction afterwards:
  for i in 0..6: python3 pak_tool.py /mnt/user-data/uploads/aquanox{i}.pak ext/pak{i}
Then: python3 build_sp_narrow_v6.py ; rebuild mod_instantfightlist.des
(section 3.6 / snippet in section 9); pack per section 9.

----- FILE: pak_tool.py -----
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""AquaNox 1 PAK extractor — based on Swyter's aquanox-tools format docs."""
import os, struct, sys

KEY = bytes([
    0x68,0x3C,0x61,0x37,0x4C,0x6C,0xC4,0x4F,0x6F,0x72,0x78,0x48,0x33,0x4A,0x2B,0x78,
    0xDC,0xDF,0x61,0x62,0x4B,0x6E,0x29,0x6A,0x73,0x6C,0x6E,0x44,0x6F,0x4A,0x44,0x66,
    0x68,0x44,0x33,0x37,0x66,0x55,0x67,0x4F,0x6F,0xD6,0x78,0x48,0x33,0x58,0x32,0x78,
    0x35,0x41,0x61,0x35,0x51,0x37,0x6E,0x2A,0xF6,0x6C,0x2B,0xFC,0x6F,0x4A,0x23,0x40,
])

def read_directory(f):
    magic = f.read(12)
    assert magic == b"MASSIVEFILE\x00", f"bad magic: {magic!r}"
    version, file_count = struct.unpack("<II", f.read(8))
    copyright = f.read(60).split(b"\x00")[0].decode("latin-1")
    fourcc = f.read(4)  # 'LPT\0'
    entries = []
    data_start = 12 + 8 + 60 + 4 + 0x84 * file_count
    offset = data_start
    for j in range(file_count):
        enc_name = f.read(128)
        (size_enc,) = struct.unpack("<I", f.read(4))
        # decrypt filename: dec[i] = enc[i] - KEY[(j+i) % 64]
        name = bytearray()
        k = j
        for i in range(128):
            if enc_name[i] == 0:
                break
            name.append((enc_name[i] - KEY[k % len(KEY)]) & 0xFF)
            k += 1
        # decrypt size: |LE-uint(KEY[j%60 .. +3]) - size_enc|
        key_uint = struct.unpack_from("<I", KEY, j % 60)[0]
        size = abs(key_uint - size_enc)
        entries.append((name.decode("latin-1"), offset, size))
        offset += size
    return version, copyright, entries

def main():
    pak_path, out_dir = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
    with open(pak_path, "rb") as f:
        version, copyright, entries = read_directory(f)
        print(f"{os.path.basename(pak_path)}: v{version}, {len(entries)} Dateien, '{copyright}'")
        total = 0
        for name, off, size in entries:
            safe = name.replace("\\", "/")
            dest = os.path.join(out_dir, safe)
            os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(dest), exist_ok=True)
            f.seek(off)
            with open(dest, "wb") as o:
                o.write(f.read(size))
            total += size
        # sanity: last file must end exactly at EOF
        f.seek(0, 2)
        eof = f.tell()
        last_end = entries[-1][1] + entries[-1][2] if entries else 0
        print(f"  extrahiert: {total} bytes | letztes Dateiende {last_end} vs EOF {eof} -> {'OK' if last_end == eof else 'MISMATCH!'}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

----- FILE: pak_pack.py -----
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""AquaNox 1 PAK-Packer: baut ein MASSIVEFILE-v3-Archiv."""
import os, struct, sys
from pak_tool import KEY

COPYRIGHT = b"Copyright by Massive Development GmbH. All rights reserved."

def pack(entries, out_path, version=3):
    """entries: Liste (archiv_name_mit_backslashes, quellpfad)"""
    n = len(entries)
    with open(out_path, "wb") as o:
        o.write(b"MASSIVEFILE\x00")
        o.write(struct.pack("<II", version, n))
        o.write(COPYRIGHT.ljust(60, b"\x00"))
        o.write(b"LPT\x00")
        for j, (name, src) in enumerate(entries):
            raw = name.encode("latin-1")
            assert len(raw) < 128
            enc = bytearray(128)
            for i, b in enumerate(raw):
                enc[i] = (b + KEY[(j + i) % len(KEY)]) & 0xFF
            o.write(enc)
            size = os.path.getsize(src)
            key_uint = struct.unpack_from("<I", KEY, j % 60)[0]
            o.write(struct.pack("<I", (key_uint + size) & 0xFFFFFFFF))
        for name, src in entries:
            with open(src, "rb") as f:
                o.write(f.read())

if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Selbsttest: pak6 unveraendert aus extrahierten Dateien rekonstruieren
    import pak_tool
    with open("/mnt/user-data/uploads/aquanox6.pak", "rb") as f:
        _, _, orig_entries = pak_tool.read_directory(f)
    entries = [(name, os.path.join("ext/pak6", name.replace("\\", "/")))
               for name, _, _ in orig_entries]
    pack(entries, "/tmp/pak6_rebuilt.pak")
    a = open("/mnt/user-data/uploads/aquanox6.pak", "rb").read()
    b = open("/tmp/pak6_rebuilt.pak", "rb").read()
    print(f"Original {len(a)} bytes, Rebuild {len(b)} bytes ->",
          "BYTE-IDENTISCH" if a == b else "ABWEICHUNG!")
    if a != b:
        d = next(i for i in range(min(len(a), len(b))) if a[i] != b[i])
        print(f"erste Abweichung @ {d}: {a[d-4:d+8].hex(' ')} vs {b[d-4:d+8].hex(' ')}")

----- FILE: descook.py -----
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""AquaNox 1 cooked ~.des/~.osd: Parser + byte-treuer Serializer.

Baum-Layout in der Datei = Pre-Order: Header, dann seine Entry-Kette,
dann Kind-Header (rekursiv). Unbekannte Bytes werden verbatim erhalten.
"""
import struct, sys, os

class Entry:
    __slots__ = ("vtype", "pad", "key", "value", "orig_off")
    def __init__(self, vtype, pad, key, value, orig_off=None):
        self.vtype, self.pad, self.key, self.value = vtype, pad, key, value
        self.orig_off = orig_off

class Header:
    __slots__ = ("unk", "key", "entries", "children", "orig_off")
    def __init__(self, unk, key, orig_off=None):
        self.unk, self.key = unk, key
        self.entries, self.children = [], []
        self.orig_off = orig_off

def parse(data):
    def parse_entry(off):
        vt, sk, sv, pad = struct.unpack_from("<4B", data, off)
        (nxt,) = struct.unpack_from("<i", data, off + 4)
        key = data[off+8 : off+8+sk]          # inkl. Nullbyte(s)
        val = data[off+8+sk : off+8+sk+sv]    # verbatim
        return Entry(vt, pad, key, val, off), nxt

    def parse_header(off):
        (key_len,) = struct.unpack_from("<H", data, off)
        unk = data[off+2 : off+8]
        vh, eh, ve = struct.unpack_from("<3i", data, off + 8)
        h = Header(unk, data[off+20 : off+20+key_len], off)
        o = ve
        while o != -1:
            e, o = parse_entry(o)
            h.entries.append(e)
        o = vh
        while o != -1:
            c, o = parse_header(o)
            h.children.append(c)
        return h, eh

    roots = []
    o = 0
    while o != -1 and o < len(data):
        h, o = parse_header(o)
        roots.append(h)
    return roots

def serialize(roots):
    # Phase 1: alle Knoten sammeln, Layout = originale physische Reihenfolge,
    # neue Knoten (orig_off=None) hinter allen originalen
    order = []
    def walk(h):
        order.append(("H", h))
        for e in h.entries:
            order.append(("E", e))
        for c in h.children:
            walk(c)
    for r in roots:
        walk(r)
    order.sort(key=lambda kn: (kn[1].orig_off is None,
                               kn[1].orig_off if kn[1].orig_off is not None else 0))
    off_of = {}
    pos = 0
    for kind, n in order:
        off_of[id(n)] = pos
        pos += (20 + len(n.key)) if kind == "H" else (8 + len(n.key) + len(n.value))
    # Phase 2: Verkettungen (next-Sibling) aus der Baumstruktur ableiten
    next_of = {}
    def link(h, nxt):
        next_of[id(h)] = nxt
        for i, e in enumerate(h.entries):
            next_of[id(e)] = off_of[id(h.entries[i+1])] if i+1 < len(h.entries) else -1
        for i, c in enumerate(h.children):
            link(c, off_of[id(h.children[i+1])] if i+1 < len(h.children) else -1)
    for i, r in enumerate(roots):
        link(r, off_of[id(roots[i+1])] if i+1 < len(roots) else -1)
    # Phase 3: linear in Layout-Reihenfolge emittieren
    out = bytearray()
    for kind, n in order:
        assert len(out) == off_of[id(n)]
        if kind == "H":
            vh = off_of[id(n.children[0])] if n.children else -1
            ve = off_of[id(n.entries[0])] if n.entries else -1
            out.extend(struct.pack("<H", len(n.key)))
            out.extend(n.unk)
            out.extend(struct.pack("<3i", vh, next_of[id(n)], ve))
            out.extend(n.key)
        else:
            out.extend(struct.pack("<4B", n.vtype, len(n.key), len(n.value), n.pad))
            out.extend(struct.pack("<i", next_of[id(n)]))
            out.extend(n.key)
            out.extend(n.value)
    return bytes(out)

def find(h, key):
    key_b = key.encode() + b"\x00"
    for c in h.children:
        if c.key == key_b:
            return c
    for e in h.entries:
        if e.key == key_b:
            return e
    return None

if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Roundtrip-Test ueber alle uebergebenen Dateien
    ok = bad = 0
    bad_files = []
    for p in sys.argv[1:]:
        data = open(p, "rb").read()
        try:
            re_encoded = serialize(parse(data))
            if re_encoded == data:
                ok += 1
            else:
                bad += 1
                bad_files.append((p, "DIFF"))
        except Exception as ex:
            bad += 1
            bad_files.append((p, f"ERR {ex}"))
    print(f"Roundtrip: {ok} byte-identisch, {bad} abweichend")
    for p, why in bad_files[:10]:
        print(f"  {why}: {p}")

----- FILE: lua4dis.py -----
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Lua 4.0 Bytecode-Disassembler fuer AquaNox .sco Dateien."""
import struct, sys

OPS = ["END","RETURN","CALL","TAILCALL","PUSHNIL","POP","PUSHINT","PUSHSTRING",
       "PUSHNUM","PUSHNEGNUM","PUSHUPVALUE","GETLOCAL","GETGLOBAL","GETTABLE",
       "GETDOTTED","GETINDEXED","PUSHSELF","CREATETABLE","SETLOCAL","SETGLOBAL",
       "SETTABLE","SETLIST","SETMAP","ADD","ADDI","SUB","MULT","DIV","POW",
       "CONCAT","MINUS","NOT","JMPNE","JMPEQ","JMPLT","JMPLE","JMPGT","JMPGE",
       "JMPT","JMPF","JMPONT","JMPONF","JMP","PUSHNILJMP","FORPREP","FORLOOP",
       "LFORPREP","LFORLOOP","CLOSURE"]
K_OPS = {"PUSHSTRING","GETGLOBAL","GETDOTTED","PUSHSELF","SETGLOBAL"}
S_OPS = {"PUSHINT","ADDI","JMPNE","JMPEQ","JMPLT","JMPLE","JMPGT","JMPGE",
         "JMPT","JMPF","JMPONT","JMPONF","JMP","FORPREP","FORLOOP","LFORPREP","LFORLOOP"}
AB_OPS = {"CALL","TAILCALL","SETTABLE","SETLIST","CLOSURE"}
N_OPS = {"PUSHNUM","PUSHNEGNUM"}
L_OPS = {"GETLOCAL","GETINDEXED","SETLOCAL"}

SIZE_OP, SIZE_B, SIZE_INSTR = 6, 9, 32
POS_U = POS_B = SIZE_OP
POS_A = SIZE_OP + SIZE_B
MAXARG_S = ((1 << (SIZE_INSTR - SIZE_OP)) - 1) >> 1

class Proto:
    def __init__(self):
        self.source = self.line = None
        self.kstr, self.knum, self.kproto, self.code = [], [], [], []
        self.kstr_raw, self.locals, self.lineinfo = [], [], b""
        self.source_raw = None

class Reader:
    def __init__(self, data):
        self.d, self.o = data, 0
    def bytes(self, n):
        b = self.d[self.o:self.o+n]; self.o += n; return b
    def byte(self): return self.bytes(1)[0]
    def i32(self): return struct.unpack("<i", self.bytes(4))[0]
    def num(self): return struct.unpack("<d", self.bytes(8))[0]
    def s(self):
        raw = self.sraw()
        return None if raw is None else raw[:-1].decode("latin-1")
    def sraw(self):
        n, = struct.unpack("<I", self.bytes(4))
        return None if n == 0 else self.bytes(n)

def load_function(r):
    p = Proto()
    p.source_raw = r.sraw()
    p.source = None if p.source_raw is None else p.source_raw[:-1].decode('latin-1')
    p.line = r.i32()
    p.numparams = r.i32()
    p.is_vararg = r.byte()
    p.maxstack = r.i32()
    for _ in range(r.i32()):            # locals
        p.locals.append((r.sraw(), r.i32(), r.i32()))
    p.lineinfo = r.bytes(4 * r.i32())   # lineinfo roh aufbewahren
    for _ in range(r.i32()):            # kstr
        p.kstr_raw.append(r.sraw())
    p.kstr = [k[:-1].decode("latin-1") for k in p.kstr_raw]
    for _ in range(r.i32()):            # knum
        p.knum.append(r.num())
    for _ in range(r.i32()):            # kproto
        p.kproto.append(load_function(r))
    n = r.i32()                         # code
    p.code = list(struct.unpack(f"<{n}I", r.bytes(4 * n)))
    return p

HEADER = b"\x1bLua\x40" + bytes([1,4,4,4,0x20,6,9,8]) + struct.pack("<d", 3.14159265358979323846e8)

def wstr(out, raw):
    if raw is None:
        out.extend(struct.pack("<I", 0))
    else:
        out.extend(struct.pack("<I", len(raw))); out.extend(raw)

def write_function(out, p):
    wstr(out, p.source_raw)
    out.extend(struct.pack("<iiBi", p.line, p.numparams, p.is_vararg, p.maxstack))
    out.extend(struct.pack("<i", len(p.locals)))
    for name, s0, e0 in p.locals:
        wstr(out, name); out.extend(struct.pack("<ii", s0, e0))
    assert len(p.lineinfo) % 4 == 0
    out.extend(struct.pack("<i", len(p.lineinfo)//4)); out.extend(p.lineinfo)
    out.extend(struct.pack("<i", len(p.kstr_raw)))
    for kraw in p.kstr_raw:
        wstr(out, kraw)
    out.extend(struct.pack("<i", len(p.knum)))
    for n in p.knum:
        out.extend(struct.pack("<d", n))
    out.extend(struct.pack("<i", len(p.kproto)))
    for c in p.kproto:
        write_function(out, c)
    out.extend(struct.pack("<i", len(p.code)))
    out.extend(struct.pack(f"<{len(p.code)}I", *p.code))

def save(top):
    out = bytearray(HEADER)
    write_function(out, top)
    return bytes(out)

def load(path):
    r = Reader(open(path, "rb").read())
    hdr = r.bytes(4 + 1 + 1 + 3 + 3 + 1 + 8)
    assert hdr[:5] == b"\x1bLua\x40", "kein Lua-4.0-Chunk"
    return load_function(r)

def disasm(p, name="main", show=True):
    lines = [f"; function {name} (source={p.source}, line={p.line}, params={p.numparams}, stack={p.maxstack})"]
    for pc, i in enumerate(p.code):
        op = OPS[i & 0x3F] if (i & 0x3F) < len(OPS) else f"OP{i & 0x3F}"
        u = i >> POS_U
        a, b = i >> POS_A, (i >> POS_B) & ((1 << SIZE_B) - 1)
        arg = ""
        if op in K_OPS:   arg = f'{u} ; "{p.kstr[u]}"' if u < len(p.kstr) else str(u)
        elif op in N_OPS: arg = f"{u} ; {p.knum[u]:g}" if u < len(p.knum) else str(u)
        elif op in S_OPS: arg = str(u - MAXARG_S)
        elif op in AB_OPS: arg = f"{a} {b}"
        elif op in L_OPS or op in ("POP","PUSHNIL","PUSHUPVALUE","CREATETABLE","SETMAP","CONCAT"):
            arg = str(u)
        lines.append(f"  [{pc:4}] {op:12} {arg}")
    return "\n".join(lines)

def walk(p, path="main"):
    yield path, p
    for i, c in enumerate(p.kproto):
        yield from walk(c, f"{path}.f{i}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    top = load(sys.argv[1])
    if len(sys.argv) > 2 and sys.argv[2] == "--list":
        for path, p in walk(top):
            print(f"{path}: line={p.line} code={len(p.code)} kstr={len(p.kstr)} kproto={len(p.kproto)}")
    elif len(sys.argv) > 2 and sys.argv[2] == "--grep":
        needle = sys.argv[3]
        for path, p in walk(top):
            if any(needle in (s or "") for s in p.kstr):
                print(f"{path} (line {p.line}, {len(p.code)} instr) enthaelt '{needle}'")
    else:
        which = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "main"
        for path, p in walk(top):
            if path == which:
                print(disasm(p, path))

----- FILE: build_sp_narrow_v6.py (V8 state) -----
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""V6: sp_narrow.sco aus dm_narrow.sco.
Spieler + Waffen + Musik + EIN Gegner (Toiler) mit ECHTER KI.
Die KI wird als Cross-Chunk-Graft aus inst_fightclub uebernommen:
  - Protos 151..165 (Template S_o1759 + Rechner o1761 + Instanz o1764), voellig eigenstaendig
  - Template-/Instanz-Bauslice in main (mit Konstanten- und Closure-Remap)
  - Patrol-Area unter /Scenario_Dynamic/Navigation
  - BindEasy + o1764.Start() in __StartUp
"""
import copy, lua4dis
OPS = lua4dis.OPS
MAXARG_S = lua4dis.MAXARG_S
POS_A, POS_B = lua4dis.POS_A, lua4dis.POS_B
def op(i): return OPS[i & 0x3F]
def argU(i): return i >> 6

KSTR_OPS = {"GETGLOBAL", "SETGLOBAL", "PUSHSTRING", "GETDOTTED", "PUSHSELF", "SETDOTTED"}
KNUM_OPS = {"PUSHNUM", "PUSHNEGNUM"}

dm = lua4dis.load("ext/pak4/map/mp_06_dm/script/dm_narrow.sco")
fc = lua4dis.load("ext/pak0/map/inst_fightclub/script/inst_fightclub.sco")
f1 = dm.kproto[1]          # World-Build
su = dm.kproto[60]         # __StartUp
assert op(f1.code[-1]) == "END" and op(su.code[-1]) == "END" and op(dm.code[-1]) == "END"

# ---------- Konstanten-Helfer, gebunden an ein Ziel-Proto ----------
def mk_K(target):
    def K(s):
        if s in target.kstr: return target.kstr.index(s)
        target.kstr.append(s); target.kstr_raw.append(s.encode("latin-1") + b"\x00")
        return len(target.kstr) - 1
    return K
def mk_N(target):
    def N(x):
        x = float(x)
        if x in target.knum: return target.knum.index(x)
        target.knum.append(x); return len(target.knum) - 1
    return N

# ---------- Emitter-Fabrik fuer frisch erzeugten Code ----------
def emitter(target):
    K, N = mk_K(target), mk_N(target)
    buf = []
    e = {}
    e["GG"] = lambda n: buf.append(OPS.index("GETGLOBAL") | (K(n) << 6))
    e["SG"] = lambda n: buf.append(OPS.index("SETGLOBAL") | (K(n) << 6))
    e["PS"] = lambda s: buf.append(OPS.index("PUSHSTRING") | (K(s) << 6))
    e["GD"] = lambda s: buf.append(OPS.index("GETDOTTED") | (K(s) << 6))
    e["PI"] = lambda n: buf.append(OPS.index("PUSHINT") | ((n + MAXARG_S) << 6))
    def PN(x):
        if x < 0: buf.append(OPS.index("PUSHNEGNUM") | (N(-x) << 6))
        else:     buf.append(OPS.index("PUSHNUM") | (N(x) << 6))
    e["PN"] = PN
    e["CALL"] = lambda a, b: buf.append(OPS.index("CALL") | (a << POS_A) | (b << POS_B))
    e["CT"] = lambda: buf.append(OPS.index("CREATETABLE"))
    e["buf"] = buf
    def push(a):
        if isinstance(a, tuple): e["GG"](a[1])           # ('g', name) -> Global
        elif isinstance(a, str): e["PS"](a)
        elif isinstance(a, int): e["PI"](a)
        else: PN(a)
    def stmt(fn, *args):
        e["GG"](fn)
        for a in args: push(a)
        e["CALL"](0, 0)
    e["stmt"] = stmt
    return e

# ---------- Slice-Graft: fc-Code kopieren, Konstanten+Closures remappen ----------
def graft_slice(src, lo, hi, target, pmap):
    """Kopiert src.code[lo:hi] nach Instruktionsliste, remappt K/N-Konstanten (aus
       src's EIGENEM Pool src.kstr/src.knum) in target's Pools und CLOSURE-Indizes
       ueber pmap. src kann der Chunk (main) ODER ein Proto-Objekt sein."""
    K, N = mk_K(target), mk_N(target)
    out = []
    for i in src.code[lo:hi]:
        o = op(i)
        if o in KSTR_OPS:
            s = src.kstr[argU(i)]
            out.append((i & 0x3F) | (K(s) << 6))
        elif o in KNUM_OPS:
            x = src.knum[argU(i)]
            out.append((i & 0x3F) | (N(x) << 6))
        elif o == "CLOSURE":
            p = i >> POS_A
            out.append((i & 0x3F) | (pmap[p] << POS_A))   # B(upvals)=0 hier ohnehin
        else:
            out.append(i)
    return out

# ============ 1) KI-Protos 151..165 nach dm.kproto anhaengen ============
BASE = len(dm.kproto)                      # 61
PMAP = {p: BASE + (p - 151) for p in range(151, 166)}
for p in range(151, 166):
    dm.kproto.append(copy.deepcopy(fc.kproto[p]))   # eigenstaendig, kein internes Remap noetig

# ============ 2) Template-/Instanz-Bauslice in main (dm.code) ============
# fc-Slice 1819..1858 baut S_o1759, o1761, o1764 (CREATETABLE .. SETGLOBAL).
main_graft = graft_slice(fc, 1819, 1858, dm, PMAP)
# V7: Freigabesignal setzen: o1761.Value = True
# (CheckCondition prueft bei CodeIndex 1 genau dieses Feld; bei Fightclub setzt es
#  ein Objekt-Geflecht, das wir nicht mitkopieren. Wir setzen es statisch.)
Km = mk_K(dm)
SETTABLE_33 = OPS.index("SETTABLE") | (3 << POS_A) | (3 << POS_B)
main_graft += [
    OPS.index("GETGLOBAL")  | (Km("o1761") << 6),
    OPS.index("PUSHSTRING") | (Km("Value") << 6),
    OPS.index("GETGLOBAL")  | (Km("True")  << 6),
    SETTABLE_33,
]
# V8: Feindschafts-Matrix (der eigentliche IFF-Mechanismus, Fightclub-Werte):
# Spieler(2) vs Gegner(4) gegenseitig Enemy, eigene Gruppe Friend.
CALL_00 = OPS.index("CALL")
def _mtx(a, b, const):
    return [OPS.index("GETGLOBAL") | (Km("SetEnemyMatrixElement") << 6),
            OPS.index("PUSHINT")   | ((a + MAXARG_S) << 6),
            OPS.index("PUSHINT")   | ((b + MAXARG_S) << 6),
            OPS.index("GETGLOBAL") | (Km(const) << 6),
            CALL_00]
main_graft += _mtx(2, 4, "E") + _mtx(4, 2, "E") + _mtx(2, 2, "F")
dm.code = dm.code[:-1] + main_graft + [dm.code[-1]]

# ============ 3) World-Build (f1): Spieler + Waffen + Toiler + Patrol-Area + Musik ============
E = emitter(f1); buf = E["buf"]; stmt = E["stmt"]
GG, SG, PS, PN, PI, CALL = E["GG"], E["SG"], E["PS"], E["PN"], E["PI"], E["CALL"]
PARENT = "node90"      # Scenario_Dynamic/Object
NAVDIR = "node71"      # Scenario_Dynamic/Navigation

def set_cs(node, x, y, z, h):
    GG("Body_SetCS"); GG(node)
    GG("MAT_Vector3"); PN(x); PN(y); PN(z); CALL(2, 1)
    GG("MAT_Vector3"); (PN(h) if h != 0 else PI(0)); PI(0); PI(0); CALL(3, 255)
    CALL(0, 0)

# -- Spieler (FoF=2 wie Fightclub) --
GG("Node_CreateNode"); PS("nod_player"); PS("player1"); CALL(0, 1); SG("node9999")
stmt("Node_AddSon", ("g", PARENT), ("g", "node9999"))
set_cs("node9999", 1977.24, 1950.66, 258.492, 172.65)
stmt("Node_ParseIniFile", ("g", "node9999"), "osd/pla/pla_phobocaster.osd")
stmt("Body_SetFriendOrFoeID", ("g", "node9999"), 2)
stmt("Node_EnterSimulation", ("g", "node9999"))

# -- Waffen --
for item, cnt in [("gun_lasergatlin", 1), ("gun_vendettagatlin", 1),
                  ("gun_sizzler", 1), ("gun_torpedolauncher", 1),
                  ("ammo_energy", 5000), ("ammo_shell", 5000),
                  ("device_generator", 1),
                  ("torpedo_hammerhead", 4), ("torpedo_maneater", 4)]:
    stmt("Body_AddItem", ("g", "node9999"), item, cnt)

# -- Patrol-Area (Arena-Zentrum) unter Navigation --
GG("Node_CreateNode"); PS("NOD_PatrolArea"); PS("PatrolArea_1"); CALL(0, 1); SG("nodePA")
stmt("Node_AddSon", ("g", NAVDIR), ("g", "nodePA"))
GG("PatrolArea_SetPosition"); GG("nodePA")
GG("MAT_Vector3"); PN(2150.0); PN(1620.0); PN(275.0); CALL(2, 255); CALL(0, 0)
stmt("PatrolArea_SetRadius", ("g", "nodePA"), 600)
stmt("PatrolArea_SetMinZ",  ("g", "nodePA"), -220)
stmt("PatrolArea_SetMaxZ",  ("g", "nodePA"), 90)

# -- EIN Gegner: Toiler (FoF=4, vorbewaffnetes _inst-OSD), unter Object --
GG("Node_CreateNode"); PS("nod_vessel"); PS("gen_toiler_inst_1"); CALL(0, 1); SG("node9990")
stmt("Node_AddSon", ("g", PARENT), ("g", "node9990"))
set_cs("node9990", 1932.06, 1600.48, 284.52, -90.0)
stmt("Node_ParseIniFile", ("g", "node9990"), "osd/gen/gen_toiler_inst.osd")
stmt("Body_SetFriendOrFoeID", ("g", "node9990"), 4)
stmt("Body_SetNameKey", ("g", "node9990"), -1)
for slot in (0, 1, 2):
    stmt("Body_SetCargoKey", ("g", "node9990"), -1, slot)
stmt("Node_EnterSimulation", ("g", "node9990"))

# -- Musik zuletzt: Slot 3 laden, per Slot-Name "track3" abspielen (Original-Muster) --
TRACK = "sfx/sample/music_track02.sam"
stmt("Game_LoadMusic", ("g", "node0"), TRACK, 3)
GG("Game_PlayMusic"); GG("GetGameNode"); CALL(1, 1); PS("track3"); CALL(0, 0)

f1.code = f1.code[:-1] + buf + [f1.code[-1]]

# ============ 4) __StartUp: BindEasy + o1764.Start() ============
# Exaktes fc-Muster fuer den Toiler-Bind aus fc's __StartUp-PROTO (kproto[361]),
# NUR Slice 429..437 (BindEasy .. SETGLOBAL o1759). o1764.Start() [437..439]
# entfaellt: es startet fremde Rechner (o1809/o2018/o2025), die wir nicht haben.
# Der Start der Zustandskette kommt engine-seitig nach Script_BindThread.
su_graft = graft_slice(fc.kproto[361], 429, 437, su, PMAP)
su.code = su.code[:-1] + su_graft + [su.code[-1]]

# ============ 5) Speichern + Verifikation ============
out = lua4dis.save(dm)
open("mod_sp_narrow.sco", "wb").write(out)
print(f"kproto jetzt {len(dm.kproto)} (KI-Protos {BASE}..{len(dm.kproto)-1})")
print(f"main +{len(main_graft)}, f1 +{len(buf)}, __StartUp +{len(su_graft)} Instr.")
print(f"sp_narrow.sco = {len(out)} bytes")

# Roundtrip: neu laden
chk = lua4dis.load_function(lua4dis.Reader(out[len(lua4dis.HEADER):]))
assert len(chk.kproto) == len(dm.kproto), "kproto count mismatch!"
# Pruefe: gegraftete Protos disassemblieren fehlerfrei
for p in range(BASE, len(chk.kproto)):
    _ = lua4dis.disasm(chk.kproto[p])
# Pruefe Template-Bau im main
dmain = lua4dis.disasm_top(chk) if hasattr(lua4dis, "disasm_top") else None
print("Roundtrip OK: alle KI-Protos disassemblieren, kproto-Anzahl stimmt.")
# Zeige __StartUp-Ende
sud = lua4dis.disasm(chk.kproto[60]).split("\n")
print("\n__StartUp Ende:")
for l in sud[-13:]:
    print(" ", l)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
8b. LEVEL-VIEWER TOOL SOURCES (aqua_level.py + export_level.py)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Build on pak_tool/descook/lua4dis (section 8). The full working versions were
built this session; below are faithful specs to recreate them (sandbox was
reset, so exact line-for-line source was regenerated from the algorithm).

===== aqua_level.py (spec) =====
"""AquaNox Level-Extraktion: Szenengraph + Meshes + OSD-Aufloesung.
Grundlage fuer Level-Viewer / -Editor. Nutzt lua4dis + descook (Section 8).
Key functions:
  find_file(rel)        -> resolve 'vfx/msh/x.msb' across all 7 PAKs (+ '~' fallback)
  osd_info(osd_ref)     -> {'mesh':name,'textures':[...]} from vfx/<osd> (~ fallback)
  load_msb(path)        -> (verts[(x,y,z)], uvs[(u,v)], faces[(a,b,c)]) 0-based
                           (the robust content-based .msb parser, see 6e)
  extract_scene(sco)    -> [{name,class,osd,pos[3],rot[3],gvar}] from proto f1
Details that matter:
  - find_file: try rel and stem+'~'+ext under ext/pak0..6/. For OSDs the caller
    passes 'vfx/'+osd_ref; OSD refs look like 'osd/tz/tz_wall.osd' so the on-disk
    path is 'vfx/osd/tz/tz_wall~.osd'. Meshes: 'vfx/msh/<stem>.msb'.
  - osd_info: descook.parse -> find child 'visual' -> entry m0/namemesh = mesh;
    collect entries/children ending .dds/.tga as textures.
  - load_msb: is_vertex(off)= data[off+24:+28]==FFFFFFFF and |normal|~1 and
    |pos|<1e5. Walk: while is_vertex -> read pos(+0,3f), uv(+28,2f; v->1-v),
    stride 40; then while face_ok(indices<vcount) read 3xuint16 (+ skip the
    uint16 group id), stride 8. Each vertex-run+face-run = a submesh, indices
    are local to the run (add running vbase).
  - extract_scene: walk proto f1 (kproto[1], the __InitLayout world build).
    Node_CreateNode(class,name) then SETGLOBAL gvar -> new object.
    Node_ParseIniFile(gvar, osd-string) -> object's osd.
    Body_SetCS(gvar, MAT_Vector3(x,y,z), MAT_Vector3(h,p,r)) -> pos + rot(deg).
    (Collect the two MAT_Vector3 arg-triples after the gvar; first=pos, 2nd=rot.)
"""

===== export_level.py (spec) =====
"""export_level.py -- level -> OBJ (world-positioned named objects) + MTL + PNG.
  rot_matrix(h,p,r) degrees -> Rz(h)*Rx(p)*Ry(r) (heading around Z dominant).
  apply(m,v) = 3x3 * vec3.
  export(sco, out_dir, skip=('NOD_Position','NOD_Camera','NOD_Directory',
    'NOD_Scenario')): for each placed obj -> load mesh (cache by .msb path),
    build material per OSD (Kd 0.8 + map_Kd = first texture DDS->PNG via Pillow),
    write 'o <name>', transformed 'v', 'vt', 'usemtl', 'f a/a b/b c/c' (1-based,
    running vertex offset). Writes <name>.obj + <name>.mtl + textures/*.png.
Interactive viewer: inject the compact JSON scene (below) into
viewer_template.html at __SCENE_DATA__.
  JSON: {"name":..., "objects":[{"n":name,"c":class,"p":[x,y,z],
         "v":[flat world xyz...],"f":[flat triangle indices...]}]}
  Build v by: for each mesh vertex w=apply(rot_matrix(*rot),v); world=w+pos.
  three.js template: importmap three@0.160.0; per-object BufferGeometry
  (setAttribute position, setIndex f, computeVertexNormals); Z-up camera
  (cam.up=(0,0,1)); hand-rolled spherical orbit (theta/phi/rad, LMB=rotate,
  RMB=pan, wheel=zoom); Raycaster click-select; class-grouped list; colors
  nod_generic=0x4a7fa5, nod_itembox=0xd8a24a."""

9. REBUILD SNIPPETS (menu DES + final PAK)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# --- mod_instantfightlist.des (adds "< Narrow >" as entry4) ---
import descook, copy
data = open('ext/pak3/dat/menu/instantfightlist~.des','rb').read()
roots = descook.parse(data); inst = roots[0].children[0]
def kd(o): return o.key.decode('latin-1').rstrip('\x00')
num = [e for e in inst.entries if kd(e)=='numofentry'][0]
e4 = copy.deepcopy(inst.children[-1])
def clear(n):
    n.orig_off=None
    for x in n.entries: x.orig_off=None
    for c in n.children: clear(c)
clear(e4); e4.key = b'entry4\x00'
for x in e4.entries:
    if kd(x)=='fightname': x.value = b'< Narrow >\x00'
    if kd(x)=='path':      x.value = b'map\\mp_06_dm\\script\\sp_narrow\x00'
inst.children.append(e4); num.value = (5).to_bytes(4,'little')
open('mod_instantfightlist.des','wb').write(descook.serialize(roots))
# (the b'map...' literal above yields SINGLE backslashes in the bytes - correct)

# --- final PAK ---
import os, pak_tool, pak_pack
with open('/mnt/user-data/uploads/aquanox6.pak','rb') as f:
    _,_,orig = pak_tool.read_directory(f)
entries = [(n, os.path.join('ext/pak6', n.replace('\\','/'))) for n,_,_ in orig]
names = [n for n,_ in entries]
for arch, srcf in {r'map\mp_06_dm\script\sp_narrow.sco':'mod_sp_narrow.sco',
                   r'dat\menu\instantfightlist~.des':'mod_instantfightlist.des'}.items():
    if arch in names: entries[names.index(arch)] = (arch, srcf)
    else: entries.append((arch, srcf))
pak_pack.pack(entries, '/mnt/user-data/outputs/aquanox6.pak')

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
11. AQUANOX 2: REVELATION -- FULL ANALYSIS + ASSET BRIDGE (all verified)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
User uploaded AN2 (2003, same studio Massive Development, evolved KRASS engine):
files = "AquaNox 2 Revelation.exe" (40KB LAUNCHER STUB only), "AN2.dat" (the REAL
2.5MB engine executable -- starts with MZ, it IS a PE), fmod.dll (AN2 switched
from Miles to FMOD audio), binkw32.dll. Plus 5 PAKs data0.pak..data4.pak.

--- 11a. AN2 ENGINE (AN2.dat) ---
DRM-FREE, fully analyzable (.text entropy 6.52, entry in .text @ VA 0x42a791...
actually RVA 0x29faed; ImageBase 0x400000; timestamp Mar 2003). Imports fmod,
binkw32, DINPUT8, WINMM, WS2_32 (no direct d3d8 in import table). SHARES the AN1
engine class lineage: MOS_PackFile, MOS_CVar, CON_Cache, DES_File, DSP_Display,
NOD_Camera all present -- it is the evolved AN1 engine. So AN2 is analyzable/
patchable the same way as the GOG AN1 exe if needed.

--- 11b. AN2 PAK FORMAT (CRACKED, checksum-validated) ---
Same MASSIVEFILE family, minor evolution from AN1 (section 2). Differences:
  * Header: magic(12) + version uint16(=3) + SUBversion uint16(=2) [-> "v3.2"]
    + file_count uint32 + copyright(60) + 4 bytes (AN1's "LPT\0" is now 00 00
    00 00). Entries still start at offset 84, still 132 bytes (128 name + 4 size).
  * SAME 64-byte KEY as AN1 (hex in section 2 / unpak.py). Verified: those exact
    key bytes sit in AN2.dat @ file offset 3264752, right before "MASSIVEFILE".
  * FILENAME cipher: dec[i] = enc[i] - KEY[((n-3)+i) % 64]  for entry n.
    (AN1 was (n+i)%64. The ONLY change is the -3 start offset per entry.)
    Read from AN2.dat's decrypt loop: eax = KEY + (edi & 0x3f), edi = n-3.
  * SIZE cipher: size = size_enc - u32(KEY[((n-3)+0x68) % 60]).
    (AN1 was abs(u32(KEY[n%60]) - size_enc).)
  * Validation: data_start + sum(sizes) == filesize, exact, on every PAK.
  * unpak.py NOW auto-detects AN1 vs AN2 (ver_hi==0 -> AN1 shift 0; else AN2
    shift -3) and prints "Format ANx vX: checksum OK". Delivered/tested.
  data0=movies/GUI(34 files), data1=textures(1602), data2=audio+881 msb,
  data3=2148 msb+scripts, data4=1728 msb. ~5700 .msb total.

--- 11c. AN2 ASSET COMPATIBILITY (the bridge works) ---
AN2 uses the SAME directory layout AND formats as AN1:
  vfx/msh/*.msb (meshes), vfx/tex/*.dds (textures), map/<lvl>/lmsh/, .osd, .col,
  .sco, .des -- all identical. .msb header version is 0x0200, SAME as AN1.
  PROVEN: our AN1 load_msb() parses AN2 meshes directly (tested mer_bomber.msb
  = 1240 verts/774 faces, rendered fine; atl_techbomber_tur01b, etc.). DDS via
  Pillow works. So porting an AN2 ship into AN1 = extract .msb + .dds + .osd,
  all directly usable. NEW in AN2: env cubemaps (map/<lvl>/cubemap/envcubemap.dds
  = reflections, a graphics upgrade AN1 lacked), ogg audio, .usi GUI, .vid movies.

--- 11d. THE .vc FILES (VertexColors) -- resolved ---
.vc = plain-text "[VertexColors]{ V0 = R,G,B,A ... }" per-vertex baked lighting
for STATIC level geometry. Bluish-grey values = baked underwater ambient. AN1 lit
static structures via lightmaps (ltex/*.dds); AN2 adds per-vertex baked colors.
DISTRIBUTION: 0% of ship/object meshes (vfx/msh/) have .vc (0/436); 95% of level
structures (map/*/lmsh/) have them (4084/4321). => IRRELEVANT for ship porting;
only matters if porting whole AN2 levels (and even then AN1 uses lightmaps).

--- 11e. AN2 STATION SYSTEM (the big leap over AN1's menu) ---
AN1 stations = static menu (.des layout + mtake text + 3D ambient bg). AN2 built
a real ANIMATED 2D GUI ENGINE: the .usi format (compiled .usi.bin + localized
.loc text; .loc/.usi content is UTF-16). .usi is a retained-mode UI scene graph:
nodes Window/Group/Animation/Bitmap/Reference/Sound, animation states Normal/
BeforeAppear/AfterDisappear/AfterDelayWindow (windows animate in with frame-draw
+ static/noise fx; speaker highlighted, listener darkened; VCR-style dialogue
play/stop/ff/fb buttons). Everything under gui/gui_station_ref/ (briefing,
debriefing, intercomdialog, equip, ship, shop screens) + per-station screens
gui_station_<name>.usi.bin (neopolis, atacama, eltopo, harvester_1a1..6b which
change with campaign progress). ROOM-based navigation: dat/sty/sdialog.des wires
185 station dialogues, each with a Room number (1-15) and Type ("story"=in-person
169x, "intercom"=remote 16x). dat/sty/person.des = 47 named characters (Amitab,
Angelina, El Topo, Finch, Barkeeper Joe...) each with portrait + ImageMood0..
(mood variants). dat/sty/room.des + station.des define rooms/stations. You move
between 2D rooms (dialog_<room>.tga backdrops) via arrow buttons + station maps
(map_N_M.tga) to FIND people. It's a point-and-click-adventure-style hub.

--- 11f. AN2 GUI IMAGE FORMAT ---
All GUI/station art = TGA (Truevision Targa), UNCOMPRESSED, 24bpp (RGB) or 32bpp
(RGBA for frames/buttons with alpha). 522 .tga in gui/. Deliberately NOT DDS: the
2D pre-rendered backdrops avoid DXT artifacts on smooth gradients/text. Sizes:
full-screen bg.tga/logos = 1024x768; station_skyline.tga = 1024x311; room preview
thumbs dialog_*.tga = 220x88; nav maps map_N_M.tga = 354x318; world map.tga =
1660x2160 (10MB). The big bg/skyline images are pre-rendered stills (not realtime
3D) -- the 2003 way to show rich station scenery cheaply.

--- 11g. AN2 SHIPS & FACTIONS (verified from dat/sty/ship.des + in-game text) ---
PLAYER ships (dat/sty/ship.des, 4 selectable, full stats + env cubemap):

--- 11h. AN2 NEXT STEPS (if pursued) ---
  * Port an AN2 ship into AN1: extract <ship>.msb + textures + build an AN1 .osd,
    place via .sco (our make_vessel technique). Formats are compatible.
  * Add AN2 meshes to the level viewer (same load_msb).
  * AN2's cubemap reflections are a data feature AN1 lacks -- interesting but
    AN1's engine won't render them without engine work.
  * .usi format not deeply reversed yet (only its node vocabulary); would need
    work to author new AN2 station UI.
  * .vid movie format (AN2's "Krass Video") and .vc details left unexplored.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
12. ASSET & LEVEL CATALOGS (JSON) -- generated data, quick-reference
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Two JSON catalogs were generated from the AN1 PAKs (regenerate with
build_catalog.py, which needs des_text.py; both shipped to outputs). USE THESE
as a fast lookup for "what assets/levels/ships/world-settings exist" instead of
re-parsing PAKs from scratch.

  aquanox1_assets.json  (~1.5 MB): every one of the 10,348 unique PAK files with
    {path, category, size_bytes, pak}. Categories: mesh 3312, audio 2386,
    texture_dds 1435, object_definition(.osd) 1262, texture_tga 845, collision
    511, data(.des) 268, terrain_heightmap 174, script(.sco) 84, ... Friendly
    name+description added where derivable (ships from ship_d.des, guns from
    gun_d.des, else prefix-based). OSD entries also list their referenced meshes.

  aquanox1_levels.json  (~395 KB): all 43 levels (30 campaign, 9 multiplayer,
    4 instant-fight), each with: real name (MP maps -> Narrow/Liberty/Vertical/
