    Nightfall/Stream/Isolation/Canyon/Sightless via maplist~.des; instant-fight
    via instantfightlist; campaign = code + "Kapitel X, Haupt-/Nebenmission"),
    type, description, world_settings (ambient_light [R,G,B], parallel_light_*,
    fog file, fx sky/rays/plankton variant, view_distance), object counts,
    ships (with resolved names), referenced_osds, and meshes_used (OSD->mesh
    resolved + own lmsh). Plus a campaign_briefings reference list (40 readable
    mission objectives from briefing_d.des).

Name sources (all PLAIN-TEXT .des, parse with des_text.parse_des): ship_d.des
(9 ships: Toiler, Drowsy Maggie, Avenger, Tiburon, Skipjack, Vortex, Succubus 2,
Voodoo Breath, Phobocaster -- each Name/ShortDescription/Info/stats), gun_d.des
(37 gun names), item_d.des (33 shop items), mission_d.des (30 campaign missions,
Name/EngineScript), briefing_d.des (40 briefings). NOTE the _d.des files are
plain text; the ~-suffixed ones are cooked (use descook).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
13. UNUSED-ASSET ANALYSIS (build_unused.py) -- what's referenced by NOTHING
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Tool: build_unused.py -> aquanox1_unused_assets.json. METHOD (important): an
asset counts as USED if its basename/stem appears in ANY corpus file -- all 84
level/MP scripts (.sco kstr, split on / \ .), ALL 1262 OSDs, ALL meshes (catches
sub-mesh refs), all .des + dat/ config. Conservative on purpose (few false
positives). KEY GOTCHAS learned building it:
  * OSD "namemesh" uses .msh (source ext) but the file is .msb (compiled) -> must
    treat .msh<->.msb as the same stem.
  * meshes are referenced by BARE basename (no path): atl_bomber, not vfx/msh/...
  * OSD files are cooked -> named "<name>~.osd"; scripts reference "<name>" (no ~)
    -> STRIP the trailing '~' before comparing, or you get ~1240 false unused OSDs.
  * many meshes are pulled in by DIFFERENTLY-named OSDs (cla_dogstar via
    wng_chaka~.osd) or assembly OSDs (atl_ghost_turret via atl_ghost~.osd), so a
    naive level->OSD->mesh chain massively over-reports. The corpus-substring
    method is what's reliable.

RESULT (of 9954 evaluated assets, 1188 referenced nowhere). aquanox1_unused_
assets.json is now a STANDALONE ENRICHED catalog: top-level counts_by_bucket +
counts_by_category, then "assets" = one record per unused file {path, category,
size_bytes, pak, bucket, name?, description?} (names/descriptions pulled from
ship_d/gun_d.des where derivable). Sorted so cut-content comes first.
BUCKETS (counts_by_bucket): cut_content_candidate 643, audio_engine_loaded 278
(mp3 via playlist/config, NOT cut), terrain_source 184, player_shop_ship 41,
ui_menu 30, hud 12. The interesting one is cut_content_candidate = 643 assets,
of which 88 meshes + 184 OSDs (+ textures/collision). Highlights:
  * ent_* = 35 meshes -- EnTrOx is the BIGGEST cut-content source in AN1 (e.g.
    ent_changbomber_* "Chang bomber" variants). Fits our AN2 finding that EnTrOx
    is a full faction THERE -- assets existed in AN1, got cut, promoted in AN2.
  * atl_pleasuredome, gen_dome2 (cut dome structures), atl_d+p_det_* (command/
    corner/stadion detail pieces), atl_tank2_body, ent_turb2, and a/b/c_schrott*
    debris-variant meshes.
  * CUT SHIP VARIANTS of shop ships (names resolve via ship_d.des): 
    gen_tiburon_docking (a docking variant of the Tiburon freighter),
    gen_vortex_inst (instant-variant of the Vortex hybrid).
  * CUT MP TEAM CONTENT: plag_phobocaster_team_blue.col / _team_red.col --
    team-coloured collision for the Phobocaster => a team/MP mode for it was
    planned and cut.
Non-cut unreferenced (used by other systems): audio .mp3, terrain source .dds,
some HUD/menu/shop-ship assets. Full per-asset records in the JSON.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
14. SCRIPT<->ENGINE API -- how the Lua scripts drive the KRASS engine
    (analysis session 1; extracted from GOG Aqua.exe + verified vs script usage)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The connective tissue: level .sco scripts (Lua 4.0) call C functions that the
engine (C++, class NOD_Script, functions named NOD_Script::LUAF_*) registers as
Lua globals. Standard Lua libs (math/string/base) ARE static luaL_reg tables in
.data (@0x6001c8 math, @0x600298 string, @0x600338 base). The GAME API is NOT a
static table -- registered per-function (C++), so no {name,func} array to walk.
Instead: extracted all API-shaped strings from the exe (1802 across 111 prefixes,
but most are DirectX/Win error enums: DMUS_/DPNERR_/DIERR_/DXFILEERR_/DERR_/
DSERR_/ERROR_ = NOISE, not script API) and INTERSECTED with the set of all
identifiers actually used across the 84 scripts. Result: 209 real, USED script
API entries. Full reference in aquanox1_script_api.json. The layered model:

  SCENE GRAPH -- Node_ (9): CreateNode, Find, AddSon, EnterSimulation,
    LeaveSimulation, ParseIniFile (loads an .osd onto a node), GetName/GetPath/
    GetTime. Node classes (for CreateNode) via NOD_ (10): NOD_Camera, Director,
    Directory, FX_Stream, NodeRef, Path, PatrolArea, Position, Scenario, Trigger.
  PHYSICS OBJECT -- Body_ (32): SetCS (pos+orientation), SetPosition/SetOrient-
    ation, GetPosition, AddItem, SetCargoKey, SetNameKey, SetFriendOrFoeID,
    Freeze, and the SHIELD system: Set/Kinetic/Emp shields, *ShieldBarrierReached
    (KIN/EMP/BIO), escape-pod + shield-guard activate/deactivate, enemy info
    (Request/Inform/Reject Kin/Emp/ShotHits). ShieldedBody_ (8) = shield detail:
    CoupleFather, Make(In)Vulnerable, MakeSelectiveVulnerableAgainst, DeepDestruct,
    SetLogarithmicMode. => AN's 3-damage-type model (kinetic/EMP/bio) lives here.
  AI / BEHAVIOUR -- Vessel_ (21) dynamic-ship AI: Goto/GotoFromTo/FromPosition,
    Patrol, AttackPatrol, AttackTurrets, Escort, Infiltrate, Sink, PseudoExplode,
    Stop(WithHeading), Dipol In/Out, Collision/Avoidance objects+terrain.
    Vehicle_ (11) = same verbs for vehicles (+ParkPatrol). Turret_ (4):
    ComplexAttack, Patrol, SetAngleArea, SetAcousticMode. Character_ (2):
    Start/StopAnimation (pilot anims).
  MISSION / WORLD -- Game_ (26): atmosphere (SetAmbientLight, SetParallelLightT/B,
    LoadFog/SetFog, SetCaustic Terrain/Object/Building, SetLightCache,
    SetTerrainDepth, SetDecompressionHeight), music (LoadMusic/PlayMusic),
    LOADING PROGRESS (Enter/Advance/Leave -- see section 6b-1!), MissionEnd,
    Enemy-Matrix (Set/Get/Reset -- the friend/foe table), SetWayPoint, SetTaskText-
    Key, LoadMTake (mission dialogue), LoadTextTable/LoadTextures, SendRadio-
    MessageTake.
  CINEMATICS -- Camera_ (11): Cut, LookAt/Ahead/From, MoveTo/Into, Roll/Zoom To/At,
    ShakeHit, SetBackPlane (= view distance!). Director_ (2): Begin/EndCutscene.
  MISSION LOGIC -- Trigger_ (14): Enter/Leave x Presence/Acoustic/Visible +
    SetPresenceSphere/VisibilityCone/BoxMode/InterestList (proximity & line-of-
    sight sensors). WayPoint_ (7): Attach, SetRadius, SetTakeKey_Enter/Inside/
    Leave/Outside/Activate (fires a dialogue "take" when the player hits a WP).
    PatrolArea_ (6), Position_ (4), Stream_ (4, water currents), LaserWall_ (2),
    ItemBox_ (3: item pickups w/ respawn -- relevant to the "own item pickup" idea).
  SCRIPT RUNTIME -- Script_ (10): BindThread, DoFile, SetTimer/StopTimer,
    Log/LogRaw/Error, GetFunctionName/GetLineNumber, IsNodeRefValid. (C_ScriptTimer
    from section 6b-1 is the per-frame driver bound via BindEasy on "/script".)
  CONSTANT ENUMS (script-visible): TORPEDO_ (6: bullshark/flashshark/maneater/
    stanley/thresh/tiger), GUN_ (4), AMMO_ (2), DEVICE_ (2: booster/repair),
    Gun_SetDog/SniperBullet.

BIG PICTURE: a level = a SCENE GRAPH of nodes; combat objects get a BODY (physics
+ 3-type shields) and a VESSEL/VEHICLE behaviour (AI verbs); TRIGGERS + WAYPOINTS
fire mission events + dialogue takes; GAME_ sets the world mood + drives loading/
mission-end + the enemy matrix; CAMERA_/DIRECTOR_ run cutscenes; SCRIPT_ timers
tick it all. BindEasy(Node_Find(path), name, S_table) wires a behaviour table
onto a node (the S_o#### state machines). Data (.des) feeds names/prices/dialogue;
.osd feeds mesh+collision+shields per object type; .sco is the per-level logic.

STILL TODO (future sessions): map individual LUAF_ C-impls to addresses; trace the
enemy-matrix + friend/foe eval; the BHV_/STY_/MT_/TAUNT_/TEAM_/MAI_ enum groups
(AI taunts, story states, team commands); how .osd shield params reach ShieldedBody.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15. ENGINE SUBSYSTEM STACK -- how a script call reaches physics/AI/network
    (analysis session 1 cont.; from GOG Aqua.exe string/subsystem extraction)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The exe's internal C++ subsystems (name-prefix = subsystem) reveal the full stack
ABOVE and BELOW the 209 script functions of section 14:

  MAI_ (18) = top application / menu-flow controller: MAI_Game_Level/Load/Save,
    MAI_Dock, MAI_Menu, MAI_Dialog, MAI_Handler, MAI_Result, MAI_Select_Movie,
    MAI_Menu_PlayerAddCredit. This is the app state machine: menu <-> dock/station
    <-> mission, save/load, movie playback, credits.
  GUI_ (35) = the menu screens themselves: GUI_Menu_Main/Multiplayer/Controls/
    Sound/Player/Setup/... one per screen. MT_ (42) = the menu TEXT-table keys
    (MT_MAIN, MT_MULTIPLAYER, MT_TAUNTS, ...) that fill those screens.
  STY_ (18) = STORY / DATA subsystem -- the bridge from the .des databases into
    the engine: STY_Base, Briefing, Command, Dialog, Difficulty, Inventory, Item,
    Mission, Mood, Movie, Option, Person, Player, Room, Ship, Station, Take,
    Acknowledge. Each maps 1:1 to a dat/sty/*.des DB (mission_d, ship_d, item_d,
    person, briefing, mood_d, movie_d, mtake_*...). So the base/station UI, shop,
    dialogue, inventory and difficulty are all driven by STY_ reading .des.
  PM_ (35) = PROCESS MANAGER = the networked execution layer. It MIRRORS the
    script API: PM_CreateNode<->Node_CreateNode, PM_EnterSimulation, PM_MissionEnd
    <->Game_MissionEnd, PM_GotoStart/PatrolStart/InfiltrateStart<->Vessel_*,
    PM_LoadMusic/PlayMusic, PM_ActivateShieldGuard, PM_BindThread -- PLUS network
    ops: PM_CreateClientScript, PM_CreatePlayerOnClient, PM_SendClientID/GameInfo/
    ServerInfo, PM_LoadServerScript. => every script command is routed through PM_,
    which executes it locally AND can replicate it to clients. This is WHY AN1 is
    multiplayer-capable at all, and WHY our error was "NOD_Script::PM_BindThread()
    node is NULL" (section: the toiler-bind bug): binding a script thread to a node
    goes through PM_BindThread, which got a NULL because Node_Find missed.
  BHV_ (26) = BEHAVIOUR implementations = the actual AI behind the Vessel_/Vehicle_
    /Turret_/Character_ script verbs. Naming: BHV_<Actor>_<A|S>_<Action>, where
    _A_ = Action, _S_ = State. E.g. BHV_Vessel_A_Attack/Goto/Patrol/Infiltrate/
    DipolIn/Out/EscapeTo/Stop, BHV_Turret_A_ComplexAttack/Follow/Patrol,
    BHV_Body_S_EscapePod/ShieldGuard/WatchEnemiePatrol, BHV_PhysicalCharacter_A_
    Attack/Goto/LookTo. So: script Vessel_Attack -> LUAF_ -> PM_GotoStart/etc ->
    BHV_Vessel_A_Attack drives the ship.
  Radar_ (24) = HUD friend-or-foe VISUALS: ColorAnchor/Object/Locked x Enemy/
    Friend/Neutral/Player/Unknown (+Offset), ColorTerrain, Scale, Translation,
    HandleReticleObject. The visible red/green/etc. of the friend-or-foe system
    (whose logic is the Game_ enemy-matrix from section 14) is set here.

MULTIPLAYER VOCAB (script-invisible; 0 uses in campaign .sco, they're MP chat/UI):
  TAUNT_ (15) nautical taunts (WALKTHEPLANK, YOHOHO, DAVEYJONES, FEEDINGFRENZY...);
  TEAM_ (13) CTF/team quick-commands (GRABTHEIRFLAG, COVERMYWAKE, IMTAKINGFIRE...).

FULL EXECUTION CHAIN (one line): .des data -> STY_ loads it; player picks a mission
in GUI_/MAI_ -> engine loads the level .sco; the script builds a Node_ scene graph,
gives combat objects a Body_ (physics + KIN/EMP/BIO shields) and binds a behaviour
table via BindEasy -> routed through PM_ (local + network) -> BHV_ actions drive
the AI; Trigger_/WayPoint_ fire mission events + dialogue Takes; Game_ sets mood +
enemy-matrix + loading + MissionEnd; Camera_/Director_ play cutscenes; Radar_ paints
friend/foe; Script_ timers (C_ScriptTimer on "/script") tick the whole thing.

STILL TODO (future sessions): resolve LUAF_<->PM_<->BHV_ addresses to confirm the
routing; dump the enemy-matrix eval; how .osd shield fields reach ShieldedBody_;
the STY_ .des loader format details; the DMUS_ (DirectMusic) dynamic-music layer.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
16. THE .osd OBJECT-DEFINITION FORMAT -- asset -> playable object (the "recipe")
    (analysis session 1 cont.; decoded from vfx/osd/gen/gen_toiler~.osd)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
.osd = cooked DES (parse with descook). It's the COMPLETE recipe that turns raw
assets (mesh/texture/collision) into a simulated object: physics + AI + shields +
weapons + FX. Node_ParseIniFile(node,"osd/...") applies one onto a scene node.
VALUE ENCODING: every field value is RAW little-endian bytes -- float32 (mass
'\x00\x80;E'=3000.0, most handling/AI params), int, or uint16 (shield values),
0xFFFFFFFF (-1) = "off/none". descook gives raw bytes; struct.unpack them.

SECTION SCHEMA (a nod_vessel; other classes are subsets):
  [sed] class=nod_vessel   [class] namecla + namenod (object's class + id)
  [visual] m0 = mesh (.msh, resolves to .msb), l0 = LOD distance
  [collision] active, restitution, mass(f32), maxnumofcollisions/shothits,
    collisiondescription (.col file)
  [control] the HANDLING model: move p/n velocity+time, strafe horr/vert vel+t,
    turn p/n vel + rate + turnr, swing, move/turnfriction. (Per-ship feel.)
  [stream] water-current factor    [dipolbehaviour] afterburner in/out FX
  [emitter] presence/visible/visibilityblocking/soundemissionlevel -> how this
    object is DETECTABLE (feeds Trigger_ sensors + enemy detection)
  [avoidance] obstacle-avoid AI (description e.g. "SmallVessel", lookahead, radius)
  [escapebehaviour] / [attackbehaviour] / [patrolbehaviour] = the AI TUNING per
    ship. attackbehaviour is big: velocity, coneoffire, align{ahead,left,back,
    right}prob, wave/strafe/torpedo timing, defensivity -> this is the dogfight
    personality of each enemy type, all data-driven (no code change to retune).
  [enemydetection] visibility range+cone, presence range, audio range+sensitivity
  [buzzerparam] buzzer-drone params    [dying] dyingdata -> std_vessel.des
  [shield] <-- ANSWERS the ShieldedBody TODO. showshield, damagepropagationratio,
    then THREE sub-shields matching AN's 3 damage types:
      [kinetic] out(regen), leftright/frontback/topbottom active flags,
        x/y/z-extension-in, and PER-FACE strengths right/left/front/back/top/bot
        {in} + {inmax} (uint16; 0xFFFFFFFF = that face unshielded). Toiler e.g.
        only rightin=8000 -> weak scout.
      [emp] out/in/inmax     [bio] out/in/inmax
    => ShieldedBody_ (section 15) reads exactly this; the KIN/EMP/BIO + per-face
       geometry is pure .osd data.
  [textkey] name + cargo0/1/2 = keys into the text table (display name / cargo)
  [sound][engine] engine loop wav + freq/vol/range
  [child0..N] = SUB-NODES bolted onto the object, each its own [class]+[localcs]
    (translation+rotation) +params. In the toiler: 4x nod_halo (running-light
    glows), nod_npc_gun (bullettype=bul_vendetta2_p, [phase0..2] shots+delays),
    nod_torpedolauncher (torpedoname=stanley), nod_buzzerlauncher, nod_particle-
    emitter (bubble trail, full [particle][visual] rgba/size keyframes), nod_trail
    (wake, rgba0..4 gradient). => WEAPONS ARE CHILD NODES: a ship's guns/torpedoes/
    buzzers are child OSD entries, not separate systems. Same for all FX.

BIG PICTURE ADDITION: the section-14 script API operates on objects whose entire
behaviour envelope (handling, AI personality, sensors, shields, weapons, FX) is
declared in .osd DATA. So "porting a ship" = its .msb + .col + textures + this .osd
(all data). Retuning an enemy = editing attackbehaviour/shield fields, no code.

STILL TODO: std_vessel.des (shared death/debris behaviour); the text-table format
(textkey -> string); nod_npc_gun bullettype -> bul_*.osd chain; how [control]
maps to the physics integrator; STY_ .des loader; enemy-matrix eval; DMUS_ music.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
17. THE COMBAT / DAMAGE MODEL -- weapons, projectiles, and the shield loop closed
    (analysis session 1 cont.; decoded bul_*/tor_* OSDs)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The elegant core of AN combat: the SAME [shield] block (section 16) is used for
BOTH defense (ships) and offense (projectiles). A projectile is a "negative
shield" -- its [kinetic]/[emp]/[bio] "out" fields are the DAMAGE it deals of each
type. So one data structure expresses the whole KIN/EMP/BIO system symmetrically.

  GUN -> BULLET: a ship's weapon is a child node (section 16) nod_npc_gun with
  bullettype="bul_<name>" (+ nod_torpedolauncher torpedoname=, nod_buzzerlauncher).
  bullettype resolves to vfx/osd/.../bul_<name>~.osd (player MP guns under
  vfx/osd/mp_weapon/). Torpedoes = tor_*~.osd. gun_d.des holds the player-facing
  names/prices; the bul_/tor_ OSD holds the actual behaviour.

  PROJECTILE .osd schema: [info] name+lifetime(s)+sniperammo; [visual] mesh/
  billboard; [control] vmax (speed); [friendlyfire] friendlyfirepoints; [impact]
  [ground]/[metal] emitter+sound (hit FX depend on surface); [emit][sound] (fire
  sound); [child] nod_trail (tracer). And crucially [shield] = the DAMAGE VECTOR:
  damagepropagationratio, terrainoutpoints (terrain dmg), and per type
  kinetic/emp/bio "out" = points dealt.

  MEASURED damage vectors (KIN / EMP / BIO):
    lasergatlin 350/0/0, sizzler 1750/0/0, vendetta 210-1300/0/0 (pure kinetic);
    empactor 0/425/0 (pure EMP); doommortar 2400/600/0, plasma* ~350/20/0 (mixed);
    torpedoes mostly kinetic & huge (tor_bigbang2 30000, maneater 7000, tiger
    6000), but tor_flash 500/2000 and tor_leech 150/1500 are EMP torps. (No BIO>0
    in player/std weapons -- BIO is the Biont/"Angel" faction's damage; ships still
    carry a bio shield to survive them.)

  THE LOOP: projectile hits a ship face -> engine subtracts the projectile's
  kinetic/emp/bio "out" from that ship's matching per-face shield "in" (section 16),
  scaled by damagepropagationratio; when a face's shield is depleted, hull/systems
  take it. This is why ships carry THREE independent shield banks and why EMP
  weapons (empactor, tor_flash/leech) exist to bypass a kinetic-tanked target.
  ShieldedBody_ (section 15) runs this; Body_*ShieldBarrierReached callbacks
  (section 14) fire when a bank breaks.

FULL COMBAT PICTURE: .osd defines a ship's guns (child nodes) + its 3-way shields;
guns reference bul_/tor_ OSDs whose [shield].out is the damage vector; the script
API (Vessel_Attack via PM_->BHV_) drives firing; Radar_ colors friend/foe; the
enemy-matrix (Game_*EnemyMatrix*) decides who shoots whom. Combat is almost
entirely DATA -- retune a weapon or shield by editing OSD numbers, no code.

STILL TODO: enemy-matrix eval (team vs team hostility table); std_vessel.des death/
debris; text-table (textkey->string) format; buzzer/mine behaviour; DMUS_ dynamic
music; and finally resolving LUAF_/PM_/BHV_ code addresses for the call routing.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
18. TEAM / ENEMY-MATRIX SYSTEM -- who attacks whom (the friend-or-foe backbone)
    (analysis session 1 cont.; derived from all campaign .sco usage)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Decides, for every pair of objects, whether they fight/ally/ignore. Two parts:
  (1) TEAM MEMBERSHIP: Body_SetFriendOrFoeID(node, teamID) puts an object on one
      of 16 teams (IDs 0-15). Observed assignment counts across the campaign:
      team 0 = 4188 (the huge default bucket: civilians, traffic, environment,
      neutral props), then 1..15 used per mission for the mission's factions
      (e.g. 2 = the player's side in our sp_1n1, 4 = the hostiles). Higher IDs are
      rarer (11-15 only a handful) = special one-off groups.
  (2) THE MATRIX: a 16x16 table; each cell (row team vs col team) is one of four
      constants: N neutral (default; 16703 sets), E enemy (660), F friend (598),
      U unknown (50). Set with Game_SetEnemyMatrixElement(row, col, VALUE),
      cleared with Game_ResetEnemyMatrix, queried with Game_GetEnemyMatrixElement.
      Cells are set per-DIRECTION (A->B and B->A set separately; normally made
      symmetric, but asymmetry is possible = A hunts B while B ignores A). Levels
      typically reset to all-N then set only the few E/F pairs they need (that's
      the repeated "= N" blocks: a shared default-fill pattern seen ~70x).

MEANING of the cell value when team A meets team B (matrix[A][B]):
  E -> hostile: A's AI (Vessel_Attack / BHV_) treats B as a valid target.
  F -> allied:  A protects/escorts B, won't fire.
  N -> neutral: ignore each other (civilians, non-combatants).
  U -> unknown: not yet identified (investigate / until IFF resolves).
This is the gate BEFORE the combat model of section 17: the enemy-matrix picks
targets; Radar_ (section 15) colors them (enemy red / friend green / neutral /
unknown); then Vessel_ AI (via PM_->BHV_) engages; then projectile [shield].out
vs ship [shield].in resolves damage (section 17).

WHY 16 teams + a matrix (not just "friend/enemy"): AquaNox missions have several
mutually-hostile factions on the field at once (Atlantic Federation, Crawlers,
EnTrOx, Tornado Zone, pirates, Bionts...) plus civilians -- the matrix lets any
faction be enemy/neutral/ally to any other independently, and lets a mission flip
a relationship mid-story (betrayal, alliance) by rewriting a couple of cells.

MODDING NOTE: to make our sp_1n1 toilers hostile we set matrix[2][4]=E, [4][2]=E
and [2][2]=F, and gave toilers FriendOrFoeID 4, player 2. That IS this system.

STILL TODO: std_vessel.des (death/debris); text-table (textkey->string); buzzer/
mine behaviour; DMUS_ dynamic music; resolve LUAF_/PM_/BHV_ code addresses.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
19. LUA BINDING IN THE BINARY -- the 398-function registry + call routing
    (analysis session 2; disassembled the GOG Aqua.exe)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
CONFIRMED how scripts reach the engine, from the exe itself. The game API is NOT a
static luaL_reg table (only the stock Lua libs math@0x6001c8/string@0x600298/
base@0x600338 are). Instead each script function is a C++ COMMAND OBJECT with a
vtable. Proof: the getter for "Node_CreateNode" is a 3-byte function
  0x4bc770: mov eax, 0x617078 ("Node_CreateNode"); ret     <- vtable[0]=GetName()
and its vtable (@0x5f8de8) next slot is
  0x4b10b0: mov eax, 2; ret                                  <- vtable[1]=GetArgCount (=2 params)
later slots = the Execute/impl. So: registration walks the command objects, calls
GetName() for the Lua global name + GetArgCount() for arity, installs a dispatcher.

EXTRACTION: scanning .text for the pattern `B8 <ptr-to-.data-string> C3`
(mov eax,name; ret) yields the COMPLETE registry -> 398 functions (getter address
each). Full map in aquanox1_engine_api_registry.json {name: getter_addr,
used_in_scripts}. Subsystems (getter counts): Vessel 56, ANT 48, Body 46, Game 35,
Vehicle 28, PhysicalCharacter 23, Camera 21, Turret 17, Trigger 16, Node 14,
WayPoint/Player 11, ShieldedBody 8, ParticleEmitter 7, PatrolArea/Light/Emitter 6,
Script/Gun/Stream 5, Position/ItemBox 4, Mine/Console 3.

REGISTERED but NOT used by any campaign .sco (183 used / 398 total -> 215 spare) =
the engine's hidden capability surface:
  * ANT_ (48) = the FULL NETWORKING layer (ANT_Client_Connect*, ANT_Message_
    Authenticate*, session/map-change/duplication) -- multiplayer netcode, exposed
    to scripts but SP campaigns never call it.
  * AI CALLBACK PROTOCOL: the *Over/*Override/*Finished/*Arrived/Inform* families
    on Vessel_/Vehicle_/Turret_/PhysicalCharacter_/WayPoint_ are ENGINE->SCRIPT
    callbacks fired when an AI action completes or is interrupted (Vessel_Goto-
    Arrived, DockingFinished, AttackOver...). So the behaviour layer (BHV_, sec 15)
    is event-driven: script starts an action, engine calls back on completion.
    Also extra verbs: Vessel_Dock/DockAndLeave.
  * Body_Get{Kinetic,Emp,Bio}Shield + Body_EMPParalyzed + Body_ForceEjection =
    the shield QUERY side + the EMP-paralysis mechanic (why EMP weapons matter).
  * Player_First/Second/ThirdWarning = the out-of-bounds warnings (seen in 1n1),
    Player_PlunderItems, Player_ActivateFF.
  * Light_ (6, dynamic lights), ParticleEmitter_ (6, runtime FX), Gun_ (3:
    AddPhase/FlushPhases/SetBullet = runtime weapon reconfigure, matches OSD
    [phase]), Mine_ (3), Console_ (3: the dev console Activate/Input), Camera_
    extras, Game_Load{Scenario,Script,ServerScript}/CreateClientScript (MP script
    loading)/EndGame/TextMessage.

TAKEAWAY: the modding surface is far larger than campaigns use -- networking,
dynamic lights, runtime particle/gun control, a dev console, and a full AI-callback
protocol are all script-reachable. The 398-entry registry (with addresses) is the
authoritative API list; section 14's 209 was only the campaign-used subset.

STILL TODO: resolve each command-object's Execute slot -> impl address (walk the
vtables from the getters); std_vessel.des; text-table format; DMUS_ music.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
20. HOW A SCRIPT CALL IS DISPATCHED -- reflection metadata, not per-fn Execute
    (analysis session 2 cont.; GOG Aqua.exe vtable analysis)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Tried to resolve each command-object's Execute address by walking its vtable from
the GetName getter. FINDING: there is NO simple "Execute is vtable slot N" per
function. The command objects share ONE enormous combined vtable/RTTI region
(~2869 slots, 0x5f70c8..0x5f9d98) that is almost entirely SMALL METADATA
ACCESSORS. Each script function is described by reflection metadata:
  * GetName()      -> the Lua global name  (the B8 <str> C3 getters of section 19)
  * GetArgCount()  -> arity. Confirmed & correct: Node_CreateNode=2, Node_Find=1,
                     Body_SetCS=2, Game_MissionEnd=1, Game_SetEnemyMatrixElement=3
                     (matches how we called them in sp_1n1).
  * GetParamType(i)-> per-argument type code (small stubs returning e.g. 0xd, 0x10;
                     these are the Lua<->C marshalling type tags).
A GENERIC DISPATCHER (registered once with Lua) uses this metadata to pull the
right number/types of args off the Lua stack, then invokes the implementation.
So the routing is DATA/REFLECTION-DRIVEN, not a hand-written C function per API
name -- which is exactly why section 19's registry is a clean list of metadata
objects and why there's no static luaL_reg table.

HYBRID API: some script-facing names have NO C getter at all -> they are LUA-
DEFINED helper functions layered on top of the C primitives. Confirmed:
Vessel_Attack and BindEasy are Lua helpers (BindEasy = Node_Find + the bind
wrapper we use everywhere; Vessel_Attack wraps lower-level C verbs). So the full
script API = C command-objects (398, metadata-driven) + a layer of Lua convenience
functions loaded at startup.

REAL IMPL SAMPLE: one substantial engine method disassembled cleanly:
NOD_Body::ParseLCS() @ 0x467da0 -- reads "LocalCS"/"Translation"/"Rotation0" from a
DES node, emits "(%s): Get \"%s\" in file \"%s\" failed" / "DES FILE ERROR" on
missing fields. Shows the engine's DES-parsing + error-reporting style (the same
"[X] ERROR" pattern seen in log.txt).

PRACTICAL UPSHOT: to understand/modify what a given API call DOES, the metadata
(name/arity/paramtypes in the shared vtable) tells you the signature; the behaviour
is reached through the generic dispatcher, so static "one function = one address"
mapping isn't the right model here. For modding this doesn't matter -- arity +
param types (now known) are what you need to call them correctly from .sco.

STILL TODO: decode the GetParamType codes (0xd/0x10/...) into concrete Lua types
(node handle / float / string / int); find the generic dispatcher entry; std_
vessel.des; text-table format; DMUS_ music.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
21. LUA<->C PARAMETER TYPE CODES -- what the dispatcher marshals
    (analysis session 2 cont.; unicorn-emulated the metadata accessors)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The generic dispatcher (section 20) reads a per-argument TYPE CODE from each
command object's metadata to pull the right value off the Lua stack. Emulated the
param-type accessor stubs (unicorn) against functions of KNOWN signature. Cleanest
readout is the Game_ class family (accessor = vtable slot+12 there); the TYPE CODES
themselves are the global Lua-stack marshalling tags:
    0x1 = int
    0x2 = enum / named constant (the E/F/N matrix values, difficulty, etc.)
    0x5 = bool / flag   (e.g. Game_MissionEnd's success arg)
    0x7 = float
    0xc = string (plain text)
    0xd = name/key string -- a string used as a LOOKUP KEY: node/class name
          (Node_CreateNode arg0, Node_Find path), waypoint key, text-table key.
Verified examples: Game_SetEnemyMatrixElement(int,int,enum)=1,1,2;
Game_SetAmbientLight(f,f,f)=7,7,7; Game_SetTerrainDepth(float)=7;
Game_LoadFog(string)=0xc; Game_SetWayPoint(key)=0xd.

IMPORTANT CAVEAT (per-class layout): the type CODES are universal, but WHICH vtable
slot exposes the param-type accessor differs per C++ base class. For the Game_
family it's slot+12; Node_/Body_/Camera_ classes put it elsewhere, so reading
slot+12 blindly on those gives unrelated values (that earlier "inconsistency" was
reading the wrong slot, not a different type system). A universal per-function type
dump would need to first classify each command object's base vtable, then read that
class's param-type slot -- a mechanical but sizeable follow-up.

MODDER TAKEAWAY: combined with the arity from section 20, this pins down each call's
signature. Rule of thumb from the codes: coordinates/lights/distances/times = float
(0x7); team/difficulty/enum args = enum (0x2); counts/ids = int (0x1); on/off =
bool (0x5); file/display text = string (0xc); and anything the engine looks up by
name (nodes, waypoints, text keys) = key-string (0xd) -- pass the exact registered
name.

STILL TODO: classify per-class param-type slots to auto-dump all 398 signatures;
find the generic dispatcher entry point; std_vessel.des; text-table format; DMUS_.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
22. NEGATIVE RESULT -- why a full auto-dump of all 398 signatures is NOT reliable
    (analysis session 2 cont.; documents a dead-end so nobody repeats it)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Attempted to auto-extract arity + param types for ALL 398 command objects by
emulating their metadata accessor slots (unicorn) and, separately, by counting
args at script call-sites. BOTH are unreliable on their own; do NOT trust a naive
full dump. Concretely:
  * BINARY SIDE: the metadata slot LAYOUT differs per C++ base class. For the Game_
    family the param-type accessor is vtable slot+12 and reads cleanly (section 21).
    Blindly reading slot+12 on other classes hits a DIFFERENT accessor -> wrong
    types. Proof: naive dump reported Node_CreateNode(int,int); the truth is
    (key,string). So per-function param types need per-CLASS calibration first:
    cluster the 398 by their shared base-vtable shape, identify each class's
    param-type slot from a member whose signature is known, then read within class.
    (Arity via the GetArgCount stub is individually OK -- it returned 2 for
    Node_CreateNode, 3 for Game_SetEnemyMatrixElement -- but see below.)
  * SCRIPT SIDE: counting pushes between GETGLOBAL name and CALL over-counts,
    because arguments are frequently NESTED calls (Node_AddSon(Node_Find("/x"),o)
    pushes Node_Find's own args too). So script-callsite arg counts run high and
    variably (observed deltas vs binary arity: +1 x49, +2 x79, +3/+4...), which
    ALSO means binary-arity vs callsite is not a clean cross-check. A correct
    callsite arity needs a real stack-depth simulation of the Lua bytecode (track
    each GETGLOBAL/PUSH/CALL's net stack effect), not a push count.
WHAT REMAINS TRUE/USABLE: sections 19 (398 names+getter addresses), 20 (metadata/
dispatcher model; arity stub exists & is per-function correct), 21 (the type-code
legend 0x1/0x2/0x5/0x7/0xc/0xd, verified on the Game_ family). The gap is only the
*bulk automated* per-function type table.
PROPER NEXT STEP (sizeable): (a) write a Lua-4 bytecode stack simulator to get true
callsite arities for the ~183 script-used fns (ground truth); (b) cluster the 398
by base-vtable shape; (c) per cluster, pick a member whose arity/types are known
from (a), locate that class's argcount+paramtype slots, apply to all members;
(d) emit aquanox1_engine_api_registry.json v2 with verified arity+param types.

STILL TODO (unchanged): the above per-class calibration; the generic dispatcher
entry; std_vessel.des; text-table format; DMUS_ dynamic music.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
23. THE SCRIPT CLASS & ITS VTABLE -- refines the "command objects" model
    (analysis session 2 cont.; found the registration entry in GOG Aqua.exe)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
CORRECTION/REFINEMENT of sections 19-20: the ~398 script functions are NOT 398
separate command objects -- they are all METHODS OF ONE BIG CLASS (the script
system, ~NOD_Script). Proof: the huge pointer array at 0x5f70c8..0x5f9d9c (2869
slots) is a single C++ VTABLE, and a constructor at 0x4a8aa0 does
  0x4a8aa5: call 0x467120            ; base-class ctor
  0x4a8aaa: mov [esi], 0x5f70c8      ; set this->vftable = the 2869-slot vtable
  ... then allocates the script system's state (malloc 0x4279e0, etc.)
This ctor is referenced from 0x4a8aac / 0x4a8c6d (the script-system init path).
So 2869 slots / ~398 functions ~= 7 vtable entries PER script function: the LUAF_
implementation + its metadata getters (GetName = the B8<str>C3 stubs of section 19,
GetArgCount, GetParamType(i)). The metadata getters and the real LUAF_ impls all
live in THIS one vtable.

CONSEQUENCE for section 22's dead-end: because it's one class with a 2869-entry
vtable, "per-class calibration" collapses to "find, within each ~7-slot record,
which slot is the param-type accessor and which is the LUAF_ impl." The records
appear grouped, so slot roles are at consistent RELATIVE offsets within a record;
the earlier failure was reading a fixed absolute slot (+12) instead of the record-
relative param-type slot. A future pass can: (1) determine the record stride +
the role offsets from a few known functions, (2) walk all records to emit both the
LUAF_ impl address AND the param types for every function.

ENTRY POINTS for next session (GOG exe): script-system ctor/registration @0x4a8aa0
(xrefs 0x4a8aac,0x4a8c6d); the vtable @0x5f70c8; a sample real impl NOD_Body::
ParseLCS @0x467da0; base ctor @0x467120. lua_register/global-install calls should
sit just after the ctor's allocation block (walk forward from 0x4a8ab0).

STILL TODO: from 0x4a8aa0 forward, find the lua_register loop + the dispatcher;
derive the record stride to map every LUAF_ impl + param types; std_vessel.des;
text-table format; DMUS_ dynamic music.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
24. LUA C-API LANDMARKS + how the game API is (and is NOT) registered
    (analysis session 2 cont.; GOG Aqua.exe)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Located the Lua runtime inside the exe and settled the registration question.

  luaL_openlib  = 0x5e7a30.  Identified from lua_mathlibopen @0x5e4890:
      push 0x17 (=23 fns) ; push 0x6001c8 (math table) ; push esi (lua_State)
      call 0x5e7a30
  It has EXACTLY THREE callers -- the three stock libs, nothing else:
      0x5e489d: (0x17=23, 0x6001c8) = math
      0x5e61fc: (0x0b=11, 0x600298) = string
      0x5e746e: (0x21=33, 0x600328) = base
  => PROVEN: the ~398 game functions are NOT registered via luaL_openlib / any
  luaL_reg array. (Settles the open question from sections 14/19.)

  Other landmarks: "too many arguments" error string @0x660bc8, used by 0x5e6ae0
  (a Lua stack/arg-check routine). Sample engine impl NOD_Body::ParseLCS @0x467da0.

  SCRIPT SYSTEM CLASS (section 23) ctor/dtor pair, both installing vtable 0x5f70c8:
      0x4a8aa0 = constructor  (call base-ctor 0x467120; mov [esi],0x5f70c8;
                 malloc 0x4279e0 for state; refcount at 0x668e4c, singleton ptr
                 at 0x668e50)
      0x4a8c60 = destructor   (mov [esi],0x5f70c8; free 0x4279f0; dec [0x668e4c])
  Neither performs the Lua-name registration -- they only build/tear down the
  object. The name/arity/paramtype getters are reached ONLY through the vtable:
  the GetName stub 0x4bc770 has ZERO direct `call` xrefs, and its vtable slot
  0x5f8de8 sits at INDEX 1864 (offset +0x1d20) of vtable 0x5f70c8. So the metadata
  is consumed by indirect `call [reg+offset]` dispatch, confirming the reflection
  model of section 20 from a second angle.

  Most-used indirect vtable offsets across .text (candidate common interface slots):
  +0x34 (idx 13) 27x, +0x04 (idx 1) 22x, +0x0c (idx 3) 15x, +0x24 (idx 9) 15x.
  These are the base-class interface methods (shared by all NOD_ objects), NOT the
  per-function metadata records, which live far out near index ~1864.

PRACTICAL: the registration loop must iterate the metadata records (around index
~1864+) and call lua_pushcclosure/lua_setglobal-equivalents. It is NOT reachable
from the ctor. NEXT ENTRY POINT: find callers of the script-system ctor 0x4a8aa0,
i.e. who instantiates the script system at level load; the registration should run
right after instantiation. Alternatively, xref the lua_State global and look for
the loop that writes ~398 globals.

STILL TODO: find that registration loop (see above); derive the record stride around
index 1864 to map every LUAF_ impl + param types; std_vessel.des; text-table format;
DMUS_ dynamic music.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
25. THE NODE CLASS FACTORY -- how Node_CreateNode("nod_x") builds an object
    (analysis session 2 cont.; GOG Aqua.exe)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Found the mechanism behind every Node_CreateNode(<classname>, <id>) in our scripts.

  CLASS FACTORY = 0x442090   (call it NOD_CreateNodeByName)
    signature: takes the class-name string; internally calls the name->ctor
    lookup 0x43e3d0 (returns NULL -> factory bails out early), then constructs.
    24 call-sites in the exe pass a class name; observed classes:
      nod_directory 8x, nod_game 3x, nod_cockpit 3x, nod_player 2x,
      nod_script 2x, NOD_Player 2x, nod_camera 1x
    Case-insensitive: both "nod_script"(@0x60f97c) and "NOD_Script"(@0x612564)
    exist as strings. Script-side names like "NOD_Directory"/"NOD_Camera"/
    "NOD_Trigger" (section 14) go through exactly this path.
  NAME->CTOR LOOKUP = 0x43e3d0 (the class registry; this is where nod_script maps
    to the script-system ctor 0x4a8aa0 -- which is why 0x4a8aa0 has NO direct call
    xrefs: it is only ever reached indirectly through this registry).
  NEW-WRAPPER for the script system = 0x4a8a70: `push 0x348; call malloc(0x4279e0);
    ... ctor` => sizeof(script system object) = 0x348 bytes.

  Typical engine call sequence (from 0x4581ce, script-system creation):
      push "nod_script" ; call 0x442090        ; create node by class name
      mov ecx, ebp ; call [edx+0x34]           ; parent->AddSon(child)
      call 0x4b6af0 ; ... ; call [ebx+0x2c]    ; virtual init/attach on the new node
  This confirms the earlier statistic: the hot indirect vtable slots +0x34 (27x)
  and +0x2c (6x) are the BASE-CLASS interface methods AddSon / init-attach shared
  by all NOD_ objects (section 24), not per-script-function metadata.

WHY THIS MATTERS: it ties the script API to the C++ object model end-to-end --
Node_CreateNode(name) -> 0x442090 -> registry 0x43e3d0 -> the class's ctor (e.g.
0x4a8aa0 for nod_script, installing vtable 0x5f70c8) -> parent->AddSon via vtable
slot +0x34. The scene graph the .sco builds is literally this factory + AddSon loop.

STILL TODO: dump the class registry table behind 0x43e3d0 (would yield the full list
of instantiable nod_* classes + their ctors -- a definitive "what can a level
contain" list); still open: the ~398-name registration loop; record stride around
vtable index 1864; std_vessel.des; text-table format; DMUS_ music.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
26. THE RTI CLASS REGISTRY -- the real object model (CORRECTS sections 23 & 24)
    (analysis session 2 cont.; GOG Aqua.exe)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
!! CORRECTION FIRST. Sections 23/24 claimed the 398 script fns are methods of ONE
big class whose vtable is 0x5f70c8 (2869 slots), with the script-system ctor at
0x4a8aa0. THAT WAS WRONG. Facts:
  * 0x4a8aa0 is the ctor of NOD_Flare (size 840 = 0x348, base NOD_Body); 0x5f70c8
    is NOD_Flare's vtable. The "push 0x348" I chased was Flare's allocation.
  * The "2869 contiguous code pointers" were simply MANY SMALL VTABLES laid back to
    back in memory -- an artifact of scanning for contiguous code pointers.
  * NOD_Script really is factory 0x48d2c0, size 30396, base NOD_Node.
Section 19's original model (each script fn = its own C++ command object) was RIGHT.

THE ACTUAL MECHANISM -- an RTI (run-time type info) class registry:
  RegisterClass = 0x43e1b0, called 471x at startup as
      RegisterClass(size, factory, "ClassName", "BaseClassName")
  It links records into a list whose head is 0x663684 (record: name@+8, next@+0x1c).
  LookupByName = 0x43e3d0 walks that list with strcmp (0x520710).
  CreateNodeByName = 0x442090 = what Lua's Node_CreateNode(class,id) calls: look up
  the class record, call its factory, get an object. (This is why class ctors have
  no direct call xrefs -- they're only reached via the registry.)

RESULT: 369 registered classes. Full dump in aquanox1_engine_classes.json
{class, base, size_bytes, factory, + for script cmds: message_id, vtable, lua_name}.
  Base-class distribution: NOD_MessagePrototype 308, NOD_Body 25, NOD_Node 15,
  NOD_ShieldedBody 13, RTI_Class 8 (the roots: NOD_Node, NOD_Message,
  NOD_MessagePrototype, COD_Emitter/Light/Physical).

  *** EVERY SCRIPT FUNCTION IS A CLASS ***  308 classes derive from
  NOD_MessagePrototype (each just 16 bytes). e.g. NOD_Node_CreateNode: factory
  0x4bc6b0 -> malloc(0x10), base-ctor 0x4c80a0 with MESSAGE ID 0x138d, sets its own
  vtable 0x5f8ddc. GetName is vtable[3] (+0xc) -- the B8<str>C3 stub of section 19
  (verified: reading vtable[3] for all 308 yields exactly their Lua names).
  MESSAGE IDs are BLOCKED PER SUBSYSTEM: Node_ 0x1388.., Camera_ 0x1b59.., up to
  0x7d06. So a script call is turned into a MESSAGE (id + args) -- which is exactly
  why the whole API mirrors into PM_ (Process Manager, section 15) and can be sent
  over the network: the command object is a message prototype, the PM dispatches/
  replicates the message. That closes the loop on sections 15/19/20.
  Script-cmd classes by subsystem: Body 38, ANT 35, Vessel 35, Game 32, Vehicle 21,
  PhysicalCharacter 18, Trigger 15, Node 13, Camera 12, ShieldedBody 8, FX 8,
  Turret/ParticleEmitter 7, WayPoint/PatrolArea/Light/Emitter 6, Script/NPC 5, ...

  SCENE-GRAPH CLASSES (61) -- the definitive "what a level can contain":
  NOD_Node -> NOD_Body -> {Terrain, Camera, Gun, NPC_Gun, Gun_Part, Halo, Light,
  Flare, Fungus, EscapePod, BuzzerLauncher, ParticleEmitter, FX_Dipol/Light/
  Plankton/Plant/Rays/Seeder/Shockwave/Spawn, ...}; NOD_Body -> NOD_ShieldedBody ->
  {Player(6008B), Bullet, Buzzer, Laserwall, Attachment}; NOD_Node -> {Directory,
  Scenario, Group, Director, Position, Path, PatrolArea, Hud, Cockpit(8616B),
  Script(30396B), FX_Sky/Sound/Stream/Infiltrate}.

STILL TODO: 111 message prototypes whose factory didn't yield an id (different
inlining) -- extract their ids; map message-id -> PM_ handler; std_vessel.des;
text-table format; DMUS_ music.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
27. THE MESSAGE-ID MAP -- all 308 script commands, complete (no gaps)
    (analysis session 2 cont.; GOG Aqua.exe)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Completed the section-26 finding: extracted the message id of EVERY one of the 308
script-command classes. Method that works: in each prototype's factory, the id is
the `push <imm>` IMMEDIATELY BEFORE `call 0x4c80a0` (= NOD_MessagePrototype::ctor(id)),
then `mov [esi], <vtable>`. (My first pass capped imm at 0x9000 and lost 93 of them;
ANT ids go up to 39029 = 0x9865.)

RESULT: 308 unique ids, range 1000..43004, ZERO missing. The scheme is beautifully
regular -- DECIMAL 1000-blocks, one block per subsystem:
   1000 Body(38)        2000 Script(5)      3000 ParticleEmitter(7)
   4000 LaserWall(2)    5000 Node(13)       7000 Camera(12)
   9000 Terrain(2)     11000 Game(32)      12000 Light(6)
  13000 Path(1)        14000 Vehicle(21)   15000 Vessel(35)
  16000 Player(4)      17000 Console(2)    19000 Position(6)
  20000 Trigger(15)    21000 Emitter(6)    22000 Character(2)
  23000 PhysicalCharacter(18)              24000 PatrolArea(6)
  25000 Turret(7)      27000 Mine(3)       32000+39000 ANT(35)
  33000 ShieldedBody(8) 34000 ItemBox(3)   35000 WayPoint(6)
  36000 FX(1)          41000 Director(2)   42000 Stream(5)  43000 Gun(5)
(Gaps at 6000/8000/10000/18000/26000/28000-31000/37000-40000 = reserved or cut
subsystems. ANT uses two blocks: 32000 session ops + 39000 message ops.)

Examples: Node_SetName=5000, Node_CreateNode=5005(0x138d), Node_Find=5007,
Game_SetEnemyMatrixElement (0x2b0?) within 11000-block, Vessel_* 15002..15056,
ANT_Message_CreateObject=39000(0x9858).

WHY IT MATTERS: this is the concrete evidence for the PM_/network story (section
15). A script call is not a direct C call -- it is CONSTRUCTED AS A MESSAGE OBJECT
(id + marshalled args, per the type codes of section 21), handed to the Process
Manager, which executes it locally and can serialize+replicate it to clients
(hence NOD_ANT_Message_* prototypes for the network side, and PM_'s mirror of the
whole API). The id is the wire/dispatch key. Modders: the id is stable per function
and is what a PM_ hook/dispatch table keys on.

All ids merged into aquanox1_engine_classes.json (fields: message_id, message_id_dec,
vtable, lua_name) alongside class/base/size/factory.

STILL TODO: map message-id -> PM_ handler (find PM's dispatch table/switch);
std_vessel.des; text-table format; DMUS_ dynamic music.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
28. FULL SCRIPT-API SIGNATURES -- solved (CORRECTS sections 21 & 22)
    (analysis session 2 cont.; GOG Aqua.exe)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The message-prototype VTABLE LAYOUT is completely regular. Dumped from
NOD_Node_CreateNode (vtable 0x5f8ddc), holds for all 308:
    vt[0] GetRTIClass      vt[1] ctor/dtor helper   vt[2] no-op (ret 4)
    vt[3] GetName()  -> the Lua global name   (the B8<str>C3 stub)
    vt[4] GetArgCount()    -> number of DECLARED params
    vt[5] GetReturnCount() -> number of return values
    vt[6] GetParamType(i)  -> type code of arg i
    vt[7] GetReturnType(i) -> type code of return i
This SOLVES section 22's dead-end: the layout is not per-class-different at all --
my earlier heuristic simply picked the wrong slots. Emulating vt[4..7] for every
prototype yields 305/308 complete signatures -> aquanox1_script_api_signatures.json.

VERIFIED EXAMPLES (arg types -> return):
    Node_CreateNode(string, string) -> node        [id 5005]
    Node_Find(string) -> node                      [id 5007]
    Node_AddSon(node) -> void                      [id 5004]
    Game_SetEnemyMatrixElement(int, int, enum)     [id 11017]
    Game_SetAmbientLight(float, float, float)      [id 11026]
    Game_MissionEnd(bool)                          [id 11036]
    Vessel_Goto(string, float, float)              [id 15006]
    Trigger_SetPresenceSphere(float)               [id 20002]

*** THE TARGET-NODE CONVENTION *** (this explains the section-22 arity confusion)
The DECLARED params do NOT include the target node. Method-style commands are
called from Lua as  fn(targetNode, <declared params>) -- e.g. Node_AddSon declares
1 param (the son) but is called Node_AddSon(parent, son). Factory-style commands
(Node_CreateNode, Node_Find) have no target node, so declared == called. That is
why binary arity vs call-site counts differed by +1 for methods (the rest of the
scatter was my broken push-counter over nested calls).

TYPE CODES (updated; CORRECTS section 21):
   0x1 int   0x2 enum   0x5 bool   0x7 float   0xa/0xb/0xc string
   0xd = NODE HANDLE  <-- section 21 wrongly called this "name/key string"; it is
         the object handle returned by Node_CreateNode/Node_Find and passed as a
         target/arg elsewhere.
   Still UNRESOLVED (documented honestly): 0x9 (38 uses -- Body_SetCS(0x9,0x9),
   ANT_Message_BodyUpdate(float,0x9,0x9,0x9): looks like a vector / coordinate-
   system component), 0x6 (15 uses -- Game_LoadProgress_Enter(0x6), Script_SetTimer
   (0x6,float), WayPoint_SetTakeKey_*(node,0x6): an integer-ish id/count/key),
   0x3 (9 uses -- Body_AddItemInteger(0x3,0x3), ANT infos).
Distribution: float 134, string 115, bool 47, int 44, 0x9 38, node 28, 0x6 15,
0x3 9, enum 3.

MODDING VALUE: every script call's exact signature (arg count, arg types, return
type, message id) is now machine-readable. Combined with the target-node rule this
is enough to author or validate any .sco call without guessing.

STILL TODO: pin 0x9/0x6/0x3 by disassembling a marshaller that consumes them;
map message-id -> PM_ handler; std_vessel.des; text-table format; DMUS_ music.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
29. THE HARDCODED CTF/TEAM MODE (Aqua_Spawn) -- exe evidence for the cut MP content
    (analysis session 2 cont.; GOG Aqua.exe + PAK cross-check)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
While hunting the Lua dispatcher I hit Aqua_Spawn -- and it settles the cut MP-team
question raised by section 13's unused-asset scan (plag_phobocaster_team_blue.col).

  Aqua_Spawn::SpawnEscapePod() @ 0x41b280 (error string "illegal escapepod: %d" @
  0x60729c, logger 0x524340). Just above it, @0x41b1xx..0x41b27c, a routine that
  HARDCODES the team players:
      push "nod_player" ; push "pla_succubus2_team_red" ; call 0x442090  (factory)
      mov edx,[ecx] ; push "osd/mp_pla/pla_succubus2_team_red.osd"
      call [edx + 0x1c]            ; = NOD_Node::ParseIniFile(osd) on the new node
      ... same for ..._team_blue ...
  => the engine, not a script, creates the two team player ships. NOD_Node vtable:
     +0x0c = GetName, +0x1c = ParseIniFile.

  TEAM/CTF vocabulary hardcoded in the exe (all 8 team_ strings):
      /Scenario_Dynamic/Object/team_blue_base , .../team_red_base
      team_blue_respawn_ , team_red_respawn_        (respawn point name prefixes)
      pla_succubus2_team_blue / _red  +  osd/mp_pla/pla_succubus2_team_{blue,red}.osd
  Matches the TEAM_ command enums (13 CTF quick-commands, section 15).

  ASSET SIDE (PAK cross-check): 16 files each for team_blue / team_red:
      vfx/msh/pla_succubus2_team_{blue,red}.msb + pla_phobocaster_team_{blue,red}.msb
      vfx/col/... same four, plus plag_* (gib/debris) variants
      BUT only vfx/osd/mp_pla/pla_succubus2_team_{blue,red}~.osd exist -- there is
      NO phobocaster team .osd. So: the Succubus 2 team ships are complete and
      engine-spawned; the PHOBOCASTER team ships have meshes+collision but no object
      definition => that variant was cut late. (Explains why section 13's unused scan
      flagged plag_phobocaster_team_*.col as orphaned while the succubus ones are
      referenced.)

MODDING OPPORTUNITY (concrete): restoring a phobocaster team ship needs only an
.osd -- clone osd/mp_pla/pla_succubus2_team_blue.osd, point [visual] m0 at
pla_phobocaster_team_blue.msh and [collision] at pla_phobocaster_team_blue.col.
The engine's spawn path is hardcoded to succubus2 names though, so a level/script
would have to create it (Node_CreateNode "nod_player" + Node_ParseIniFile) rather
than relying on Aqua_Spawn.

DISPATCHER STATUS (honest): still not found. There are ZERO `call [reg+0x18]`
(GetParamType) and ZERO `call [reg+0x10]` (GetArgCount) sites, so the Lua glue does
NOT invoke the metadata getters through plain indirect calls at those offsets --
it likely caches the metadata at registration time. The 15 `call [reg+0xc]` sites
are NOD_Node::GetName on scene nodes, not prototype GetName. Next idea: find who
reads the prototype list / builds an id-indexed table, e.g. xref the RTI class
object of NOD_MessagePrototype (0x66b678 is NOD_Node_CreateNode's RTI object,
returned by its vt[0]).

STILL TODO: the dispatcher/registration loop (see above); type codes 0x9/0x6/0x3;
message-id -> PM_ handler; std_vessel.des; text-table format; DMUS_ music.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
30. *** THE LUA REGISTRATION LOOP AND THE GENERIC DISPATCHER -- SOLVED ***
    (analysis session 2 cont.; GOG Aqua.exe)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The last big open question (sections 20/22/24/29) is answered. Two parts.

A) HOW CLASSES REGISTER THEMSELVES (C++ static initializers, run by the CRT):
   Each script-command class has a tiny static-init function. For NOD_Node_CreateNode
   at 0x4bc660:
       push 0x10                      ; sizeof(object)
       push 0x4bc6b0                  ; factory
       push "NOD_Node_CreateNode"     ; class name
       push "NOD_MessagePrototype"    ; base class name
       mov  ecx, 0x66b678             ; this = the class RTI object (zero-filled statically)
       call 0x43e1b0                  ; RegisterClass  (section 26)
       push 0x4bc690 ; call 0x42a750  ; atexit(deregister -> 0x43e320)
   factory 0x4bc6b0 = malloc(0x10); NOD_MessagePrototype::ctor(msgid 0x138d); vtable 0x5f8ddc
   GetRTIClass 0x4bc6e0 -> 0x66b678.
   (This confirms the RegisterClass arg order used in section 26: size, factory,
   name, base -- with the RTI object in ecx.)

B) HOW THE NAMES REACH LUA -- inside NOD_Script::ctor (factory 0x48d2c0 -> ctor
   0x48d320; object size 0x76bc = 30396 bytes):
       call 0x5d3c90                  ; lua_open  -> lua_State (cached at 0x668a40)
       call 0x5e7460 / 0x5e61f0 / 0x5e4890   ; base / string / math lib open
       eax = [0x668a54]               ; N = number of message prototypes
       edx = [0x668a50]               ; PROTOTYPE ARRAY (stride 8; ptr at +4)
     loop i = 0..N-1:
       ecx = [edx + i*8 + 4]          ; prototype i
       eax = [ecx] ; call [eax+0xc]   ; GetName()  -> the Lua global name
       call 0x5d3580                  ; lua_pushnumber(i)      <-- the UPVALUE
       push 1 ; push 0x48ff30 ; call 0x5d3680   ; lua_pushcclosure(dispatcher, 1 upvalue)
       call 0x5d38b0                  ; lua_setglobal(name)
   => EVERY script function is the SAME C closure 0x48ff30, distinguished only by
   its upvalue = the prototype's INDEX in the array at 0x668a50. That is exactly why
   there is no per-function C entry point, no luaL_reg table (section 24), and no
   `call [reg+0x18]` in the wild (section 29): the metadata is read inside the one
   dispatcher, off the prototype it looks up.

C) THE GENERIC DISPATCHER = 0x48ff30 (verified):
       reads its upvalue; errors "[SCRIPTERROR] upvalue is missing" (0x612df4) and
       "[SCRIPTERROR] upvalue must be a number" (0x612dcc) -- the same [SCRIPTERROR]
       prefix we see in log.txt;
       lua_isnumber 0x5d3290 / lua_tonumber 0x5d33e0 -> index
       mov edx,[0x668a50] ; mov ebp,[edx+eax*8+4]   ; fetch prototype
       mov eax,[ebp]                                 ; its vtable
       ... then uses vt[4] GetArgCount / vt[6] GetParamType(i) / vt[5],vt[7] returns
       (section 28) to marshal the Lua stack, builds the MESSAGE (id from section 27)
       and hands it to the Process Manager.

LUA C-API MAP (GOG exe): lua_open 0x5d3c90, lua_pushnumber 0x5d3580 (writes tag 2),
lua_pushcclosure 0x5d3680, lua_setglobal 0x5d38b0, lua_gettop-ish 0x5d3110,
lua_isnumber 0x5d3290, lua_tonumber 0x5d33e0, luaL_openlib 0x5e7a30,
lua_baselibopen 0x5e7460, lua_strlibopen 0x5e61f0, lua_mathlibopen 0x5e4890.
Globals: lua_State ptr 0x668a40, prototype array 0x668a50, prototype count 0x668a54.

WHAT THIS COMPLETES: script call -> closure 0x48ff30 -> prototype[upvalue] ->
metadata (name/arity/param+return types) -> marshalled MESSAGE (id) -> Process
Manager -> local execute + optional network replication -> BHV_ behaviour. Sections
14/15/19/20/21/26/27/28 now form one verified chain.

STILL TODO: type codes 0x9/0x6/0x3 (read them off the dispatcher's marshalling
switch, which starts right after 0x49000a); message-id -> PM_ handler table;
std_vessel.des; text-table format; DMUS_ dynamic music.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
31. THE MARSHALLING SWITCH -- all 14 type codes decoded (CORRECTS sections 21/28)
    (analysis session 2 cont.; GOG Aqua.exe)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Inside dispatcher 0x48ff30, right after it resolves the target node:

  TARGET-NODE RESOLUTION (proves the section-28 convention from the binary):
    call [vtable+0x10]              ; GetArgCount -> n
    edi = -(n+2)                    ; Lua stack index of the FIRST argument
    lua_type 0x5d3330 ; compare with the node tag at [lua_State+8]
    lua_touserdata 0x5d34d0 ; node = [eax+4]
    if NULL -> "[SCRIPTERROR] a %s command has been sended to a NULL node"
    else if not a node -> uses the default/current node at [0x668a48]
    then malloc(0x198) = 408-byte MESSAGE object, vtable 0x5f43e8   (= NOD_Message)

  ARG LOOP + TYPE SWITCH:
    for i in 0..n-1:  call [vtable+0x18]  ; GetParamType(i)
                      cmp eax,0xd ; ja <illegal> ; jmp [eax*4 + 0x4911e8]
  Jump table @0x4911e8 (14 entries). Decoded by the store width + the error text
  each case emits:
    0x0 -> "in argument of 'none' type" (error)
    0x1 -> lua_tonumber + ftol, store BYTE            => int8
    0x2 -> lua_tonumber + ftol, store WORD            => int16
    0x3 -> lua_tonumber + ftol, store WORD            => uint16
    0x4 -> lua_tonumber + ftol, store WORD            => int16 (variant)
    0x5 -> lua_tonumber + ftol, store DWORD           => int32
    0x6 -> lua_tonumber + ftol, store DWORD           => uint32
    0x7 -> lua_tonumber, store DWORD (no ftol)        => float32
    0x8 -> lua_tonumber, store DWORD (no ftol)        => float32 (variant)
    0x9 -> lua_touserdata, tag [lua_State+4]          => *** MAT_Vector3 ***
           error text: "[SCRIPTERROR] %s expects a MAT_Vector3 as argument %d"
    0xa,0xb,0xc -> string cases
           error text: "[SCRIPTERROR] %s expects a string as argument %d"
    0xd -> node handle (userdata, tag [lua_State+8])
  Wrong/missing args produce "[SCRIPTERROR] %s expects a number/string/MAT_Vector3
  as argument %d" and "[SCRIPTERROR] %s expects an argument no. %d" -- exactly the
  [SCRIPTERROR] lines we hunted in log.txt during the sp_1n1 debugging.
  There is a mirrored OUT switch for return values ("out argument of 'none' type"
  @0x490ca9, "illegal out argument type" @0x4910fc) and the trace formatter
  "[SCRIPTERROR] %s - %s function "%s" [%d] - line %d" @0x491316.

CORRECTION to sections 21 & 28: the codes are C STORAGE WIDTHS, not the semantic
labels I guessed. Concretely, my earlier legend said 0x1=int, 0x2=enum, 0x5=bool,
0xc=string, 0xd=key-string. The truth: 0x1=int8, 0x2=int16, 0x5=int32, 0x7=float,
0x9=MAT_Vector3, 0xa/b/c=string, 0xd=node handle. Section 28's 0xd=node was right.
The old "enum"/"bool" readings were coincidences of width (E/F/N fits int16; a bool
is passed as int32). Re-derived signatures now in aquanox1_script_api_signatures.json.

WORKED EXAMPLES (now exact):
    Node_CreateNode(string, string) -> node
    Body_SetCS(MAT_Vector3, MAT_Vector3)          ; position + orientation
    Game_SetEnemyMatrixElement(int8, int8, int16) ; team ids fit a byte; E/F/N a short
    Game_MissionEnd(int32)                        ; success flag as 32-bit
    Game_LoadProgress_Enter(uint32)               ; the object count N
    Body_AddItemInteger(uint16, uint16)
Remember the target-node rule: these are the DECLARED params; method-style commands
are called from Lua as fn(targetNode, <declared params>).

STILL TODO: message-id -> PM_ handler table; NOD_Message (408 B) layout; MAT_Vector3
userdata construction from Lua; std_vessel.des; text-table format; DMUS_ music.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
32. MAT_Vector3 + the LUA HELPER LAYER -- the non-message globals
    (analysis session 2 cont.; GOG Aqua.exe + campaign script cross-check)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Section 31 left one practical question: how does a script produce the MAT_Vector3
(type code 0x9) that Body_SetCS and friends demand? Answer, straight from the
campaign bytecode (1n1.sco, __InitLayout):

    Body_SetCS( node8,
                MAT_Vector3(2058.769, 790.8252, 278.103),   -- position
                MAT_Vector3(83.9634, 0, 0) )                -- orientation
  (bytecode: GETGLOBAL Body_SetCS / GETGLOBAL node8 / GETGLOBAL MAT_Vector3 /
   PUSHNUM x3 / CALL A=2 B=1 / GETGLOBAL MAT_Vector3 / ... / CALL A=3 B=255 /
   CALL A=0 B=0.  Note the last vector call uses B=255 = LUA_MULTRET.)

MAT_Vector3 is NOT one of the 308 message prototypes. It belongs to a separate
HELPER LAYER that NOD_Script registers directly with lua_pushcclosure +
lua_setglobal (registration fn around 0x491d00-0x491f1d):

    Script_AssertFile -> 0x493a30      Script_DoFile -> 0x4917e0
    MAT_Vector3       -> 0x492a10      (constructor)
    gettable          -> 0x492c80      settable -> 0x492f40      gc -> 0x492ba0
    add -> 0x492150   sub -> 0x492370  mul -> 0x491f20  div -> 0x492590
    pow -> 0x492840
  i.e. MAT_Vector3 is a Lua-4 USERDATA TYPE with a full set of tag methods:
  indexing (v.x/v.y/v.z via gettable/settable), garbage collection, and ARITHMETIC
  (vectors can be added/subtracted/scaled/divided/pow'd directly in script).

CONSTRUCTOR 0x492a10 = NOD_Script::LUA_Vector3_Create() (name string @0x612f88;
"out of memory" @0x612fac): mallocs 0xC bytes (= 3 floats), then either
  * COPY-CONSTRUCTS: if arg1 is already a vector userdata (lua_type == tag at
    [lua_State+4]) it copies x,y,z, or
  * reads three numbers off the stack.
Tags: [lua_State+4] = MAT_Vector3 tag, [lua_State+8] = node tag (per section 31).

SO THE FULL SCRIPT-VISIBLE GLOBAL SET IS:
  (a) 308 engine commands  = message prototypes, dispatched by closure 0x48ff30
      (sections 26/27/30/31)
  (b) this small C helper layer (MAT_Vector3 + its operators, Script_DoFile,
      Script_AssertFile)
  (c) stock Lua libs: base(33) / string(11) / math(23) via luaL_openlib (section 24)
  (d) pure-Lua convenience functions loaded from script at startup -- e.g. BindEasy
      and Vessel_Attack have NO C getter and are not in (a)/(b)/(c) (section 20).

PRACTICAL FOR OUR MODS: to place an object you write
    Body_SetCS(node, MAT_Vector3(x,y,z), MAT_Vector3(heading,pitch,roll))
and you may compute positions with vector arithmetic, e.g. MAT_Vector3(a) + ...,
because add/sub/mul/div are registered tag methods. Our build_sp_1n1.py already
emits this pattern; it is now confirmed as the intended API.

STILL TODO: NOD_Message (408 B) layout + message-id -> PM_ handler table; find where
the pure-Lua helper file (BindEasy etc.) is loaded from (Script_DoFile call sites);
std_vessel.des; text-table format; DMUS_ music.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
33. THE LUA SCRIPT LIBRARY (dat/script/lib/*.sco) -- where BindEasy actually lives
    (analysis session 2 cont.; exe string -> PAK -> bytecode)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Section 32 left open where the pure-Lua helpers come from. The exe holds exactly one
script path string: "dat\script\lib\glue.sco" @0x6125a8. It is the entry point of a
full SCRIPT LIBRARY shipped inside the PAKs -- 14 modules under dat/script/lib/:

  glue.sco            21905 B, 86 protos, 72 globals   <- the core glue
  misc.sco            26594 B  (LM_MilkRunGoto/Patrol, LM_ComplexGotoAttack,
                                LM_ProtectedGoto/Patrol, LM_FollowProtect, LM_Infiltrate)
  formation.sco       22240 B  (LF_Formation/Leader/Follower/GotoInFormation)
  vesselattack.sco    17419 B  (LVA_ClearArea/GuardArea/GotoAttack/GuardAreaRespawn)
  dock.sco            14911 B  (LD_DockMaster/Docking, LV_Docking)
  sensor.sco          13455 B  (SS_AvpStack, SS_ShotHit, SS_ShieldGuard, SS_EnemyGuard,
                                SS_Threatening)
  vesselgoto.sco      12229 B  (LVG_SimpleGoose/ComplexGoose/MultiPatrol/ComplexGoto)
  vehiclegoto.sco     11157 B  (LCG_*)      vehicleattack.sco 6152 B (LCA_ClearArea/GuardArea)
  dipol.sco            5290 B  (LD_Jump)    oldone.sco 4452 B (OO_Goto/Attack/Sink)
  synchronization.sco  3920 B  (LSYNC_Semaphore/Barrier/Switch)
  turret.sco           2132 B  (LT_Attack)  camera.sco 2129 B (LC_Move/LC_General)

LOADING: glue.sco defines LibList + LoadLib; LoadLib's constants are
  "LibList", "_Basis", "Script_DoFile", "dat\script\Lib\", ".sco"
=> LoadLib(name) simply Script_DoFile("dat\script\Lib\"..name..".sco"). So the C
helper Script_DoFile (0x4917e0, section 32) bootstraps the whole Lua layer.

glue.sco's 72 globals (the real "scripting API" a level author touches):
  Debug/logging: G_Debug, Script_Debug
  World bootstrap: Boot (asserts TerrainName/FogName/PlayerOsdName are strings, then
    Node_Find("/"), Node_CreateNode("nod_terrain","terrain"), Node_AddSon, ...),
    LoadTerrain, LoadTextures
  Player: SetPlayerToPosition, SetPlayerPosition(Vector), SetPlayerCS, GetPlayerNode
  Nodes/enemy matrix: GetGameNode, GetCameraNode, SetEnemyMatrixElement,
    GetEnemyMatrixElement, SendRadioMessageTake
  THREADS/STATE MACHINES: __ThreadTable, BindThread, UnBindThread, BindEasy,
    BindPlayer, BindBlock, TriggerThread, GotoState, __GetStateFunction, __BeginPlay
  Timers: C_ScriptTimer, SetTimer, StopTimer, __Timer, CallFunction, DelayedFunction,
    DelayedFunctionStop, InvokeFunction
  Sensor behaviours: StartShieldGuard/StopShieldGuard, StartShotHit/StopShotHit,
    StartEnemy/StopEnemy, StartEnemyPatrol/StopEnemyPatrol, InsertSensorBehaviour,
    RemoveSensorBehaviour, CheckSensorBehaviour, G_SensorCallbackTable,
    StopAllActiveBehaviours(_AndCallSetEngineValue)
  Modification stack: IsNotLastModification, ApplyModification, ForceModification,
    RestoreModification, StoreInnerValue, GetInnerValue
  Tables/utility: CloneTable, CopyTableInto, Clone, MacroFuse
  TAGS + tag methods: __Tag_Nil, __Tag_Num, __Tag_MAT_Vector3, __Tag_NOD_NodeRef,
    settagmethod(_hook), tostring(_hook)
  Libs: LibList, LoadLib

BindEasy (glue.sco proto 15) -- now readable:
    if tag(node) ~= __Tag_NOD_NodeRef then Script_Error("BindThread() - Scenario is
       invalid") return end
    if type(state) ~= "table" then Script_Error("BindEasy() - State is invalid, the
       nodename is '"..name.."'") ... 
  i.e. it TYPE-CHECKS the node handle against __Tag_NOD_NodeRef (the userdata tag of
  section 31/32!) and the state against a table, then builds the thread table.
  => The "PM_BindThread() node is NULL" we hit in sp_1n1 was the C-side failure that
  happens when Node_Find returned nil BEFORE these checks could help.

CONSEQUENCE: everything a mission script calls that ISN'T one of the 308 message
prototypes lives here, in readable, moddable Lua bytecode inside the PAKs. Wave/
patrol/dock/formation AI used by campaigns is LIBRARY code (misc/vesselattack/
vesselgoto/formation), not engine code -- so it can be changed without touching the exe.

STILL TODO: decompile glue.sco's state-machine helpers (GotoState/__BeginPlay) into
readable pseudo-Lua; NOD_Message (408 B) layout + message-id -> PM_ handler table;
std_vessel.des; text-table format; DMUS_ music.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
34. THE THREAD / STATE-MACHINE MODEL (glue.sco) -- how mission logic really runs
    (analysis session 2 cont.; decompiled glue.sco protos)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Decompiled the core of glue.sco. This is the framework every mission + our own wave
scripts run on. A "Thread" is a plain Lua TABLE (not an engine object) with fields:
    TypeId = 666            -- the magic marker that identifies a Thread table
    Node                    -- the NOD_NodeRef this thread is bound to
    State                   -- the current state table { Node=, Thread=, CommandTable, ... }
    __TimerList             -- table of pending timers (id -> {CallBack=, ...})
    __ThreadTable[node]     -- global registry mapping node -> thread
    ShieldGuardActive / ShotHitActive  -- sensor-behaviour flags (0/1)

BindThread(nodePath, nodeName, stateTable)  (glue proto 13):
    assert tag(scenario)==__Tag_NOD_NodeRef  else "BindThread() - Scenario is invalid"
    assert type(nodeName)=="string"          else "BindThread() - NodeName '..' is invalid"
    assert type(stateTable)=="table"         else "BindThread() - Thread is invalid, ..."
    node = Node_Find(scenario, nodeName)     -- <-- if this returns nil, the C side
                                                later fails with PM_BindThread()NULL
    stateTable.State=nil ; stateTable.TypeId=... ; register in __ThreadTable ; __BeginPlay
BindEasy (proto 15) is the thin wrapper we use: same checks, builds the table, calls
BindThread. => our sp_1n1 "PM_BindThread() node is NULL" was Node_Find returning nil
INSIDE BindThread (wrong directory path), before any Lua assert could catch it.

__BeginPlay(thread) (proto 82): sets ShieldGuardActive=0, ShotHitActive=0; then if
thread.CommandTable is a table -> GotoState(thread, thread.CommandTable); elseif
thread.BeginPlay is a function -> thread:BeginPlay(). So a bound state table either
has a CommandTable (data-driven state list) or a BeginPlay function (code-driven).

GotoState(threadOrState, newState) (proto 68): normalises arg (if TypeId==666 use
.Thread), assert both are tables, wires newState.Node/Thread, swaps thread.State.
This is the STATE TRANSITION primitive -- how a vessel moves attack->patrol->flee etc.

__GetStateFunction (proto 84): resolves the function to run this tick: if
G_SensorCallbackTable[thread] is set (a sensor fired) use it, else
thread.State.CommandTable[...] ; returns (func, state). This is how sensor callbacks
PRE-EMPT the normal state function.

C_ScriptTimer(thread, timerId) (proto 23): pulls thread.Thread.__TimerList[timerId]
(errors "[SCRIPTERROR] [ScriptTimer] ... the threads timer list is nil"), clears it,
logs "[TIME] Function <CallBack> for timer <id> will be executed", then CallFunction.
This is the per-frame driver we bind on "/script" (section 6b-1): the engine ticks
C_ScriptTimer, which fires due timers, which run state functions, which issue engine
commands (the 308 message prototypes).

FULL MISSION-RUNTIME PICTURE (all Lua-side, in the PAKs):
  level .sco builds nodes -> BindEasy(node, name, stateTable) registers a Thread ->
  __BeginPlay enters the first State -> each tick, C_ScriptTimer + sensor callbacks
  pick a state function -> it calls library AI (LVA_*/LVG_*/LF_* from section 33) and
  engine commands -> GotoState transitions -> Triggers/WayPoints fire more transitions.
NONE of this is in the exe; it is all editable bytecode. Retuning mission logic =
editing these library .sco files.

MODDING NOTE for our wave scripts: a "wave" is just a sequence of Threads whose state
functions spawn the next group; the timer list + GotoState give the sequencing. Our
build_narrow_waves / build_sp_1n1 already produce Thread tables with State/CommandTable;
this section explains exactly what fields the engine+glue expect (TypeId=666, Node,
State, __TimerList).

STILL TODO: NOD_Message (408 B) layout + message-id -> PM_ handler; std_vessel.des;
text-table format; DMUS_ music; (optional) a readable pseudo-Lua export of glue.sco.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
35. THE TIMER CHAIN (glue.sco) -- SetTimer / DelayedFunction / CallFunction
    (analysis session 2 cont.; decompiled glue.sco protos 24, 26, 27)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Completes section 34. Exact semantics, straight from the bytecode:

SetTimer(threadOrState, delaySeconds)   [proto 24]
    if threadOrState.TypeId ~= 666 then threadOrState = threadOrState.Thread end
    assert type(thread)=="table"   else Script_Error("SetTimer() - Thread is invalid")
    assert type(delay)=="number"   else Script_Error("SetTimer() - Delay is not a number")
    id = Script_SetTimer(thread.__UniqueID, delay)      -- ENGINE call (Script_ block)
    return id
  => the timer is registered against the thread's __UniqueID. A table WITHOUT
  __UniqueID (i.e. one never passed through BindThread/BindEasy) cannot get a timer.

DelayedFunction(seconds, threadOrBlock, callbackName, param)   [proto 27]
    (internally named "CallFunctionWithDelay")
    assert type(threadOrBlock)=="table" else Script_Error("CallFunctionWithDelay () -
        Thread or Block is invalid")
    assert type(seconds)=="number"      else Script_Error("... Seconds must be a number")
    rec = { ThreadOrBlock=threadOrBlock, CallBack=callbackName, Param=param }
    id  = Script_SetTimer( G_ScriptTimerThread.__UniqueID , seconds )   -- !!
    G_ScriptTimerThread.__TimerList[id] = rec                            -- !!
    return id
  *** KEY FACT *** DelayedFunction does NOT put the timer on the thread you pass in.
  It always registers on the GLOBAL timer thread G_ScriptTimerThread and stores the
  record in ITS __TimerList. The passed table is only carried inside the record as
  ThreadOrBlock (and is merely type-checked, not required to be a bound thread).
  Therefore DelayedFunction REQUIRES G_ScriptTimerThread to already exist, i.e. it
  must run AFTER  G_ScriptTimerThread = BindEasy(Node_Find("/"), "/script",
  C_ScriptTimer)  in __StartUp (section 6b-1). Called before that, G_ScriptTimerThread
  is nil -> GETDOTTED on nil -> the script thread dies (or errors) and nothing fires.

CallFunction(threadOrBlock, callbackName, param)   [proto 26]
    assert type(x)=="table" else "CallFunction () - Thread or Block is invalid"
    if type(x.NumOfThreads)=="number" then      -- it's a BLOCK (multi-thread group)
        ... resolves each member ...
    else
        state = x.State
        if type(state)~="table" then
            if type(x)~="table" then Script_Error(" CallFunction() - No valid block")
        end
        f = state.CommandTable[callbackName] (or x[callbackName])
        if type(f)~="function" then Script_Error(" CallFunction() - "..name.." is not
            a valid function")
        f(...)
  => the callback name is looked up in the STATE's CommandTable (or the block), not
  in an arbitrary table. C_ScriptTimer (proto 23) pops the record from
  G_ScriptTimerThread.__TimerList and hands it to CallFunction.

FULL TIMER FLOW:
  DelayedFunction(sec, X, "Name", p)
      -> Script_SetTimer(G_ScriptTimerThread.__UniqueID, sec) = engine timer id
      -> G_ScriptTimerThread.__TimerList[id] = {ThreadOrBlock=X, CallBack="Name", Param=p}
  ...engine fires the timer on the "/script" node...
      -> C_ScriptTimer(thread, id): reads __TimerList[id], clears it, logs
         "[TIME] Function <CallBack> for timer <id> will be executed"
      -> CallFunction(X, "Name", p) -> resolves X.State.CommandTable["Name"] -> calls it

*** TOUCHPOINT (noted, not worked on here) *** This bears directly on the separate
"intro radio message at level start" investigation (see aquanox_funk_intro_problem
handoff). Two facts fall out of the bytecode above:
  (a) A FREE table (Iteration 16's S_INTRO) is type-checked OK by DelayedFunction, but
      CallFunction later resolves the callback via X.State.CommandTable -- a free
      table has no .State, so it takes the "No valid block" path and the callback is
      never called. A bound thread (Iteration 17) has .State and works. This predicts
      Iteration 17 succeeds where 16 failed.
  (b) Any DelayedFunction issued BEFORE G_ScriptTimerThread is bound (i.e. early in
      __StartUp, before the BindEasy on "/script") indexes a nil global and dies.
      That matches Iterations 9/12/13 (patching __StartUp) showing no effect.
  Also note C_ScriptTimer logs "[TIME] Function ... will be executed" via Script_Debug
  -- so running with -logfile -logunknown AND G_Debug enabled should show whether a
  timer ever fires, which is the "prove where a callback actually runs" step that
  investigation asked for.

STILL TODO: NOD_Message (408 B) layout + message-id -> PM_ handler; std_vessel.des;
text-table format; DMUS_ music; readable pseudo-Lua export of glue.sco.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
36. MESSAGE DELIVERY + the built-in SCRIPT SNOOP TRACE (corrects sec. 25)
    (analysis session 2 cont.; GOG Aqua.exe)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Tail of the dispatcher (whose real name the exe gives us):
    NOD_Script::LUAF_Interface()        (string @0x612cd4)
    error "no target node" @0x612d80 if the resolved target is still NULL.

MESSAGE DELIVERY (the final step of a script call):
    msg  = malloc(0x198)                 ; 408-byte NOD_Message, vtable 0x5f43e8
    msg[+8]  = prototype->[+8]           ; via tiny getter 0x5efc40 (mov eax,[ecx+8])
    msg[+0xc]= 0 ; msg[+0x10]=0xFD ; msg[+0x11]=1 ; msg[+0x12]=0 ; msg[+0x14]=0
    ... marshalled args appended (allocator 0x4543c0 hands out arg slots) ...
    node = target ; vt = *node
    call [vt + 0x2c] (msg)               ; <<< NOD_Node::ReceiveMessage(msg)
  So a script call literally becomes "send this message object to that node", and the
  node's virtual message handler at vtable slot +0x2c executes it (routing further
  into PM_ / BHV_). Return value in eax.

  *** CORRECTION to section 25 *** There I guessed the hot indirect slot +0x2c was a
  virtual "init/attach". It is NOT: +0x2c is the MESSAGE HANDLER. (In the Aqua_Spawn
