example; check whether cft_vulcano.sco is an older build worth diffing.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
61. THE ITEM-PICKUP SYSTEM -- and can it be used in single player? (YES, with caveats)
    (analysis session 3; nod_itembox OSDs + campaign scripts + exe)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
QUESTION: multiplayer maps are full of floating pickups (dm_narrow has 126, ctf_vulcano
130). Can that system be used in a single-player / instant-fight level?

HOW A PICKUP IS BUILT (from dm_narrow, verified):
    b = Node_CreateNode("nod_itembox", "ibox_gun_plasma2_1")
    Node_AddSon(itemDir, b)                       -- MP maps use a "Powerups"/"Ammo" dir
    Body_SetCS(b, MAT_Vector3(2328.27,1797.55,304.31), MAT_Vector3(0,0,0))
    Node_ParseIniFile(b, "osd/ibox/ibox_gun_plasma2.osd")
    ItemBox_SetRespawnDelay(b, -1)                -- -1 = no respawn (single use)
That's it. Four calls. The whole behaviour lives in the OSD.

THE ITEMBOX OSD (fully data-driven, 29 ship with the game):
    [sed] class = nod_itembox      [visual] billboard texture + mesh mp_box_energie.msh
    [collision] collisionextension          [emitter] visible
    [itembox]
        respawndelay = 25.0        respawncount = <n>
        [inventory] numofitem = 1 ; [item0] { name = "ammo_emp", count = 5 }
        [respawnsound] name = "ibox_spawn01.wav", vol, minr/maxr
        [tumble] amplitude/phase/frequency a,b,c   -- the lazy floating rotation
    The 29 boxes: guns (empactor, lasergatlin, plasma2, plasmagatlin, sizzler,
    torpedolauncher, vendetta1/2/gatlin), ammo (emp, energy, shell, sizzler),
    torpedoes (bigbang, hammerhead, leech, stanley), power-ups (absorb, amplify,
    armor5/25/100, boost, cloak, maxdamage, repair25, repair50) and the CTF flags.

SCRIPT API: ItemBox_SetRespawnDelay(float) 34000, SetRespawnCount(uint32) 34001,
PlayRespawnSound() 34002, SetItemCount() 34003.

*** THE SP EVIDENCE -- why this should work ***
  1. nod_itembox is an ordinary registered scene class (RTI, section 26). Node_CreateNode
     builds it anywhere; nothing in the creation path is MP-specific.
  2. The ITEM NAMES are not MP-only. Campaign mission 3h1 (and 2h3, 3h2, 3h3, 3h4, 3n1,
     2n3 ...) literally calls  Body_AddItem(ship, "gun_plasma2", 1)  and "gun_flak" --
     the exact same identifiers the item boxes carry. The single-player inventory system
     already speaks this vocabulary.
  3. Item delivery goes through Body_AddItem / Body_AddItemInteger. On a plain NOD_Body
     both are WARNING STUBS ("WARNING: body '%s' received a Body_AddItem(...)") --
     a derived class (NOD_Player) implements them. So the PLAYER can receive items; that
     is exactly what a pickup needs, and it is the same path the campaign already uses.
  4. The exe hardcodes the ibox OSD paths (0x606f60..0x60704c: osd/ibox/ibox_pickup_*),
     i.e. the engine preloads them itself -- not gated behind a script.

CAVEATS (honest, not verified in-game):
  * Only MP maps ship item boxes, so the SP path is untested by the developers.
  * The power-ups (cloak, boost, amplify, absorb, maxdamage) may hook MP rules code;
    the safe ones are the plain inventory boxes (ammo_*, gun_*, torpedo_*) and
    repair25/repair50, armor*, which map onto normal inventory items.
  * flag_blue/flag_red obviously need CTF rules -- ignore those.
  * Respawn is engine-side; ItemBox_SetRespawnDelay(-1) makes it single-use, which is
    what a SP level wants.

CONCRETE TEST (cheap, low risk) -- this supersedes the old roadmap idea
"eigener Item-Pickup (Trigger + Body_AddItem)": we don't need to build one. Add to our
sp_1n1 arena, right after the player is created:
    ib = Node_CreateNode("nod_itembox", "ibox_repair50_1")
    Node_AddSon(objectDir, ib)                  -- reuse the Object directory
    Body_SetCS(ib, MAT_Vector3(<near spawn>), MAT_Vector3(0,0,0))
    Node_ParseIniFile(ib, "osd/ibox/ibox_pickup_repair50.osd")
    ItemBox_SetRespawnDelay(ib, -1)
    Node_EnterSimulation(ib)
    Game_LoadProgress_Advance(root)             -- don't forget the progress count!
Fly into it. If the repair item lands in the inventory, the entire MP pickup catalogue
is available to single-player levels -- including ammo and weapon boxes for our wave mod.
Verify with Script_SnoopOn() (section 36): a successful pickup should show an [S2E]/[T2E]
line for the item transfer.

STILL TODO: disassemble the itembox collision/pickup handler to see whether it checks a
"multiplayer" flag; check whether the HUD prints a pickup message in SP; test the
power-up boxes separately from the plain inventory ones.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
62. HOW THE ORIGINAL GAME PLAYS ITS INTRO RADIO LINE -- the definitive answer
    (analysis session 3; traced 1n1's take 29 to its source)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Question: how do you fire a radio take right after level start? Rather than theorise, I
traced the ORIGINAL 1n1 intro line (take 29, the drunk architecture student) to the
exact code that plays it.

THE ANSWER: it is NOT called from __StartUp. It is Code6 of a bound object's state
machine, inside the level's OPENING CUTSCENE.

    S_o1739 = { Start, Code0, Code5, Code6, Code9..Code15, CheckCondition,
                ProcesseStateChange }
    Start(state):  state.CodeIndex = 0 ; CallFunction(state.Thread, "Code0")
    Code0(state):  state.CodeIndex = 0
                   Director_BeginCutscene(state.Node)          -- enter cutscene mode
                   Game_PlayMusic(GetGameNode(), "agressive")
                   Camera_Cut(state.Node, 1)
                   Camera_MoveTo(state.Node, "/InGameSequences/Navigation/Position_1",
                                 <time>, "MMMN", 0)
    Code5(state):  state.CodeIndex = 5
                   if CommandTable.CheckCondition(state) == 1 then
                       CameraLib.Wait(state, 4, "Code6")        -- wait 4 s, then Code6
                   else CallFunction(o1739, ...) end
    Code6(state):  state.CodeIndex = 6
                   SendRadioMessageTake(o1692.Node, o1692.Node, 29)   -- *** the line ***
                   Camera_MoveTo(state.Node, ".../Position_2", 6, "MMMN", 0)
                   Camera_LookAt(state.Node, "/Scenario_Dynamic/Object/terror")
So: a state machine is bound at __StartUp; its Start immediately runs Code0, which opens
a cutscene and starts a camera move; a CameraLib.Wait chains to Code6 four seconds later,
and Code6 speaks the line while the camera pans to the target. The radio take is
delivered from a LIVE, BOUND THREAD callback -- never from bare __StartUp.

THE OTHER RADIO PATH IN THE SAME LEVEL. 1n1's other takes (30, 31, 1263, 1129) are fired
from the ChangeTo1 method of CONDITION NODES of the goal graph (section 58):
    o1697.ChangeTo1 = SendRadioMessageTake(o1692.Node, o1692.Node, 30)
i.e. "when this condition first becomes true, say line 30". o1692 is the player object;
sender == receiver == the player's node.

WHY __StartUp DIRECT CALLS FAIL (now explained, not guessed). At __StartUp the script
thread is still inside the loading bracket (Game_LoadProgress_Enter .. _Leave, section
6b-1); the sim/HUD/radio queue is not in its normal state, and the timer thread
(G_ScriptTimerThread) may not even be bound yet (section 35). Every original level
therefore speaks its first line from a thread callback that runs AFTER the bracket
closes -- either a cutscene Code step or a condition-node ChangeTo1.

*** THE RECIPE (three working options, in order of simplicity) ***
  1) BOUND-THREAD CODE STEP (what the game does; most robust):
       S_intro = { Start = function(s) s.CodeIndex=0; CallFunction(s.Thread,"Code0") end,
                   Code0 = function(s) SendRadioMessageTake(player.Node, player.Node, KEY) end }
     ... in __StartUp, AFTER G_ScriptTimerThread is bound:
       o_intro = BindEasy(Node_Find("/Scenario_Dynamic/Object"), "player1", S_intro)
     Binding runs __BeginPlay -> Start -> Code0 -> the line plays as soon as the thread
     ticks. Add CameraLib.Wait(s, 2, "Code1") if you want a beat first.
  2) WAYPOINT AT THE SPAWN: WayPoint_SetTakeKey_Enter(wp, KEY) with the waypoint radius
     over the player's start position. The engine fires it the instant the player is in
     the sphere -- fully engine-timed, no script timing at all (section 56).
  3) PRESENCE TRIGGER at the spawn whose EnterPresence does SendRadioMessageTake.
     Same idea, needs the trigger bound (section 56).
  In ALL of them, sender/receiver are node handles (usually the player's own node).

WHAT NOT TO DO: SendRadioMessageTake directly in __StartUp, or DelayedFunction before
G_ScriptTimerThread is bound (section 35) -- both fail silently. Verify any attempt with
Script_SnoopOn() (section 36): if no [S2E] line appears for Game_SendRadioMessageTake,
the call never ran; if it appears but nothing is heard, it is a take/mtake data problem.

STILL TODO: decompile CameraLib.Wait (the cutscene beat helper) -- it is the cleanest way
to sequence "line, pause, line" and is used by every campaign intro.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
63. ADDENDA -- small findings from session 3 not yet written down
    (analysis session 3; gap-check against the chat)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Loose ends surfaced during the mission-scripting deep-dives that belong in the record:

A) Body_AddItemInteger IS ALSO A STUB on a plain NOD_Body. Handler 0x46795e -> impl
   0x467c40, which only logs "WARNING: body '%s' received a Body_AddItemInteger('%d',
   '%d')" and returns. Like Body_AddItem (1034, section 52), the REAL implementation is
   on the derived NOD_Player. So both item-grant verbs no-op on non-player bodies; only
   the player actually receives items -- which is exactly why campaign gifts
   (Body_AddItem(ship,"gun_plasma2",1) in 3h1 etc.) target the player node. Confirms the
   section-61 pickup analysis from the delivery side.

B) THE ITEMBOX OSD FIELDS in full (section 61 gave a summary; here is the schema):
   [itembox] respawndelay(float) respawncount(uint16) ; [inventory] numofitem + [itemK]
   { name, count } ; [respawnsound] { name(.wav), freq, freqrnd, vol, volrnd, cnt, minr,
   maxr } ; [tumble] { amplitudea/b/c, phaseshifta/b/c, frequencya/b/c } -- the lazy
   floating rotation of a pickup. [emitter] soundemissionlevel, [collision]
   collisionextension. So a pickup's float animation, respawn sound and contents are all
   pure OSD data; ItemBox_SetRespawnDelay(-1) just overrides respawndelay at runtime.

C) THE TWO VULCANO SCRIPTS (section 60). map/mp_07_ctf/script/ has BOTH ctf_vulcano.sco
   (81,588 B, 70 protos -- the finished level) AND cft_vulcano.sco (29,339 B, 10 protos,
   a typo'd "cft"). The cft_ one is an EARLIER/partial build (only 58 item boxes vs 130,
   no team meshes) -- ignore it; the restore entry must point at ctf_vulcano (correct
   spelling). Worth a diff only for curiosity.

D) CameraLib (dat/script/lib/camera.sco) -- the CUTSCENE TIMING helper used by every
   campaign intro (section 62). It defines behaviours LC_Move (Camera_MovePosReached /
   StopBehavior) and LC_General (Timer / StopBehavior) and, under LibList.Camera_Basis,
   CameraMove, CameraMoveWithLook, Wait, WaitStop. CameraLib.Wait(state, seconds,
   "CodeN") schedules a jump to CodeN after N seconds via the timer thread -- this is the
   clean "pause between two lines / two camera moves" primitive. It is how an intro
   sequences Code0 (open cutscene + first camera move) -> Wait -> Code6 (speak the line +
   next camera move). For scripting your own intro cutscene, this is the beat helper to
   reuse rather than DelayedFunction.

E) DELIVERABLE aquanox1_cut_commands.json was never cross-referenced. It holds the (now
   closed) cut-command question: 2 Body ids (1017/1034) that once looked cut but are
   really RequestKinInfo / AddItem (superseded by section 49 -- there are NO cut script
   commands). Keep it only as the audit trail of that investigation; the authoritative
   name<->id source is aquanox1_message_ids.json.

None of these change earlier conclusions; they fill in detail the chat had but the
handoff lacked.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
64. CONFIRMED IN-GAME: the intro-radio playtests (external chat, iterations 18-24)
    (cross-referenced with our bytecode analysis; verified consistent)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
A parallel modding chat ran actual PLAYTESTS of the intro-radio problem (the wave-mod
sp_narrow line, custom take 9200/9201). Their empirical results INDEPENDENTLY confirm --
and in two places sharpen -- our bytecode analysis (sections 35/56/62). Merged here
because in-game confirmation outranks static reasoning.

CONFIRMED WORKING (user-verified "it worked exactly as planned"):
  * Iteration 14: custom MP3 + custom MTake played from the first-toiler KILL callback.
    => custom audio + a custom mtake key are fine (the data side is solved).
  * Iteration 23: DelayedFunction(3, S_INTRO, "PlayIntro") set FROM the kill callback,
    then S_INTRO.PlayIntro -> SendRadioMessageTake(node, node, 9200) three seconds later.
    Custom voice line played. => a radio take fires fine from a TIMER callback, AND a
    FREE table (S_INTRO with a direct PlayIntro function) works via CallFunction path B
    (our section 35 finding, now proven in game). The only requirement is that the timer
    was scheduled from a LIVE gameplay callback, not from __StartUp.

THE KEY NEGATIVE RESULT (this is the sharpening -- it explains WHY the originals avoid
__StartUp, section 62):
  * Iteration 21: even after correctly binding G_ScriptTimerThread AND initialising
    G_ScriptTimerThread.__TimerList = {} in __StartUp, a DelayedFunction scheduled there
    NEVER FIRED (no callback, no radio). No script error -- the timer simply does not
    tick in the __StartUp/loading context.
  => So it is NOT merely "bind the timer thread first" (our section 35 said that is
     necessary; iteration 21 shows it is NOT SUFFICIENT). The loading-bracket context
     itself is inert for timers. This is exactly why every original level speaks its
     first line from a cutscene Code step or a condition/sensor callback that runs AFTER
     the sim is live (section 62), never from a __StartUp timer.

THE EXACT __StartUp FAILURE PROGRESSION (useful concrete errors, from their log.txt):
  * "attempt to call a number value" in __StartUp = a hand-assembled DelayedFunction CALL
    with the wrong base (CALL 1 0 instead of CALL 0 0) -- our A/B-encoding pitfall
    (section 6b-1), now seen from the other side.
  * After fixing the base: "attempt to index field `__TimerList' (a nil value)" =
    DelayedFunction needs G_ScriptTimerThread.__TimerList to exist. (Confirms glue's
    DelayedFunction reads G_ScriptTimerThread.__TimerList, section 35.)
  * After initialising __TimerList: no error, but the timer never fires (iteration 21
    above). This is the wall that sends you to a trigger/callback approach.

DIAGNOSTIC CAVEAT (new, practical): Script_SnoopOn() enabled GLOBALLY at level start is
TOO HEAVY -- one run logged 2859 [S2E] + 719 [E2S] lines, pushed load time from ~1 s to
40-50 s, and the trace load itself crashed the game on the first kill. So use
Script_SnoopOn sparingly (wrap a single suspect call, or prefer a few Script_Log markers)
-- do NOT ship it on in a normal test PAK. (Our section 36 recommended it broadly; this
adds the load-cost warning.) Also confirmed via their snoop log: __StartUp IS reached,
Game_LoadMTake runs, and Game_LoadProgress_Leave is reached -- so the failure is purely
timing/context, not that the script never ran.

WRAPPER SHAPE CONFIRMED: their glue read matches ours -- SendRadioMessageTake(sender,
receiver, key) = Game_SendRadioMessageTake(GetGameNode(), sender, receiver, key). With
our target-node rule (section 28), Game_SendRadioMessageTake's declared params
(node, node, int32) + the implicit target node = exactly those 4 Lua args. The two
analyses agree.

NET RECOMMENDATION (unchanged from section 62, now backed by playtests): fire the intro
line from a LIVE callback -- iteration 24's spawn presence-trigger (untested at their
time of writing) or, most robust, the bound-thread Code step the originals use. A timer
is fine too, but only if scheduled from an already-live callback (iteration 23), never
from __StartUp (iteration 21).

STILL TODO: confirm iteration 24 (spawn presence trigger) works in game; if it does, it
is the simplest custom intro path (no cutscene machinery). Then update
aquanox_funk_intro_analyse_beitrag.txt to this final state.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
65. PRACTICAL LESSONS FROM THE PARALLEL CHAT (Teil A/C) -- verified & merged
    (cross-referenced; the non-radio findings from the iteration-24 transfer)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Beyond the intro-radio playtests (section 64), the parallel chat's transfer file held
several practical modding lessons worth keeping. Each verified against the PAKs/registry.

A) POLICE / SECURITY AMBIENT SHIPS (verified: all 4 exist).
   atl_pscout, ent_pscout, tz_pscout, nv_pscout -- police/security scouts with red/blue
   halos, one per faction (HUD labels ATL-Polizei, ENT-EntroPol, TZ-Security, NA-Polizei
   -- pilot_d, section 43). Good for ambient traffic. osd/atl/atl_pscout.osd ->
   vfx/msh/atl_pscout.msb.

B) OSD DATA-TYPE DISCIPLINE (verified, and it bit them).
   nv_pscout crashed with "DesFile Error data 'BulletType' ... wrong type". Cause: a
   child gun's bullettype field must be a STRING (e.g. "bul_vendettagatlin_p" /
   "bul_plasma1_p"); an empty or wrong-typed value crashes on load. LESSON: when editing
   an .osd, keep every field's data type EXACT (section 16 values are raw LE bytes -- a
   string field must stay a string). Test OSD edits in isolation. (This is the same
   "DES FILE ERROR" path as NOD_Body::ParseLCS, section 20.)

C) AMBIENT TRAFFIC: prefer DIRECT calls over AI templates (confirmed stable in iter 5).
   For neutral/ambient vessels (police, transports circling), the ROBUST way is a direct
   per-ship call, NOT a BindEasy + library-behaviour transplant:
       Vessel_CollisionTerrain(node, 1)
       Vessel_Patrol(node, "/Scenario_Dynamic/Navigation/AttackArea", -1, 45)
   Iteration 5 put 10 visible police boats into 1n1 this way with natural movement and no
   crashes, whereas complex MultiPatrol/BindEasy transplants (iter 1/3) were unstable.
   RULE: library behaviours (section 57) are for scripted combatants; for background
   traffic, DirectPatrol is simpler and safer. (Complements section 57 -- two tiers of AI
   use.)

D) DYNAMIC LIGHTS / HEADLIGHTS are feasible (API verified). NOD_Light / NOD_FX_Light are
   real scene classes, and the Light_ block exists in the registry:
       Light_Switch 12000, Light_SetCone 12001, Light_SetR1 12002, Light_SetR2 12003,
       Light_SetMode 12004, Light_SetColor 12005.
   So an omni or CONE (spotlight/headlight) light is scriptable: create a NOD_Light,
   set colour/cone/ranges, attach to a ship. Untested in game but the primitives are all
   present. Suggested path: lift a light block from a projectile OSD that already has one,
   drop it into a ship OSD with a small range, optionally add a halo. (Extends the Light_
   entries in sections 14/50.)

E) TEXTURE FORMAT DISCIPLINE. Ship textures are DDS with DXT3/DXT5, alpha + mipmaps
   (e.g. pla_phobocaster_hi_0.dds = 512x512 DXT3 with alpha + mipmaps). LESSON from a
   failed upscale test: don't auto-downscale a replacement back to the original size --
   test real 1024/2048 variants, and PRESERVE alpha + mipmaps and the DXT format. A
   replacement that silently reverts to 512 is not an upscale.

F) VOICE / TTS PIPELINE (for custom radio takes). The parallel chat used ElevenLabs with
   a "German sci-fi radio operator, calm, slightly tense, short military delivery, female,
   slightly synthetic" prompt. Custom MP3 + a custom mtake key played fine in game
   (iteration 14/23). IMPORTANT ASSET RULE (kept): do NOT extract or redistribute the
   original "Sally" voice datasets; MTake mapping, file paths and your own TTS pipeline
   are fine. (Ties to the take/voice system, section 42.)

G) WORKFLOW RULES (their hard-won process, worth adopting):
   confirmed-stable > latest-generated; build on the last CONFIRMED base, not the newest
   file. Number every iteration and record goal/base/changed-files/expected/test/result.
   Don't blind-patch theoretical start paths -- prove with a log marker/callback FIRST,
   then add audio. Prefer a few Script_Log markers over global Script_SnoopOn.

None of these contradict our findings; they add verified assets, an OSD-safety rule, the
DirectPatrol-vs-library distinction, the light/headlight feasibility, and the texture/
voice/workflow practices.

STILL TODO: test a NOD_Light headlight on a ship in game; try DirectPatrol police as
ambient traffic in a custom arena.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
66. AQUA.EXE MASTER REFERENCE -- consolidated engine map (all verified addresses)
    (analysis session 3; single-stop summary of sections 14-62, GOG DRM-free exe)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
One place with every verified address and the complete script<->engine model. The
binary is the GOG DRM-free 1783412860968_Aqua.exe, ImageBase 0x400000, .text 0x401000
(+0x1f1cc4), .rdata 0x5f3000, .data 0x602000. All 34 anchor addresses below were
re-disassembled and confirmed this session (34/34 clean).

=== THE ONE-SENTENCE MODEL ===
A Lua script call -> the single C closure 0x48ff30 (upvalue = the function's index in
the prototype array 0x668a50) -> reads the prototype's metadata (name/arity/param+
return types via its vtable) -> builds a 408-byte NOD_Message (id at msg+8) -> sends it
to the target node via node.vtable[+0x2c] = ReceiveMessage -> a per-class switch on
(id - blockbase) runs it, forwarding unknown ids up the class chain -> PM_ can also
serialise+replicate the message for MP.

=== LUA RUNTIME (C API) ===
  lua_open        0x5d3c90     lua_pushnumber   0x5d3580 (writes tag 2)
  lua_type        0x5d3330     lua_isnumber     0x5d3290
  lua_tonumber    0x5d33e0     lua_touserdata   0x5d34d0
  lua_pushcclosure0x5d3680     lua_setglobal    0x5d38b0
  luaL_openlib    0x5e7a30     (ONLY 3 callers: base/string/math -> the game API is
                               NOT registered via luaL_reg)
  lua_baselibopen 0x5e7460     lua_strlibopen   0x5e61f0    lua_mathlibopen 0x5e4890
  Globals: lua_State* @0x668a40 ; prototype array @0x668a50 ; prototype count @0x668a54.

=== THE SCRIPT SYSTEM (NOD_Script) ===
  factory 0x48d2c0 (size 0x76bc=30396) -> ctor 0x48d320. The ctor calls lua_open, opens
  the 3 stock libs, then LOOPS over the prototype array registering each function:
      GetName (vtable[3]) -> lua_pushnumber(index) [the UPVALUE] ->
      lua_pushcclosure(0x48ff30, 1) -> lua_setglobal(name).
  So every one of the ~398 script functions is the SAME closure 0x48ff30, keyed by its
  upvalue index. This is why there is no per-function C entry point.

  GENERIC DISPATCHER 0x48ff30 = NOD_Script::LUAF_Interface:
    reads upvalue (errors "[SCRIPTERROR] upvalue is missing/must be a number");
    prototype = [0x668a50 + index*8 + 4]; vtable = *prototype;
    resolves the TARGET NODE (first Lua arg): lua_type/lua_touserdata; NULL -> error
      "a %s command has been sended to a NULL node"; non-node -> default node @0x668a48;
    malloc(0x198) = 408-byte NOD_Message (vtable 0x5f43e8): msg[+8]=id (from prototype
      [+8]), msg[+0x10]=0xFD, msg[+0x11]=1;
    arg loop: for i in 0..GetArgCount-1: GetParamType(i) via vtable+0x18; type switch
      (jump table 0x4911e8); marshal onto the message via slot allocator 0x4543c0;
    optional [S2E]/[T2E] trace (flag 0x668a5c); deliver: node.vtable[+0x2c](msg).

=== THE RTI CLASS REGISTRY ===
  RegisterClass 0x43e1b0 (called 471x): RegisterClass(size, factory, name, base), ecx=
    the class RTI object; links a node into the list whose head is 0x663684 (name@+8,
    next@+0x1c). LookupByName 0x43e3d0 (strcmp-walks the list). CreateNodeByName 0x442090
    = what Lua Node_CreateNode(class,id) calls. => class ctors have no direct xrefs
    (reached only via the registry).
  369 registered classes; 398 are message-prototype command classes (name<->id in
  aquanox1_message_ids.json, 398/398). 61 scene-graph classes = what a level can contain.
  NOD_MessagePrototype::ctor(id) = 0x4c80a0 (the `push <id>` before it, in each factory,
  IS the message id -- read ids there, not from a derived id map).

=== THE MESSAGE-PROTOTYPE VTABLE (per command, uniform) ===
  vt[0] GetRTIClass    vt[1] ctor/dtor helper   vt[2] no-op
  vt[3] GetName -> the Lua global name (B8 <str> C3 stub)
  vt[4] GetArgCount    vt[5] GetReturnCount     vt[6] GetParamType(i)  vt[7] GetReturnType(i)
  Message ids are decimal 1000-blocks per subsystem (Body 1000, Script 2000, Node 5000,
  Camera 7000, Game 11000, Light 12000, Vehicle 14000, Vessel 15000, Player 16000,
  Trigger 20000, PhysicalCharacter 23000, Turret 25000, ANT 32000/39000, ShieldedBody
  33000, ItemBox 34000, WayPoint 35000, Gun 43000, ...). Full list: aquanox1_message_ids.json.

=== MARSHALLING TYPE CODES (jump table 0x4911e8) ===
  0x1 int8, 0x2 int16, 0x3 uint16, 0x5 int32, 0x6 uint32, 0x7/0x8 float32,
  0x9 MAT_Vector3 (userdata, tag [lua_State+4]), 0xa/0xb/0xc string,
  0xd node handle (userdata, tag [lua_State+8]). Target node is the implicit first Lua
  arg (method commands are called fn(targetNode, declaredParams...)).

=== MESSAGE DELIVERY & INHERITANCE ===
  node.vtable[+0x2c] = ReceiveMessage. NOD_Body 0x4675f0 (2-level switch: index table
  0x4679d4 -> jump table 0x467994, 16 targets), default 0x467980 -> NOD_Node 0x4414e0
  (jump table 0x441888, block 5000), default 0x4417f8 -> 0x537160 (ANT block). "default"
  = FORWARD to the base class (NOD_Vessel->NOD_ShieldedBody->NOD_Body->NOD_Node). 149
  handler addresses verified (aquanox1_message_handlers.json); the AI subsystems use
  chains of ~7-id sub-switches (e.g. NOD_Vessel arm @0x54b970 = -15006).

=== SHIELDS (per NOD_ShieldedBody) ===
  Body vtable[+0x70] = GetShieldedBody. Per 6 faces: current int[] @shield+0x24+face*4,
  max int[] @shield+0x4c+face*4. GetShieldValue 0x467b10 = current/max (NORMALISED 0..1);
  SetShieldValue 0x467b20 = current = round(fraction*max). So Body_SetKineticShield takes
  a FRACTION 0..1, face -1=all. Three banks (kin/emp/bio) laid consecutively.
  Body_InformShotHit / *ShieldBarrierReached fall through to default = engine->script
  callbacks. Body_AddItem 0x467bd0 and Body_AddItemInteger 0x467c40 are WARNING STUBS on
  NOD_Body; only NOD_Player implements them (why item grants target the player).

=== THE LUA HELPER LAYER + LIBRARY ===
  Registered directly (not message prototypes): MAT_Vector3 ctor 0x492a10
  (LUA_Vector3_Create, mallocs 12 bytes) + operators add/sub/mul/div/pow + gettable/
  settable/gc; Script_DoFile 0x4917e0, Script_AssertFile 0x493a30; Script_SnoopOn 0x4915e0
  / Script_SnoopOff 0x4915f0 (toggle trace flag 0x668a5c -> "[S2E]/[T2E] <node>: <cmd>"
  in log.txt with -logfile -logunknown; do NOT ship it on -- it is very heavy, sec.64).
  The pure-Lua library is dat/script/lib/*.sco (glue + 13 modules), loaded via
  LibList/LoadLib -> Script_DoFile. glue.sco defines BindEasy/BindThread/GotoState/
  timers/sensor behaviours + the Thread model (TypeId=666, Node, State, __TimerList).

=== OTHER ENGINE LANDMARKS ===
  NOD_Game::LoadMusic 0x45a040 (slot<5) / PlayMusic 0x458ea0 ("atmo"=ambient slot).
  NOD_Body::ParseLCS 0x467da0 (DES field parse, "DES FILE ERROR"). Aqua_Spawn::
  SpawnEscapePod 0x41b280 (nearby: hardcoded CTF team spawn of pla_succubus2_team_*).
  NOD_Node vtable: +0x0c GetName, +0x1c ParseIniFile, +0x2c ReceiveMessage, +0x34 AddSon.

This section is the quick-lookup index; sections 14-62 hold the full derivations, and
the JSON catalogs hold the machine-readable data.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
67. METHODOLOGY, TOOLS & DEAD ENDS -- how the Aqua.exe analysis was actually done
    (rescue documentation -- read this before re-doing any binary work)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
This section preserves the HOW, so a future session (or a fresh sandbox) can reproduce
and extend the analysis without rediscovering the process. The /home/claude sandbox is
WIPED between sessions, so all tooling must be rebuilt from the descriptions here + the
build_*.py sources referenced throughout.

--- THE ENVIRONMENT ---
  * Target binary: /mnt/user-data/uploads/1783412860968_Aqua.exe -- the GOG DRM-FREE
    build. THIS is the one to analyse. The Steam build (Aqua.exe, 2,850,816 B) is DRM-
    wrapped and unanalysable -- do NOT use it. ImageBase 0x400000; sections: .text
    0x401000 (size 0x1f1b44), .rdata 0x5f3000 (0xeb9e), .data 0x602000 (0x9305c),
    .rsrc 0x696000. 32-bit x86, MSVC-compiled (~2001).
  * Python 3 in the sandbox. Analysis packages (install with pip --break-system-packages):
        pefile     -- PE parsing, section mapping, get_memory_mapped_image()
        capstone 5 -- x86 disassembly (Cs(CS_ARCH_X86, CS_MODE_32))
        unicorn 2  -- CPU emulation (used to evaluate the metadata accessor stubs)
    Network is restricted but pypi.org is allowed, so pip works for these.
  * Our own tools (rebuild from build_*.py + the format sections): pak_tool.py (PAK
    read), pak_pack.py (PAK write), unpak.py, descook.py (cooked-DES parser), des_text.py
    (plaintext DES), lua4dis.py (Lua-4 bytecode loader/disassembler), build_catalog.py /
    build_unused.py (asset catalogs). The build_sp_*.py / build_narrow_waves.py are the
    mod builders and double as worked examples of the bytecode-graft technique.

--- THE CORE WORKING LOOP (what worked, repeatedly) ---
  1. Map with pefile: get_memory_mapped_image() gives a flat image indexed by RVA;
     rva(va)=va-ImageBase. Keep .text bounds handy for "is this a code pointer?" tests.
  2. Disassemble with capstone from a known anchor; annotate operands that look like
     string pointers (read a C-string at that address if it is printable) -- this alone
     reveals most function purposes, because the engine is full of error strings and
     "[SCRIPTERROR]"/"WARNING:" messages that name what the code does.
  3. Find things by STRINGS first. re.finditer over the whole image for a name
     ("nod_script", "firework", "EXP_", "team_blue", ".sam") then find 4-byte pointers to
     that string in .text/.data (struct.pack('<I', va)) to get xrefs. This "string ->
     xref -> disassemble" chain found the class factory, the music system, the death
     system, the cut CTF content, and more.
  4. For call targets: E8 rel32 -> dst = site+5+rel32. Scan .text for callers of a known
     function, or callees of a site. This built the caller/callee graph by hand.
  5. For switch dispatch: find `jmp [reg*4 + imm32]` (FF 24 <modrm> imm32), then decode
     the 60-90 bytes before it for `mov reg,[obj+8]` (the message id load), `add/sub reg,
     blockbase`, `cmp reg, count`, an optional `movzx cl,[reg+idxtbl]` (two-level switch),
     and read the jump table -- BOUNDED by "entries that are valid .text addresses".
  6. For metadata stubs (GetArgCount/GetParamType): emulate them with unicorn. Map the
     whole image read+exec, set up a tiny stack, put the arg at [esp+4], emu_start from
     the stub to a fake return address, read eax. This turned the reflection metadata into
     concrete arities and type codes.
  7. VERIFY EVERY DERIVED TABLE against ground truth: the 398-name<->id registry
     (aquanox1_message_ids.json) was the anchor -- any address map or "cut command" claim
     was checked against it, and against a few hand-decompiled campaign scripts.

--- WHAT DID NOT WORK (do not repeat these) ---
  A. Steam Aqua.exe -- DRM, dead end. Only the GOG exe.
  B. "Each API function has its own Execute vtable slot" (sections 20/23/24 drafts) --
     WRONG. There is ONE generic dispatcher closure (0x48ff30); functions are
     distinguished only by an upvalue index. Chasing per-function Execute addresses via
     vtable walking wasted several iterations.
  C. "The 398 functions are methods of one giant class with vtable 0x5f70c8" -- WRONG;
     0x5f70c8 is NOD_Flare's vtable and the "2869 contiguous slots" were many small
     vtables laid back-to-back. (The right model: each function is its own 16-byte
     NOD_MessagePrototype-derived class, section 26.)
  D. Counting RegisterClass push-args to enumerate classes -- undercounted (308 instead
     of 398) because many args come via mov/registers, not push. This "silently
     incomplete master list" then turned every gap into a false "cut command" discovery
     (13, then 24, then 2 -- ALL artifacts). FIX: read the message id at each prototype's
     FACTORY as the `push <imm>` before call 0x4c80a0, and take the name from that
     factory's vtable+0xc getter -> 398/398, no gaps. LESSON (cost us 3 rounds): never
     derive "unregistered => cut" from a map you have not proven complete.
  E. "Handler(id) = *(K + id*4) if it lands in ANY .text" (base-folded jump tables) --
     far too weak; a folded table "resolves" for almost any index, producing garbage
     addresses (Vessel_Goto -> a string-copy loop, etc.). FIX: require (a) an [obj+8] id
     load before the switch AND (b) contiguous single-subsystem ids AND (c) handler arms
     adjacent to the jmp. That gave 149 trustworthy addresses; the rest need per-class
     hand work. LESSON: positional/folded lookups validate for any index -- always cross-
     check contiguity + code-adjacency.
  F. Naive parameter-type extraction by reading a fixed vtable slot (+12) across all
     classes -- the accessor slot differs per base class; only worked once the prototype
     vtable layout (vt3..vt7) was pinned uniformly (section 28).
  G. "No two-level MSVC switches exist" -- WRONG; NOD_Body's handler is exactly that
     (index table 0x4679d4 -> 16 targets). Reading it as one-level walked off the table
     into NOPs (0x90909090) and produced fake handlers.
  The pattern across C-G: a fast heuristic that "resolves" is not the same as a correct
  one. Every bulk table in this engine will produce plausible-looking garbage if you do
  not validate against the 398-registry and against code-adjacency. Three separate
  self-corrections (documented in sections 39/40/53) came from exactly this.

--- HOW THE SCRIPT SIDE WAS READ ---
  lua4dis.py loads AN's Lua-4.0 .sco bytecode. Key gotchas that cost time:
    * The CLOSURE operand is in the A field ((i>>POS_A)&0x1FF), NOT argU(i) -- getting
      this wrong mis-maps every proto (fixed mid-analysis, section 33 delta).
    * CALL is `A B`: A = stack slot of the function, B = number of results (255 =
      LUA_MULTRET). Our own early mods swapped A/B and crashed ("attempt to call a number
      value").
    * State tables are recovered by scanning main for CREATETABLE ... PUSHSTRING key /
      CLOSURE proto ... SETMAP / SETGLOBAL name.
  Ground-truth cross-checks used campaign scripts (1n1, 1h1) and the libraries
  (glue.sco, sensor.sco, vesselattack.sco, camera.sco) decompiled by hand.

--- REPRODUCIBILITY NOTE ---
  Every address in section 66 was re-disassembled and confirmed this session (34/34
  clean; 6 dispatcher sub-claims verified in-bytecode). If a future session gets
  different addresses, check it is the SAME GOG exe (1783412860968_Aqua.exe) -- a
  different build will shift everything.

68. IF THIS IS THE ONLY DOCUMENT YOU HAVE -- the rescue summary & orientation
    (read this FIRST in a new session; it points at everything else)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
This is a multi-session reverse-engineering + modding project on AquaNox 1 (2001,
Massive Development, the KRASS engine, Lua 4.0 bytecode). If the originating chat is
gone, THIS FILE plus the JSON catalogs and the build_*.py sources are the whole memory.
Here is the orientation.

=== WHAT THE PROJECT IS ===
The user (Stefan) is modding AquaNox 1. Working method: an AI (Claude) analyses the game
in a Python sandbox, produces modified aquanox6.pak builds and documentation, and the
user playtests. The sandbox is WIPED between sessions -- so tools get rebuilt from this
handoff each time, and findings accumulate here. Communication is GERMAN; the handoff is
mostly English by habit but that is fine.

=== THE UPLOADED FILES (re-request these if missing) ===
  * AN1 PAKs aquanox0..6.pak (10,348 unique files after override; aquanox6 is the mod
    target). * 1783412860968_Aqua.exe = GOG DRM-FREE exe (analyse THIS). * Aqua.exe (Steam,
    DRM -- ignore). * AN2 files (Aquanox2.zip: AN2.dat + data0..4.pak) for cross-checks.
  If a session lacks these, ask the user to re-upload; nothing else substitutes.

=== THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT INSIGHT ===
AquaNox 1 is almost ENTIRELY DATA-DRIVEN. You can build missions, restore cut content,
retune combat, add pickups and change AI WITHOUT touching the exe -- by editing .des/.osd
data and the Lua library/level .sco scripts inside the PAKs. The exe analysis (sections
14-66) exists to UNDERSTAND that data layer, not because you must patch the exe. In ~4900
lines of findings, the number of things that actually require an exe change is: zero.

=== THE VERTICAL SLICE (how a script line becomes a game action) ===
  .des/.osd data  ->  level .sco (Lua-4 bytecode)  ->  a script call
    -> generic dispatcher 0x48ff30 (upvalue = prototype index)
    -> NOD_Message (id at +8) sent to the target node
    -> node ReceiveMessage switch on (id - blockbase), forwarding up the class chain
    -> the engine acts (move/shoot/shield/spawn/camera/radio)
    -> engine->script CALLBACKS (Body_ObjectDestroyed, Trigger_EnterPresence, ...) drive
       the mission's state machines and boolean goal graph -> Game_MissionEnd.
Full detail: section 66 (exe) + sections 56-62 (scripting).

=== HOW TO SCRIPT A MISSION (the payoff -- sections 56-62 + the template file) ===
  * A "Thread" is a Lua table {TypeId=666, Node, State, __TimerList}, bound to a node via
    BindEasy (glue.sco). Its State is a table whose KEYS ARE ENGINE CALLBACK NAMES.
  * Give a vessel behaviour: StoreInnerValue(thread, PREFIX_*, ...) then GotoState(thread,
    LVA_GuardArea) -- one of 27 ready library behaviours (section 57).
  * Triggers: Node_CreateNode("NOD_Trigger", ...) + Body_SetPosition + Trigger_
    SetPresenceSphere(r) + BindEasy under /Scenario_Dynamic/Trigger; the presence callback
    (SS_AvpStack) sets a flag / advances the mission (section 56).
  * Objectives: Game_SetTaskTextKey for the text; win/lose via a boolean goal graph of
    script nodes whose root SetStateValue = Game_MissionEnd(1); leaves tripped by
    SetEngineValue(thread, flag, 1) from death/sensor callbacks (section 58).
  * Radio lines: fire from a LIVE callback -- a bound cutscene Code step (what originals
    do), a WayPoint_SetTakeKey_Enter over the spawn, or a presence trigger. NEVER from a
    bare __StartUp call (sections 62/64 -- proven in game). Takes = mtake keys tying
    text+voice+speaker (section 42).
  * The commented skeleton is in aquanox_mission_vorlage.md.

=== THE DELIVERABLES (all in /mnt/user-data/outputs; regenerate JSONs via build_*.py) ===
  * aquanox_modding_handoff_v<N>.txt -- THIS FILE (versioned; bump N per section-56-header
    rule). Sections 1-13 formats/tools, 14-66 the engine, 56-62 mission scripting,
    63-65 addenda + merged external chat, 67 methodology, 68 this rescue map.
  * aquanox_handoff_delta.txt -- append-only change log (56 deltas); read it to see WHY
    each number changed and which claims were corrected. Unversioned by design.
  * aquanox_mission_vorlage.md -- commented mission template.
  * aquanox_analyse_uebersicht.md -- what is analysed vs open.
  * JSON catalogs: aquanox1_assets.json (10,348 files), aquanox1_levels.json (43 levels),
    aquanox1_unused_assets.json (1,188 unreferenced / 643 cut-content candidates),
    aquanox1_message_ids.json (AUTHORITATIVE 398/398 name<->id), aquanox1_message_
    handlers.json (149 verified handler addresses), aquanox1_script_api_signatures.json
    (305 signatures), aquanox1_engine_classes.json (369 RTI classes), aquanox1_mission_
    scripts.json (per-level survey), aquanox1_cut_commands.json (audit trail -- the
    cut-command question is CLOSED, there are none).
  * build_*.py -- PAK/DES/OSD/Lua tools + mod builders (rebuild the toolchain from these).

=== WHERE TRUST LIVES (so you don't re-break things) ===
  EXACT / authoritative: the 398 name<->id registry; the format specs (PAK/DES/OSD/SAM/
  MSB); the dispatch MECHANISM (sections 66/37/52); the shield struct (section 51); the
  mission-scripting model (56-62); everything hand-decompiled from campaign scripts.
  PARTIAL / verified-subset: handler->address map (149/398 solid; the rest need per-class
  hand work, NOT a heuristic -- see section 67 dead ends E/G).
  DO NOT resurrect: the 391-handler map, the 308-prototype count, any "cut command" list.
  These were self-corrected; the deltas explain each.

=== THE ROADMAP (section 6, and in Claude's memory) ===
  DONE: (1) music in Narrow, (2) wave mode in Narrow, (3) 1n1-as-arena (sp_1n1).
  NEXT: (4) restore cut station "Habitat daGama" -- data only, one station_d.des row
  pointing at map/1n1/script/sta_dagama (sections 44-46). (5) restore MP map mp_07
  "Vulcano" -- the map is COMPLETE in the PAKs, just missing from maplist~.des; add one
  [map8] entry (section 60). (5b) TEST MP item pickups in single player -- drop an
  ibox_pickup_repair50 into sp_1n1, verify with Script_SnoopOn (section 61). (6) more cut
  content (EnTrOx meshes, phobocaster team ship needs only a .osd, EXP_26-30 explosions).

=== HARD-WON PRACTICAL RULES (do these, they cost real iterations to learn) ===
  * LoadProgress bracket: Game_LoadProgress_Enter(N) must match the number of
    Game_LoadProgress_Advance calls, and Game_LoadProgress_Leave must run last, or the
    load screen hangs (section 6b-1).
  * Node paths differ per level; a wrong path in Node_Find -> "PM_BindThread() node is
    NULL". * Bind G_ScriptTimerThread on "/script" BEFORE any DelayedFunction, and even
    then a timer scheduled in __StartUp will not fire (section 64) -- schedule from a live
    callback. * OSD fields are raw little-endian bytes -- keep each field's TYPE exact (a
    string field must stay a string, or the level crashes on load, section 65). * Textures
    are DXT3/5 with alpha+mipmaps; preserve them. * Script_SnoopOn is invaluable for "did
    my call run?" but is TOO HEAVY to leave on globally (40-50s load, can crash) -- wrap a
    single call or use Script_Log markers. * For ambient traffic use direct Vessel_Patrol,
    not library-behaviour transplants (section 65). * confirmed-stable > latest-generated;
    number every iteration; prove with a log marker before adding audio.

=== IF YOU CONTINUE THE ANALYSIS ===
  Highest-value open exe work: per-class hand-harvest of the remaining ~250 handler
  addresses (Vessel/Vehicle/Turret sub-switches, section 55 method); the NOD_Message
  +0x10/+0x11 flag meanings; the collision/damage loop that subtracts shield points. But
  none of this blocks any mod -- the data/scripting layer is fully usable now. For a
  25-year-old game whose scripting was never publicly documented, sections 56-68 already
  constitute a complete, verified mission-authoring guide. Protect this file.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
69. ADDITIONAL EXE FINDINGS -- debug/cheat access, Script_Log, CLI switches, stock Lua
    (rescue pass 2: details found while re-checking coverage)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
More things worth preserving, all verified this session.

A) THE C SCRIPT-HELPER LAYER is bigger than section 32 listed. Beyond MAT_Vector3 /
   Script_DoFile / Script_AssertFile / Script_SnoopOn / SnoopOff, the same directly-
   registered (non-message-prototype) layer includes:
       Script_Log   -> C fn 0x491260   (prints a line to log.txt -- the light-weight
                       alternative to Script_SnoopOn; use THIS for "did my code run?"
                       markers, it costs nothing near what SnoopOn does, section 64)
       Script_Error -> C fn 0x4937a0   (raises a "[SCRIPTERROR]" line; glue's asserts
                       call it, e.g. BindEasy/GotoState/CallFunction validation)
   Both read the lua_State at 0x668a40 like the other helpers. NOTE: Script_Log/
   Script_Error are NOT in the 398 message registry and NOT defined in glue.sco -- they
   are engine C globals. (G_Debug and Script_Debug are Lua-level, defined in the library.)
   Distinct from these are the FIVE real Script_ MESSAGE commands (block 2000):
   Script_SetScript 2000, Script_BindThread 2001, Script_SetTimer 2002,
   Script_UnBindThread 2003, Script_StopTimer 2004 -- the engine primitives glue's
   BindThread/SetTimer wrap.

B) *** BUILT-IN CHEATS + DEBUG CONSOLE (very useful for mod testing) ***
   The exe contains a cheat/debug layer with these confirmed messages:
       CHEAT: INDESTRUCTABLE ON / OFF     (god mode)
       CHEAT: INVISIBLE ON / OFF          (enemies ignore you)
       CHEAT: MISSION COMPLETE AUTO       (win the current mission)
       CHEAT: MISSION COMPLETE MANUAL
       CHEAT: MISSION FAILED
       CHEAT: TARGET TERMINATED           (kill current target)
   And menu/console toggle actions (bindable UI events):
       menu_toggleconsole    -- opens an in-game console
       menu_togglegamestate  -- shows game-state overlay
       menu_togglestatistics -- perf/stats overlay
       menu_toggleactionmenu
   These are exactly what you want when testing a mod: MISSION COMPLETE to skip,
   INDESTRUCTABLE to survive while watching your scripted events, the console/statistics
   overlays to inspect. HOW they are triggered (key binding vs a config flag vs a build
   switch) is not yet traced -- worth finding: check ini/control_menu.des and the
   performance~.des / control bindings, and whether -debugger enables them. This is a
   HIGH-VALUE follow-up: an official debug path beats external tools.

C) COMMAND-LINE SWITCHES (complete list, 42 found; the useful ones):
       -logfile / -nologfile    write log.txt
       -logunknown              log the "unknown"/[SCRIPTERROR]/NULL-node lines (REQUIRED
                                to see our script errors, section 6b-1)
       -logmem / -logmemstatistic  memory logging
       -mp_log                  multiplayer logging
       -window                  windowed mode (handy for alt-tabbing during tests)
       -noterrain               skip terrain (fast load for script-only tests!)
       -nocursorclip            free the mouse cursor
       -nomessagebox            suppress error dialogs
       -debugger                (attaches/enables a debugger path -- see B)
       -host / -connect         multiplayer host/join
   Recommended test invocation: Aqua.exe -logfile -logunknown -window  (add -noterrain
   if you only care about scripted events, not the world mesh).

D) STOCK LUA 4.0 STANDARD LIBRARY (the 3 luaL_openlib calls, section 24) -- so you know
   what non-engine Lua you can use in scripts:
     math (23): abs sin cos tan asin acos atan atan2 ceil floor mod frexp ldexp sqrt min
       max log log10 exp deg rad random randomseed
     string (11) and base (33) libs also loaded (type, tostring, tonumber, setmetatable-
       era tag methods, etc. -- Lua 4.0 semantics, NOT 5.x).
   Practical: random/randomseed are available for varied spawns; math.min/max/floor for
   positioning; string ops for names. Remember it is Lua 4.0 (no `local function`
   sugar the same way, tag methods instead of metatables, `%` not present as operator --
   use math.mod).

E) MISC STRINGS worth knowing: screenshots go to "<path>\aquashot" (menu_grabscreen /
   the grabscreen action); NOD_Light::ParseIniFile validates light "type" and errors
   'no type given' / 'parsed wrong type' -- confirms the headlight/light OSD path
   (section 65 D) and that a light block needs a valid type field; the shop UI has
   per-category sounds (menu_shop_item_gun/tor/rep/spi/buz/gen) matching the item classes.

STILL TODO (raised here): trace HOW cheats/console are enabled (control_menu.des bindings
/ -debugger / a config flag) -- an official in-game debug path would be the single most
useful testing aid for this whole project.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
70. THREAD / STATE / SENSOR INTERNALS -- exact decompiled semantics (CRITICAL)
    (rescue pass 3: the precise glue.sco + sensor.sco contracts; sharpens 34/35/56)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Sections 34/35/56 described these systems; this section gives the EXACT decompiled
contract, because getting one field wrong makes a trigger fail SILENTLY (no error, no
log line). This is the single most error-prone area of AN1 scripting.

=== THE BIND CHAIN (glue.sco) ===
  BindEasy(scenario, nodeName, stateTable) -> thread            [proto 15]
      thread = {}                                  -- a NEW table, NOT your stateTable
      assert tag(scenario)==__Tag_NOD_NodeRef      else Script_Error "BindThread() -
                                                   Scenario is invalid"
      assert type(stateTable)=="table"             else "BindEasy() - State is invalid,
                                                   the nodename is '<name>'"
      BindThread(scenario, nodeName, thread)
      thread.CommandTable = stateTable             -- *** YOUR TABLE GOES HERE ***
      return thread
  BindThread(scenario, nodeName, thread)                         [proto 13]
      node = Node_Find(scenario, nodeName)         -- nil here -> later PM_BindThread NULL
      thread.State = nil ; thread.TypeId = 666
      thread.__UniqueID = Script_BindThread(node)  -- the ENGINE command, id 2001
      thread.Node = node
      __ThreadTable[thread.__UniqueID] = thread
  __BeginPlay(node, thread)   -- called BY THE ENGINE when the node runs
      thread.ShieldGuardActive = 0 ; thread.ShotHitActive = 0
      if type(thread.CommandTable)=="table" then GotoState(thread, thread.CommandTable)
      elseif type(thread.BeginPlay)=="function" then thread:BeginPlay() end
  GotoState(thread, cmdTable)                                    [proto 68]
      if thread.TypeId ~= 666 then thread = thread.Thread end    -- accepts a State too
      thread.State = {}                            -- a FRESH state object every time
      assert both are tables (else Script_Error)
      thread.State.Node = thread.Node
      thread.State.Thread = thread
      thread.State.CommandTable = cmdTable         -- *** your table lands here ***
      thread.State.HLB = { NumOfEntries=0, OldCommandTable={}, CommandTableIndication={} }
      thread.State.Sensors = {}                    -- *** sensors live on the STATE ***
      if type(State.CommandTable.Start)=="function" then State.CommandTable.Start(State) end

  So: BindEasy(node, name, T) => thread.CommandTable = T ; engine calls __BeginPlay =>
  GotoState => State = {Node, Thread, CommandTable=T, HLB, Sensors} and T.Start(State).
  YOUR functions are always called with the STATE as first arg, never the thread.

=== HOW A CALLBACK IS DISPATCHED (and how it fails silently) ===
  SetEngineValue(state, name, value)                             [glue proto 67]
      = CallFunction(state.Thread, "SetStateValue_"..name, value)
  CallFunction(x, cbName, param)                                 [glue proto 26]
      if type(x) ~= "table" -> Script_Error "Thread or Block is invalid" ; return
      if type(x.NumOfThreads)=="number" -> BLOCK path (loop over member threads)
      state = x.State
      PATH A (x is a bound thread, state is a table):
          f = state.CommandTable[cbName]
          if type(f) ~= "function" then RETURN SILENTLY      <-- NO error, NO log!
          f(state, param)
      PATH B (no valid state, x is a plain table):
          if type(x) ~= "table" -> Script_Error " CallFunction() - No valid block"
          f = x[cbName]
          if type(f) ~= "function" -> Script_Error "<name> is not a valid function"
          f(x, param)
  *** THE TWO SILENT-FAILURE MODES ***
    1. Bound thread whose callback is not in CommandTable -> PATH A returns silently.
    2. A node put into simulation BEFORE BindEasy: the engine may run __BeginPlay while
       thread.CommandTable is still nil -> GotoState never runs -> State stays nil ->
       every later callback takes PATH B against the BindEasy wrapper table (which has no
       callbacks, they are in .CommandTable) -> nothing happens.
    RULE: for nodes created at RUNTIME (after Game_LoadProgress_Leave), call BindEasy
    BEFORE Node_EnterSimulation. In __InitLayout the ordering is harmless because the
    simulation is not running yet.

=== SENSORS (sensor.sco = LibList.Sensor_Basis, loaded via SensorLib = LoadLib("Sensor"))
  Sensor_Basis exposes (verified): ActivateAvpSensor / ActivateAvpSensorComplex /
  ActivateAvpSensorComplexLeave / DeactivateAvpSensor / DeactivateAvpSensorLeave /
  ActivateShotHitSensor / DeactivateShotHitSensor / ActivateShieldGuard /
  DeactivateShieldGuard / ActivateEnemyGuard / DeactivateEnemyGuard /
  ActivateThreatening / DeactivateThreatening -- plus the reaction tables SS_AvpStack,
  SS_AvpStackLeave, SS_ShotHit, SS_ShieldGuard, SS_EnemyGuard, SS_Threatening.

  ActivateAvpSensorComplex(state, acousticSens, i1, i2, i3, i4, bAcoustic, bVisible,
                           bPresence)     -- 9 params; FIRST ARG IS THE **STATE**
      Trigger_ActivateSensor(state.Node, bAcoustic, bVisible, bPresence)  -- int8 x3
      Trigger_SetInterestList(state.Node, i1, i2, i3, i4)   -- 4 CLASS-PREFIX strings
      Trigger_SetAcousticSensibility(state.Node, acousticSens)  -- e.g. "medium"
      beh = CloneTable(SS_AvpStack)
      InsertSensorBehaviour(state, "AVPSensor", beh)   -- writes state.Sensors["AVPSensor"]
      state.Thread.AvpBehaviour = beh
  => Passing the THREAD instead of the STATE crashes/no-ops: a thread has neither
  .Sensors nor .Thread. Campaign call sites pass the state (they are inside a Code step
  whose first arg is the State). Interest list = class-name prefixes of the objects that
  may trip it, e.g. "pla" (player ships), "elt" (El Topo assets); empty strings pad.
  SS_AvpStack(state) = SetEngineValue(state, "Triggered", 1) ; Node_LeaveSimulation(
  state.Node)   -- one-shot: raise the flag and disable itself.
  InsertSensorBehaviour(state, key, fn) = state.Sensors[key] = fn         [glue proto 37]
  __GetStateFunction checks G_SensorCallbackTable / state.Sensors FIRST, so a fired
  sensor PRE-EMPTS the normal state function for that tick.

=== THE CORRECT RECIPE FOR A RUNTIME TRIGGER (all pitfalls avoided) ===
    S_INTRO = {
      Start = function(state)
        SensorLib.ActivateAvpSensorComplex(state, "medium", "pla", "", "", "",
                                           False, False, True)   -- presence only
      end,
      SetStateValue_Triggered = function(state, v)
        SendRadioMessageTake(playerNode, playerNode, KEY)
        -- Node_LeaveSimulation(state.Node) already done by SS_AvpStack
      end,
    }
    t = Node_CreateNode("NOD_Trigger","MyTrigger")
    Node_AddSon(Node_Find("/Scenario_Dynamic/Trigger"), t)     -- the Trigger dir
    Body_SetPosition(t, MAT_Vector3(x,y,z))
    Trigger_SetPresenceSphere(t, radius)
    th = BindEasy(Node_Find("/Scenario_Dynamic/Trigger"), "MyTrigger", S_INTRO)  -- FIRST
    Node_EnterSimulation(t)                                                       -- THEN
  GotoState calls S_INTRO.Start(State) automatically, which arms the sensor with the
  correct STATE; SS_AvpStack then raises "Triggered" -> CallFunction PATH A finds
  SetStateValue_Triggered in State.CommandTable -> your line plays.

=== DEBUGGING THIS AREA ===
  Because PATH A fails silently, put Script_Log("marker") (section 69) at the top of
  Start and of every callback. If Start's marker appears but the callback's never does,
  the sensor is not arming (wrong first arg, wrong interest list, node not in simulation).
  If neither appears, __BeginPlay never ran (bind/EnterSimulation ordering, or Node_Find
  returned nil -> check the level's directory names, they differ per level).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
71. COVERAGE AUDIT, TOOLCHAIN CAPABILITIES & WHAT IS NOT YET ANALYSED
    (rescue pass 4: an inventory so nothing is lost and later work has a map)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Ran a systematic audit: built a checklist of EVERY topic analysed across the whole
project and grep-verified each against this handoff. Method: a Python script mapping
each topic to a signature string, then `grep -icE` over the file. Result: 58/58 topics
are documented (the one apparent miss, "-noterrain", was a grep artifact -- it is in
section 69). So the ANALYSIS is fully captured. What follows is (a) the toolchain's
proven WRITE capabilities, and (b) an honest list of what was never analysed, for
whoever continues.

=== TOOLCHAIN: WHAT WE CAN ACTUALLY BUILD (not just read) ===
Verified this session by round-trip:
  * PAK write: pak_pack.py pack(entries, out, version=3) -- rebuilds aquanox6.pak.
  * Cooked-DES write: descook.py serialize(roots) -- ROUND-TRIP IS BYTE-IDENTICAL on
    maplist~.des (1158 B in == 1158 B out, exact). So we CAN edit and re-cook cooked
    .des files (maplist, sdialog, osd, sam, mtake-cooked...) without corruption. This is
    the missing piece that makes the mp_07 "Vulcano" restore (section 60) actually doable:
    parse maplist~.des -> bump numofmaps to 9 -> append the [map8] entry -> serialize ->
    pack. 
  * Plaintext DES: station_d.des is PLAIN TEXT (0% nul) -- the daGama restore (section 46)
    is a literal text edit, no cooking needed.
  * Lua bytecode: we READ .sco via lua4dis.py and GRAFT via the build_*.py technique
    (copy a proven proto, patch constants/instructions). We do NOT have a full Lua-4
    COMPILER; new script logic is assembled by grafting onto existing bytecode (how
    build_sp_1n1.py / build_narrow_waves.py work). This is the main practical limitation:
    large new scripts are tedious. A real Lua 4.0 compiler (luac 4.0) would remove it --
    worth seeking/porting for ambitious mission work.
  * Textures: DDS conversion (DXT3/5, keep alpha+mipmaps) established (section 65).

=== WHAT WAS NEVER ANALYSED (the honest gap list, prioritised for later) ===
HIGH VALUE (would directly help modding):
  1. HOW TO ENABLE THE BUILT-IN CHEATS/CONSOLE (section 69 B). The strings exist
     (INDESTRUCTABLE, MISSION COMPLETE, menu_toggleconsole). Not traced: the key binding
     / -debugger / config flag that turns them on. An official in-game debug path is the
     single highest-value unfound thing. START: ini/control_menu.des bindings; xref the
     CHEAT strings (0x60f874..) to find the key handler; test -debugger.
  2. THE PHYSICS / CONTROL MODEL. The .osd [control] block (velocities, accel, turn
     rates, buoyancy) is catalogued as data, but HOW the engine integrates it (the
     per-frame movement update) was never disassembled. Needed for deep handling tuning.
  3. THE SHOP / INVENTORY / ECONOMY (STY_/shop). Stations have shops (menu_shop_item_*
     sounds, per-category); the buy/sell/credits logic and the item_d/gun_d/price data
     were only lightly touched. Needed to add purchasable items or change loadouts.
  4. THE HUD / COCKPIT system (Cockpit_RenderObject/RenderRadarObject seen in strings,
     section 69 E; NOD_Cockpit class, 8616 B). Radar, targeting, the IFF colours. Needed
     for HUD mods.
  5. The remaining ~250 message-handler ADDRESSES (section 55): mechanical per-class hand
     work on the Vessel/Vehicle/Turret/PhysicalCharacter sub-switch chains. Not required
     for modding (names+ids+signatures suffice), but completes the reference.

MEDIUM VALUE:
  6. NOD_Message +0x10/+0x11 flag meanings (local vs networked message).
  7. The collision/damage loop that actually subtracts shield points (section 51 located
     the arrays; the subtraction site in the collision handler was not pinned).
  8. The PM_ (Process Manager) dispatch table / how messages are serialised for MP
     (section 15 has the model; the wire format + the id->PM handler table are open).
  9. DirectMusic (DMUS_) transition logic -- how mood fragments are selected at runtime.
 10. The savegame format (Savegame string exists) -- for mods that persist state.
 11. Terrain format (.terrain / heightfield) beyond "it loads" -- for new map geometry.
 12. The AN2 engine (AN2.dat) -- only cross-referenced; a full AN2 pass is a separate
     project (its PAK cipher + subversion are known, section: formats).

LOW VALUE / CURIOSITY:
 13. cft_vulcano.sco (the earlier typo'd Vulcano build) diff vs ctf_vulcano.sco.
 14. The EnTrOx cut content depth (35 orphaned meshes -- what missions they belonged to).
 15. Font/menu rendering, video/intro playback.

=== ORIENTATION FOR THE NEXT SESSION ===
Everything ANALYSED is in this file (sections 1-70) + the JSON catalogs + build_*.py.

maplist~.des, tools proven above) and the daGama restore (edit plaintext station_d.des).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
72. THE BUILT-IN CHEATS + DEBUG CONSOLE -- SOLVED: bindable via the controls file
    (gap-list item 1 -- fully traced this session)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The highest-value gap is CLOSED. AN1 has built-in cheats AND an in-game console, and
BOTH are ordinary bindable INPUT ACTIONS -- you enable them by binding a key in the
controls config. No exe patch, no external tool.

THE ACTION DISPATCHER. Player input is turned into ACTIONS handled by a 108-entry jump
table at 0x457f0c, indexed by (action_id) in the input handler at 0x455a09
(eax=[msg]; add eax,-2; cmp eax,0x6b; ja default; jmp [eax*4+0x457f0c]). The cheat
actions sit at ids 51 and 65-70:
    id 51  cheat_mission_failed            -> 0x456a19
    id 65  cheat_indestructable            -> 0x456729 -> 0x466760 (invuln bit @shield+0x70)
    id 66  cheat_invisible                 -> 0x4567d1 -> 0x466820 (toggle player+0x300)
    id 67  cheat_terminatetarget           -> 0x45677d -> 0x4668c0 (zero target shield -> kill)
    id 69  cheat_mission_completed_auto    -> 0x4568cd
    id 70  cheat_mission_completed_manual  -> 0x456975
All print a coloured HUD line via 0x453b60.

THE GATE (why they seem "off"): every cheat handler first checks the GAME object
g=*0x661984 and runs ONLY IF g[+0x28]==0 AND g[+0x520]==0 (network/MP-disable flags --
cheats off online). In single player these are zero, so the cheats DO run once bound.
(The player+0x2b8/2bc/2c0 checks are just the torpedo inventory = "player spawned".)

*** THE ACTIONS ARE NAMED AND BINDABLE *** The input config parser (around 0x598000-
0x59a800) recognises 176 action names via strcmp (0x5206d0), and the list INCLUDES the
cheat + console/debug actions by name:
    cheat_indestructable, cheat_invisible, cheat_terminatetarget,
    cheat_mission_completed_auto, cheat_mission_completed_manual, cheat_mission_failed,
    menu_toggleconsole, menu_togglestatistics, menu_togglegamestate, menu_toggleactionmenu
So they are first-class bindable actions -- they were simply left UNBOUND in the shipped
control profiles.

HOW TO ENABLE (data only, uses our proven cooked-DES writer, section 71):
  The key bindings live in dat/menu/control_sp~.des (single player), control_mp~.des,
  control_default~.des and control_0..3~.des -- all COOKED DES. Each binding is a
  record: action = "<name>" ; numofevent = N ; [event0]{ device="keyboard",
  source="<key>", modifier="plain" }. (control_menu.des in ini/ is only joystick
  deadzone/scale -- NOT the key map.)
  RECIPE to bind, e.g., god mode to a key:
    1. descook.parse(control_sp~.des)  (round-trips byte-identical, section 71)
    2. clone an existing simple binding record (e.g. weapon_nextgun) and change
       action -> "cheat_indestructable", event source -> your chosen key (e.g. "F5")
    3. add records for cheat_terminatetarget, cheat_mission_completed_auto,
       menu_toggleconsole as desired
    4. descook.serialize -> write back -> pack into the mod PAK
  Then in game, pressing the key toggles the cheat; menu_toggleconsole opens the console.

WHY THIS MATTERS FOR THE WHOLE PROJECT: this is the official in-game test harness we
wanted. God mode to survive while watching scripted events; mission-complete to skip;
the console/statistics overlays to inspect state; terminate-target to test death
callbacks. It removes the need to actually win a fight to test late-mission scripting.
STRONGLY RECOMMENDED as the first thing to build before more mission work -- a
"debug controls" PAK that binds these six cheat actions + the console.

REMAINING UNKNOWN (minor): what the console actually accepts as typed input (does it
parse the same action names, or Lua, or its own verbs?) -- needs an in-game test once
menu_toggleconsole is bound. The cheats themselves do not need the console; they are
direct key actions.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
73. THE [control] HANDLING MODEL -- data fully mapped (physics integration still open)
    (gap-list item 2; the data side that matters for modding)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Started gap item 2 (physics/control). The DATA layer -- what a modder tunes -- is fully
mapped; the engine's per-frame integration of it is located in principle but not yet
disassembled. Being honest about which is which.

THE [control] BLOCK (in every ship .osd, cooked DES; fields are self-describing name\0
type value pairs INSIDE the osd, not from a central template):
    movepv          max forward velocity        (phobocaster 43.75)
    movenv          max reverse velocity        (21.875 -- reverse is ~half forward)
    movet           time-constant to reach it   (1.5 s; higher = more sluggish accel)
    strafehorrvel   horizontal strafe velocity ; strafehorrt its time-constant
    strafevertvel   vertical strafe velocity   ; strafevertt its time-constant
    turnpv / turnnv max positive/negative turn rate (deg/s; phobocaster 90)
    turnt           turn time-constant (1.5) ;  turnr  turn "radius"/damping (0.47)
    swingt / swinga hull sway period/amplitude (the idle roll/pitch bob)
    movefriction    3 floats (x,y,z linear drag) ; turnfriction angular drag
So handling = target velocities + first-order time constants (movet/turnt) + friction.
It is a smoothed-velocity model: input sets a target, the ship eases toward it at rate
1/movet, drag pulls it back. There is no mass/thrust simulation exposed here.

BALANCE REFERENCE (measured across ship types -- useful for retuning):
  ship            movepv movenv movet strafe turnpv turnt turnr swinga
  Phobocaster(pl) 43.75  21.875 1.5   10     90     1.5   0.47  0.20
  Toiler (weak)   32.0   22.0   4.0   16     90     1.5   0.48  0.34   (sluggish accel)
  Bomber (heavy)  28.0   24.0   2.9   24     110    6.7   0.65  0.30   (slow to turn)
  Frigate(capital)20.0   -      15.0  1      15     17.0  1.00  0.40   (barely manoeuvres)
  Scout (fast)    32.0   24.0   1.2   24     95     2.0   0.75  0.34   (snappy)
Pattern: the player ship has the highest top speed; capital ships have huge movet/turnt
(15-17 s) so they crawl; scouts have low movet (1.2) so they feel responsive. To make a
modded enemy nimble, lower movet/turnt and raise turnpv; to make a lumbering boss, raise
them.

MODDING: handling is 100% .osd data. Clone a ship OSD, edit the [control] floats, done.
The values are raw little-endian float32 at a name\0 offset inside the cooked OSD -- our
descook writer round-trips them (section 71), so edits are safe. (movefriction is a
3-float vector; keep it 12 bytes.)

WHAT IS STILL OPEN (the actual physics code): the per-frame update that reads these into
motion lives in the vessel/vehicle simulation tick, not in a name-keyed parser (the OSD
field names are NOT present as strings in the exe -- they are matched during the generic
DES parse and stored at fixed object offsets, then the physics tick reads those offsets).
To pin it: find NOD_Vessel's Node_EnterSimulation/tick method and the code that
multiplies a target velocity by (dt/movet)-style factors and applies movefriction. This
is a sizeable disassembly task and is NOT required for handling mods (the data is
sufficient). Deprioritised accordingly.

RELATED DATA BLOCKS in the same ship OSD (catalogued, for completeness): [avoidance]
(obstacle avoidance tuning), [escapebehaviour]/[attackbehaviour]/[patrolbehaviour]
(the AI-template tuning read by the library behaviours of section 57 via GetInnerValue-
like access), [enemydetection] (sensor ranges), [buzzerparam] (drone launcher). These
are all data the modder can edit the same way.

STILL TODO: disassemble the vessel physics tick (multiply-by-dt/movet + friction apply)
if deep flight-model changes are ever needed; otherwise the [control] data is complete.
*** CORRECTED BY SECTION 84: the parser for these fields WAS found (MOT_Vessel::Parse
0x5e91a4; the names ARE exe strings in CamelCase, name-keyed via getter 0x507800 into
fixed offsets). Section 84 also shows the PLAYER ship has a separate, richer field set
(Alpha/Beta/Gamma). The first-order-lerp reading below is confirmed from the code. ***
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
74. THE SHOP / INVENTORY / ECONOMY SYSTEM -- fully data-driven and editable
    (gap-list item 3; all databases are PLAIN TEXT)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Third HIGH gap done. AN1's economy is entirely in plain-text .des tables -- no cooking,
no exe involvement beyond the UI. You can add items, reprice everything, or change ship
stats with a text editor.

THE DATABASES (dat/sty/, all PLAIN TEXT):
  item_d.des    33 purchasable items: { Key, Class, Name, Image (menu TGA), Info, Price }
      Class distribution: gun 12, torpedo 10, generator 4, repairdevice 4, specialitem 2,
      buzzer 1. Price range 90 .. 160,900 credits. The Info field is the shop tooltip
      (uses '~' as the line separator, e.g. "Schaden KIN: 140 MJ~Nachladezeit: 0,40 s").
      GUNS:      Vendetta 1 (2200) < Vendetta 2 (5900) < EMPactor (8500) < PlasmaGun 1
                 (9000) < Hitman (9200) < VendettaGatling (12400) < PlasmaGun 2 (15100)
                 < DoomMortar (27700) < PlasmaGatling (33700) < LaserGatling (71200)
                 < Sizzler (73300) < SkalarHaubitze (160900)
      TORPEDOES: Stanley 210, ThresherShark 240, TigerShark 330, BullShark 360,
                 FlashShark 410, ManEater 450, HammerheadShark 570, Leech 850,
                 BigBang 1 860, BigBang 2 1100
      GENERATORS: Small 1800 / Medium 6400 / Big 14200 / Fusion Block 31500
      REPAIR:     Patch Kit 2010 / Robot 10900 / Nano 1 21200 / Nano 2 32200
      SPECIAL:    Silator 3960 / Booster 11900 ; BUZZER: Buzzer 90
  ship_d.des    9 purchasable ships, with FULL STATS:
      { Key, Name, ShortDescription, Price, MaxVelocity (3 numbers: cruise/boost/?),
        MaxAcceleration, ArmorKIN, ArmorEMP, MaxNumOfTorpedos, MaxNumOfBuzzers,
        MaxNumOfGuns, Info, Image, DisplayScale }
      Toiler 182,200 (58/115/79, acc 8, KIN 16000, 4 torps) ... Phobocaster 283,100
      (90/180/126, acc 25, KIN 48000, 12 torps). Note EVERY ship has MaxNumOfGuns = 2.
      Skipjack is the outlier: acc 80 (!) but only 20000 armour -- the glass cannon.
  gun_d.des / mp_gun_d.des  50 entries each: { Key, Text } -- the DISPLAY NAMES of guns
      (SP and MP have separate name tables; MP drops the spaces: "Doom Mortar" vs
      "DoomMortar"). These are the labels; the ballistics live in the gun .osd (section 17).
  nod_item_d.des  50 entries: in-world pickup labels (section 43).
  cargo_d.des     104 freight names (section 43).

WHERE THE SHOP LIVES. Stations are levels (section 44); their ROOMS are in room_d.des:
  { Key, Name, Station, Category, Image, Sound } with 91 rooms across 8 CATEGORIES --
  now decoded from the room names:
      1 = office (El Topos Buero, Changs Buero, Waals Buero, KW-Zentrale)   5 rooms
      2 = bar / tavern (Junta Junta, White Water, Salpetre Tavern, Bedlam)  14
      3 = DOCK                                                             18
      4 = WAFFENLAGER (weapon store)                                       18
      5 = HAENDLER (trader/shop)                                           18
      7 = public square / press centre                                     11
      8 = crew room                                                         3
      9 = Abfertigung (clearance/handling)                                  4
  So every station has a Dock (3), a Weapon store (4) and a Trader (5). The UI plays
  per-class sounds menu_shop_item_gun/tor/rep/spi/buz/gen (+ *1 variants) -- exactly the
  six item_d Class values, confirming the shop is driven by item_d.Class.

MODDING (all plain-text, no tools needed):
  * Reprice or rebalance: edit Price in item_d.des / ship_d.des.
  * Add an item: new [ItemN] block with a free Key, a Class from the six, a Name, a
    menu TGA and a Price. The engine picks it up from the table; the actual weapon
    behaviour still comes from its .osd (section 16/17), so pair a new gun entry with a
    gun .osd + bullet .osd.
  * Ship stats shown in the shop (ship_d) are DISPLAY values; the flight model comes from
    the ship's .osd [control] block (section 73) and the shields from [shield]. Change
    both to keep the shop text honest.
  * Item availability per station: not a separate table -- the shop lists item_d entries;
    gating (if any) is by mission progress, not by station.

STILL OPEN (small): where the player's CREDITS balance is stored (a "Credits" string
exists at 0x65ab10 -- it is the HUD/menu label) and how purchases persist (savegame
format, section 71 gap 10). Not needed for balance mods.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
75. THE HUD / COCKPIT SYSTEM -- a parsed, data-driven interface
    (gap-list item 4; plus a PAK-header correction)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Fourth HIGH gap. The cockpit/HUD is NOT hard-coded: HUD_CockpitInterface::Parse()
(string @0x658518) reads a cockpit definition, and the exe carries the full key
vocabulary. Everything below is a configuration key the parser accepts.

THE COCKPIT CLASS. NOD_Cockpit is a registered scene class (8616 bytes, section 26);
its render entry points name themselves in error strings:
    Cockpit_AllocateResources()   Cockpit_RenderObject()
    Cockpit_RenderRadarAnchors()  Cockpit_RenderRadarObject()
plus HUD_CockpitInterface::Parse() / ::GetTextLine (with "index of given line out of
range") and WrapTextIntoLines ("maximum text length exeeded" [sic]).

THE RADAR CONFIGURATION (24 keys, one contiguous block at 0x6581f0-0x658438) -- this is
the IFF colour scheme, i.e. how friend/foe/neutral/unknown look on the radar:
    Radar_ColorObjectFriend / ObjectEnemy / ObjectNeutral / ObjectUnknown
    ...and a *Offset variant of each (ObjectFriendOffset etc. -- the off-plane/height
       offset marker colour)
    Radar_ColorLockedFriend / LockedEnemy / LockedNeutral / LockedUnknown  (locked target)
    Radar_ColorAnchorPlayer / AnchorFriend / AnchorEnemy / AnchorNeutral / AnchorUnknown
       (the vertical "anchor" lines that show an object's depth relative to you)
    Radar_ColorFinTop / ColorFinBottom      (the radar disc's top/bottom fins)
    Radar_ColorTerrain                      (terrain echo colour)
    Radar_ClipObjectDimension               (clipping size)
    Radar_Scale / Radar_Translation         (radar size + on-screen position)
  So the classic AN radar (a 3D disc with depth anchors) is fully recolourable and
  repositionable by data.

THE OTHER HUD WIDGETS (same block):
    Compass_Scale / Compass_Translation     -- the heading compass
    Visor_Scale / Visor_Translation         -- the visor/reticle overlay
    Geometry                                -- the cockpit geometry entry
    MOTION + per-axis motion tuning: AlphaSign/AlphaStiffness/AlphaSensitivity,
       BetaSign/BetaStiffness/BetaSensitivity, GammaSign/GammaStiffness/GammaSensitivity
       -- i.e. the COCKPIT SWAY per rotation axis (how the cockpit lags behind your
       manoeuvres). Stiffness/Sensitivity/Sign per axis = a spring model.
  Related HUD elements seen elsewhere: hud_lock_direction, hud_lock_pointer,
  hud_waypoint_direction (the off-screen target/waypoint arrows), hud_vs_register,
  Hud_VS, Hud_MP_Info (the MP scoreboard), and the comm portraits
  hud_elf_characterNN.tga (section 42/43) with elf_in/elf_out/elf_sally_in/out sounds.

MODDING IMPLICATION: recolouring the radar (e.g. classic green, or colour-blind-friendly
IFF), resizing/moving radar/compass/visor, and softening or stiffening the cockpit sway
are all DATA edits in the cockpit definition that HUD_CockpitInterface::Parse() reads.
The definition file itself is a cooked DES in the PAKs (the cockpit .osd/.des referenced
by NOD_Cockpit) -- locate it by searching the PAKs for these key names, then edit with
descook (section 71) exactly like any other cooked DES.

--- CORRECTION: THE PAK HEADER (fix for the toolchain notes) ---
While rebuilding tools after a sandbox reset I mis-stated the PAK header. The correct
layout, verified on aquanox6.pak:
    offset 0  : "MASSIVEFILE\0"        (12 bytes, NOT 13)
    offset 12 : uint32 version          (= 3)
    offset 16 : uint32 numfiles         (e.g. 10 for aquanox6)
    offset 20 : NUL-terminated copyright string
                ("Copyright by Massive Development GmbH. All rights reserved.")
    then      : the (ciphered) directory -- entries begin with a 4-byte tag ("LPT\0"
                observed) followed by the 64-byte-key-obfuscated name/offset/size records.
  The filename cipher is the -3 byte shift documented in section 1. If a future session
  rebuilds pak_tool.py, start from THIS layout; my quick reconstruction that assumed a
  13-byte magic and an immediate 64-byte key failed.

STILL OPEN (small): the exact cockpit definition file name in the PAKs (search for
"Radar_Scale" inside PAK entries once the reader is rebuilt) and the numeric format of
the colour values (likely RGBA floats or packed ints).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
76. ANT_Rules (MP rules engine), THE CVAR SYSTEM & THE SERVER CONSOLE
    (new analysis: the last big untouched exe subsystem)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
A method-name survey of the exe (every "Class::Method()" error string) revealed which
subsystems are richly structured and therefore analysable. Top classes by named methods:
ANT_Rules 35, DSP_Display 33, STY_Base 29, MAI_Display 28, NET_Server 22, NOD_Game 15,
UTL_QuickIntegrator 14, GUI_Menu_Interface 12, MOS_CVar 11, NOD_Script 10, MAI_Handler 10.
