(NOTE: UTL_QuickIntegrator is NOT the physics integrator as its name suggests -- it
creates/destroys display, keyboard, mouse, joystick and the VEngine. The physics tick
remains unfound, section 73.)

=== ANT_Rules -- the complete multiplayer rules engine (25 methods, code ~0x52f000-0x536000)
  Lifecycle:   ANT_Rules(), ParseIniFile() (@0x533680, reads a "Property" section),
               StartGame(), PM_StartTime()
  Events:      Event_PlayerJoin, Event_PlayerLeave, Event_PlayerRespawn, Event_Kill,
               Event_Drop
  Heartbeat:   HeartBeat_CheckFragLimit, HeartBeat_CheckTimeLimit,
               HeartBeat_CheckCaptureLimit, HeartBeat_DroppedFlags
  CTF:         TouchOurFlag, TouchEnemyFlag, ResetFlag, Team_SetFlagStatus,
               GetFlagNameBase, TeamName, OtherTeam
  Queries:     Query_GetPlayerInfo/PlayerState/TeamState/PlayerTeamName,
               PlayerState_GetPointer
  So a CTF round is: StartGame -> per-frame HeartBeat_* limit checks + DroppedFlags
  timeout -> TouchEnemyFlag (pick up) / TouchOurFlag (capture or return) ->
  Team_SetFlagStatus -> CheckCaptureLimit ends it. GetFlagNameBase resolves the flag
  node names (matching team_blue/red_base + team_*_respawn_1..8 of sections 29/60).
  DIRECTLY RELEVANT to the mp_07 "Vulcano" restore: the map already contains those
  team bases and respawn nodes, so ANT_Rules will drive it once the map is listed.

=== THE CVAR SYSTEM (MOS_CVar) -- 90 configuration variables ===
MOS_CVar provides Find / Load / Save plus typed accessors S32_Get/Set, F32_Get/Set,
CString_Get/Set. The variables (all found as strings) group by prefix:
  dsp_ (16) display: width, height, depth, vsync, fsaa, triplebuffer, gammacorrection,
       pixelshader, trilinear, anisotropyquality, usemipmap, usecompressedtextures,
       texturecachesize, forcealphatest, avoidframebuffering, display
  ren_ (11) renderer QUALITY: terrain_lod, terrain_bumpmap, terrain_dlight,
       terrain_dshadow, terrain_singlepass, object_dlight, objectprelit_bumpmap,
       plankton_detail, plant_detail, radar_detail, rays_enable
  inp_ (8) input: keyboard, mouse, joystick, joystickenable, ff_enable/force/fire/impact
  men_ (7) menu state: activegametype, activenet, activeprofile, mapname, showempty,
       showfull, sortby
  si_  (7) server info: name, map, maxplayers, password, pinglimit, dedicated, advertise
  snd3d_(5)/snd2d_(4): enable, volume, channels, provider, speakertype, rate
  g_   (4) gameplay: autochangegun, showgun, identify_target, cdkey
  gi_  (4) game info: gametype, fraglimit, timelimit, capturelimit
  pi_  (4) player info: name, ship, team, password
  net_ (1) bandwidth
  CONFIG FILES: config.txt (general), ini\mp_gi.des (game info), ini\mp_si.des (server
  info), ini\mp_ban.des (ban list, via ANT_BanList::Load).
  MODDING VALUE: the ren_* switches are per-feature quality toggles (terrain bump
  mapping, dynamic lights/shadows, plankton/plant density, god rays). On modern hardware
  these can simply be turned up beyond what the options menu exposes -- a cheap visual
  upgrade for a 25-year-old game. dsp_pixelshader / dsp_anisotropyquality likewise.

=== THE SERVER CONSOLE (dedicated-server commands) ===
A command set exists alongside the CVars (block at 0x657bd4-0x657d10):
  cfg_list, cfg_edit, cfg_info, cfg_description, cfg_hostname, cfg_password,
  cfg_adminname, cfg_adminmail, cfg_advertise, cfg_location, cfg_fraglimit,
  cfg_timelimit, cfg_maxplayers, cfg_pinglimit, cfg_gametype,
  cfg_addmap, cfg_remmap, cfg_swapmap, cfg_listmapcycle,
  svr_shutdown, svr_restart, svr_listmapcycle
  ANT_Console methods: CheckMaps(), Map_GetDefaultMap(), Map_GetMapName(), with errors
  "map '%s' is not valid in %s mode", "invalid gametype", "error: no more maps can be
  added", "error: illegal map slot", "mapcycle set to default for new gametype!".
  *** THIS CONFIRMS A MAP-CYCLE API *** -- cfg_addmap adds a map at runtime and
  CheckMaps validates it against the gametype. So besides editing maplist~.des
  (section 60), a server can add mp_07 Vulcano via cfg_addmap. Worth testing as an
  ALTERNATIVE (and quicker) route to playing the cut map.

=== ALSO IDENTIFIED (not yet analysed, good next targets) ===
  STY_Base (15 *_LoadTable methods -- one per .des database: Item, Ship, Station, Room,
    Person, Take, Mission, Briefing, Dialog, Movie, Option, Mood, Difficulty,
    Acknowledge) = the loader layer that confirms our whole data model.
  STY_Player (Load, AddMovie, AddOption, LoadAndMergeMovies) = the SAVEGAME/story state.
  MAI_Handler (Handle_Dock, Handle_Equip, Handle_Equip_Item, Handle_Station,
    Handle_Dialog, Handle_Arrival, Handle_Options, RunOptionCommands) = the station/shop
    UI flow -- the missing half of section 74.
  Aqua_State_SinglePlayer (StartGame, HeartBeat, EquipPlayerToEngine) = how an SP mission
    boots and the player gets equipped.
  NET_Server (22) / NET_Client (9) / ANT_Client (9) = the network layer (PM_ wire format).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
77. THE STORY / STATION-MENU LAYER (STY_ + MAI_) -- incl. THE SAVEGAME STRUCTURE
    (new analysis; closes the savegame gap and completes section 74's shop half)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The STY_ (story/state) and MAI_ (station menu) layers are now mapped. This is the
between-missions half of the game: docking, dialogue, equipping, buying, saving.

=== STY_Base -- the DATABASE LOADER (one method per .des table) ===
  Take_LoadTable, Mood_LoadTable, Person_LoadTable, Dialog_LoadTable, D_LoadTable,
  Room_LoadTable, Option_LoadTable, Movie_LoadTable, Mission_LoadTable,
  Station_LoadTable, Item_LoadTable, Ship_LoadTable, Briefing_LoadTable,
  Acknowledge_LoadTable, Difficulty_LoadTable
  Each reads its dat/sty/*_d.des with a "NumOf<Thing>" count + per-entry blocks
  ("Movie"/"NumOfMovie", "Mission"/"NumOfMission", "Station"/"NumOfStation",
  "Briefing"/"NumOfBriefing", "Acknowledge"/"NumOfAcknowledge", "Difficulty"/
  "NumOfDifficulty", "NumOfShip"). Errors are "<...>_LoadTable() - file not found".
  => independent confirmation that our whole data model (sections 12/42/43/44/74) is
  exactly the set of tables the engine loads. Per-record loaders: STY_Take::Load,
  STY_Person::Load, STY_Room::Load, STY_Station::Load, STY_Item::Load, STY_Ship::Load,
  STY_Mission::Load, STY_Briefing::Load, STY_Dialog::Load, STY_Movie::Load,
  STY_Option::Load, STY_Mood::Load, STY_Difficulty::Load, STY_Acknowledge::Load.
  STY_Item::GetTypeByName validates the item Class against exactly six names --
  gun, torpedo, buzzer, generator, repairdevice, specialitem -- else "illegal item name".
  (Confirms section 74's class list is complete and closed.)

*** THE SAVEGAME / PLAYER-STATE STRUCTURE (STY_Player::Load, fields at 0x65aa78-0x65ab54)
  TimeStamp                save time
  GameDifficulty           chosen difficulty (-> difficulty_d.des)
  Credits                  *** the player's money *** (answers section 74's open question)
  AdditionalStationKey     extra station unlocked beyond the story default
  NumOfActiveOptions + OptionKey + OptionTick    the active story OPTIONS (see below)
  NumOfBannedOptions + BannedKey                 options permanently disabled
  NumOfFinishedMovies + MovieKey                 cutscenes already seen
  InventoryItemPlayer                            items owned
  InventoryItemEquipped                          items currently fitted to the ship
  (STY_Inventory::Load / GetItemInfoByIndex manage the two inventories.)
  So a savegame = time + difficulty + credits + unlocked station + the option/movie
  progress key lists + the two inventories. Progress is expressed as OPTION KEYS, not
  as a linear mission number -- the story is a graph of enabled/banned options.

=== STY_Command -- the OPTION COMMAND LANGUAGE (the story scripting of the menu) ===
  MAI_Handler::RunOptionCommands (@~0x574000) executes a list of commands attached to a
  story option, dispatching on a 9-entry jump table at 0x5741ec (types 0..8; an invalid
  type logs "illegal option type"). The arms call STY_Player-side helpers
  (0x563ea0 = AddOption, 0x563f80/0x5640a0/0x564010 = AddMovie / inventory / flags).
  STY_Command::Load + ::GetCommand ("index out of range") read them from option~.des
  (dat/sty/option~.des, 68 KB, COOKED -- the biggest story data file).
  => This is how the campaign advances between missions: completing something runs the
  option's commands, which unlock the next options, movies, stations and items. To add
  custom story progression you edit option~.des (cooked; use descook, section 71).

=== MAI_Handler -- the station UI flow (10 handlers) ===
  Handle (dispatcher), Handle_Arrival (you dock), Handle_Station (station overview),
  Handle_Dock, Handle_Dialog + Handle_Select_Dialog (talk to people -> sdialog/stake,
  sections 44/45), Handle_Equip + Handle_Equip_Item (the SHOP/loadout -> item_d/ship_d,
  section 74), Handle_Options (the story options above), RunOptionCommands.
  MAI_Display drives the widgets in named button groups:
    TK1_BUT_* / TK2_BUT_* (dialogue take buttons: SetHead/SetName/SetRoom/On/Off/Empty)
    MOV_BUT_*  (movie list)      EQU_BUT_*  (equipment slots)
    GAL_BUT_*  (galaxy/station map: SetName/SetRoom/On/Off)
    LEV_BUT_*  (level/mission select: SetHead/SetName/SetRoom/On/Off/Empty)
  So the station screen is a data-driven button UI over the STY_ tables -- portraits
  (SetHead -> person_d ImageElf), names, and room assignment all come from the databases.

MODDING IMPLICATIONS
  * Give the player money/items at start: edit the savegame fields (Credits,
    InventoryItemPlayer) once the savegame file format is confirmed on disk.
  * Add story progression (new missions/stations unlocking): edit option~.des commands.
  * The six item classes are fixed in code -- you can add ITEMS but not new CLASSES.
  * Station screens need no code: add rooms (room_d), dialogues (sdialog/stake) and
    items (item_d) and the MAI_ UI picks them up.

STILL OPEN: the on-disk savegame FILE (path/extension and whether it is cooked DES --
STY_Player::Load reads named fields, so it is very likely a DES); the exact semantics of
the 9 STY_Command types (needs option~.des decoded alongside the jump-table arms).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
78. THE NETWORK LAYER (NET_ / ANT_) -- the complete MP protocol, 48 message types
    (closes the "PM_ wire format" gap; refines the ANT block map of section 27)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The multiplayer stack is now mapped end to end. It also completes the story that runs
through this whole document: a script call becomes a message, and THESE are the messages
that travel over the wire.

=== TWO LAYERS ===
  TRANSPORT (DirectPlay-style session handling):
    NET_Server (15): Startup, Shutdown, Host, ConnectDirectly, DisconectRemote [sic],
      Send, Receive, GetName, GetSessionInfo, StartSessionEnumeration, and the callback
      processors Process_CONNECT_COMPLETE / CREATE_PLAYER / DESTROY_PLAYER /
      TERMINATE_SESSION / RECEIVE.
    NET_Client (6): Startup, ConnectDirectly, Disconnect, Send, Receive,
      StopSessionEnumeration.
  GAME LAYER (the PM_ = Process Manager side, section 15):
    ANT_Client (10): HeartBeat, ReceiveNetworkMessages, CreateObject, and the PM_ senders
      PM_CreateObject, PM_CreatePlayerOnClient, PM_SendClientID, PM_SendGameInfo,
      PM_SendServerInfo, PM_TimeStamp. Plus ANT_Client_Transport::ReceiveNetworkMessages.
    ANT_Server: PM_StartScenegraphDuplication (+ ANT_Server_Transport).
  *** THE KEY IDEA: PM_StartScenegraphDuplication *** -- on join, the server DUPLICATES
  ITS SCENE GRAPH to the client by sending node-creation messages; afterwards only deltas
  (body/shield/state updates) are sent. That is exactly why every script command is a
  self-contained (id + args) message (sections 26/27/37/41): the same message that runs a
  command locally can be serialised to recreate or update the node remotely.

=== THE 48 REGISTERED NETWORK MESSAGES (four id blocks -- refines section 27) ===
  32000 ANT_Rules (7): ConnectCompleted, RulesTeamUpdate, RulesPlayerUpdate,
        RulesPlayerInfoUpdate, EventCTF, StartTime, ChangeTeam
  37000 ANT_Server (6): RemoteJoined, RemoteLeft, SessionTerminated,
        StartScenegraphDuplication, StartScenegraphSync, RemoteConsole
  38000 ANT_Client (5): ConnectCompleted, ConnectAborted, SessionTerminated,
        DuplicationComplete, MapChange
  39000 ANT_Message (30): CreateObject, TimeStamp, ObjectStateChange, SendClientInfo,
        ClientIsLeft, AskForPlayerRecreation, CreatePlayerOnClient, BodyUpdate,
        BodyAnglesUpdate, ShieldUpdateAbsolute, ShieldUpdateRelative, DestroyNode,
        ObjectWasKilled, SetNodeFlags, SpawnBullet, SpawnTorpedoOnClient,
        SpawnBuzzerOnClient, CreateTorpedoOnServer, CreateBuzzerOnServer,
        TorpedoImpactEffect, SimpleExplosion, SpawnSplash, SendVoice, SetBandwidth,
        SendClientInfo/SetClientInfo, SendGameInfo, SendServerInfo, Authenticate{Query,
        Reply,Failure}
  (Section 27 recorded ANT as "32000 + 39000"; the correct picture is FOUR blocks:
   32000 rules, 37000 server, 38000 client, 39000 object/state messages.)

=== WHAT THE PROTOCOL TELLS US ===
  * State replication is explicit and coarse: BodyUpdate / BodyAnglesUpdate (movement),
    ShieldUpdateAbsolute vs ShieldUpdateRelative (damage -- absolute for resync, relative
    for deltas), ObjectStateChange, SetNodeFlags, ObjectWasKilled, DestroyNode.
  * Spawning is split client/server: CreateTorpedoOnServer / SpawnTorpedoOnClient (and
    the same for buzzers) -- the client asks, the server authorises, then tells everyone
    to spawn the visual. SpawnBullet, SimpleExplosion, SpawnSplash, TorpedoImpactEffect
    are pure effect broadcasts.
  * There is voice chat (SendVoice) and per-client bandwidth control (SetBandwidth,
    the net_bandwidth cvar of section 76).
  * ANT_Server_RemoteConsole (37005) = a REMOTE CONSOLE message -- the cfg_*/svr_*
    commands of section 76 can be sent over the network to a dedicated server.
  * Authentication exists (AuthenticateQuery/Reply/Failure) alongside the g_cdkey cvar.
  * ANT_ServerInfo (Load/Save/SetMapName/RemoveMapName/SwapMapNames) and ANT_GameInfo
    (Load/Save/GetNameByGameType) persist to ini\mp_si.des / ini\mp_gi.des -- the
    SetMapName/SwapMapNames/RemoveMapName trio is what cfg_addmap/swapmap/remmap drive
    (section 76), confirming the runtime map-cycle route for restoring mp_07 "Vulcano".

MODDING RELEVANCE: for the Vulcano restore, nothing here needs changing -- the map is
driven by ANT_Rules (section 76) and the standard messages. The value of this section is
(a) it closes the PM_/wire-format gap, (b) it confirms the scene-graph duplication model
that explains the whole message architecture, and (c) it documents the remote console and
map-cycle API as an alternative route to adding maps.

STILL OPEN (low priority): the byte-level packet encoding (an ANT_MessageBuffer with an
"Encode_AddMessage" path and a "buffer is to small" [sic] error exists; the per-message
serialisation format was not disassembled), and the ban list format (ini\mp_ban.des via
ANT_BanList::Load).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
79. THE GAMEPLAY CLASSES (NOD_Game / NOD_Player / NOD_Gun / Aqua_Spawn)
    -- and PROOF that item pickups work in single player
    (refines section 72's cheat gate; strongly supports gap item 5b)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
=== Aqua_Spawn -- the central spawn factory (11 methods) ===
  SpawnBullet, SpawnTorpedo, SpawnGun, SpawnItemBox, SpawnEmitter, SpawnDipol,
  SpawnEscapePod, SpawnSplashDamage, GetBulletRange, NOD_GetBulletVelocity,
  PreCache_MultiPlayer.
  Everything that appears in the world at runtime comes through here. Note
  *** SpawnItemBox *** is a first-class engine spawner, right next to SpawnBullet and
  SpawnTorpedo -- item boxes are not an MP bolt-on but a normal spawnable (supports
  section 61). GetBulletRange / NOD_GetBulletVelocity are the ballistics queries the AI
  uses for lead/aim (they read the bullet .osd, section 17).

=== NOD_Player (9) -- input, pickups, radar ===
  Input_Handle, HeartBeat, HeartBeat_MP_Ghost, HeartBeat_MP_Thing, ParseIniFile,
  Item_Add, Item_Plunder, PlayPickupSound, Radar_HandleReticleObject.
  * Item_Add maps the item onto an internal gun-type index (7..12, error "illegal gun
    type"), then stores it -- this is the real implementation behind the Body_AddItem /
    Body_AddItemInteger stubs on NOD_Body (sections 52/63).
  * PlayPickupSound selects one of SIX sound slots (object offsets +0x1498, +0x14b0,
    +0x14c8, +0x14e0, +0x14f8, +0x1510) with errors "illegal pickup type" / "illegal item
    class" -- six slots for the six item classes of section 74. So the pickup FEEDBACK
    is per class and already wired.
  * Item_Plunder (the Player_PlunderItems script command, id 16007) takes items from a
    wreck, same type mapping.
  * HeartBeat_MP_Ghost / _MP_Thing are the MP spectator/observer modes.

*** THE MP-SESSION FLAGS -- identified (refines section 72) ***
  In NOD_Player::Item_Add:
      eax = *0x661984                  ; the GAME object
      ecx = [eax+0x28] ; if != 0 -> do the extra block
      else ecx = [eax+0x520] ; if == 0 -> SKIP the extra block
  i.e. the extra work runs IF ([+0x28] != 0) OR ([+0x520] != 0). The cheat handlers
  (section 72) run only if BOTH are ZERO. The extra block builds names/handles and calls
  into the node/message helpers -- i.e. it is the NETWORK REPLICATION path.
  => [game+0x28] and [game+0x520] are the ANT CLIENT and SERVER SESSION pointers.
     Non-zero = multiplayer active. This explains BOTH observations consistently:
       - cheats are disabled online (both must be null),
       - item pickups replicate to the network only when a session exists.
  *** CONSEQUENCE FOR GAP 5b (item pickups in single player): in SP both pointers are
  null, so Item_Add simply SKIPS the replication branch and adds the item locally. There
  is NO single-player block anywhere in the pickup path. Together with section 61 (the
  boxes are ordinary RTI nodes, the campaign already uses the same item identifiers) and
  Aqua_Spawn::SpawnItemBox being a normal spawner, the evidence that MP pickups work in
  SP is now strong -- only the in-game test remains.

=== NOD_Gun (6) -- the ammunition system ===
  Ammo_CurrentAmmoType, Ammo_WouldHaveAmmo, GetAmmoClassForAmmoType, SpawnBullet,
  HeartBeat, ParseIniFile. So a gun resolves ammo TYPE -> ammo CLASS, checks availability
  before firing (Ammo_WouldHaveAmmo), then Aqua_Spawn::SpawnBullet emits the projectile.
  NOD_Gun_Part (4): TriggerPrimaryAttack, HandleEffects, ParseEffect, ParseIniFile --
  the per-barrel muzzle effects (the [child] gun parts of section 16).

=== NOD_Game (13) -- the PM_ mirror, confirmed ===
  HeartBeat, Input_HandleAIE, ANT_SetPlayer, ANT_GetPlayerDirectory, and the PM_ twins of
  the script commands: PM_LoadScript, PM_LoadServerScript, PM_CreateClientScript,
  PM_LoadTextTable, PM_LoadMusic, PM_PlayMusic, PM_MissionEnd, PM_EndGame.
  This is the direct evidence for the PM_ mirror claimed since section 15: every
  game-level script command has a PM_ counterpart that can be executed locally or
  replicated (section 78). Note PM_LoadServerScript / PM_CreateClientScript -- MP levels
  can run a SERVER script and a per-CLIENT script separately.

=== NOD_Body extras ===
  GetMass (mass DOES exist as a body property -- relevant to the still-unfound physics
  tick, section 73), ParseLCS (section 20), Key_ParseIniFile, PM_SetCargoKey.

MODDING TAKEAWAYS
  * Pickups: proceed with the section-61 test; the code path has no SP gate.
  * Weapons: to add a gun you need the item_d row (section 74), the gun .osd + bullet .osd
    (sections 16/17) and a matching ammo type -- NOD_Gun resolves type->class at runtime.
  * MP levels may carry separate server/client scripts (PM_LoadServerScript /
    PM_CreateClientScript) -- worth remembering when reviving mp_07 "Vulcano".
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
80. THE RENDERER (DSP_ / Aqua_Renderer / GFX_) -- textures, LOD levels, shaders
    (the last big exe subsystem; completes the HUD picture of section 75)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
=== DSP_Display (33 methods) -- the D3D abstraction ===
  Device/resolution: OpenBoard, OpenResolution (+ a typo'd twin "OpenResoultion"),
    QueryResolutions, Clear, Export.
  Geometry: VertexBuffer_Create/Destroy/Lock/Unlock, IndexBuffer_Create/Destroy/Lock/
    Unlock, DrawVertexArray.
  Textures: Texture_Create, Texture_CreateFromFile, Texture_LoadFromFile,
    Texture_GetTexture, Texture_Lock/Unlock, Texture_Refresh, Texture_FlushTextures,
    Texture_ReleaseTextureSurfaces, RequestTexture, IsTextureTypeSupported,
    SetTextureStageState.
  *** SHADERS: VertexShader_CreateFromFile / _Set / _Destroy and PixelShader_
    CreateFromMemory / _Set / _Destroy. So AN1 (2001!) already used vertex shaders
    loaded FROM FILES and pixel shaders built in memory -- gated by the dsp_pixelshader
    cvar (section 76). Vertex-shader files are a modding surface nobody has touched.

=== DSP_Texture (14) -- the texture pipeline, and what the _0/_1 suffixes mean ===
  LoadFromFile, SetLoadInfo, CollectInformation, DDS_CollectInformation, CheckTGA,
  GetNumOfMipLevels, GetTexelCount, GetTexelSize, GetTextureWidth/Height,
  TGA_AppendTextureLevelNameExtension, plus DSP_TextureCache: AllowFormat, AllowLevel,
  GetTextureLevel.
  *** TEXTURE LEVELS *** The engine has a per-texture QUALITY LEVEL system:
  TGA_AppendTextureLevelNameExtension appends a level suffix to the base name -- this is
  exactly the "_0" / "_1" you see on the shipped DDS files (pla_phobocaster_hi_0.dds).
  DSP_TextureCache::AllowLevel / GetTextureLevel decide which level is loaded, driven by
  dsp_texturecachesize / dsp_usemipmap / dsp_usecompressedtextures (section 76).
  Validation errors worth knowing when authoring textures: "unequal width and height"
  (textures must be SQUARE), "illegal texture width", "illegal dimension",
  "illegal texturelevel", "loadtexturelevel is not valid", and for the menu path
  "rgba8888 or rgba4444 texture format is required".
  => When replacing a texture: keep it SQUARE, keep the level suffix scheme, keep
  mipmaps and the DXT/RGBA format (this hardens the empirical rule of section 65 E).

=== GFX_PictureTGA (6) -- the TGA reader ===
  Open + ReadData_ARGB1555 / RGB565 / ARGB8888 / BGRA8888 / RGBA8888 -- five pixel
  formats, with "read data error (<format>)" diagnostics. So menu/HUD TGAs may be 16-bit
  (1555/565) or 32-bit; DDS is used for world textures.

=== Aqua_Renderer (8) -- the game-side renderer ===
  LoadTextures, Terrain_Triangulate, Terrain_SetMaterial, Plankton_Set,
  AddPermanentBody, Clear_SetColors, Cockpit_AllocateResources,
  Menu_InGame_AllocateResources.
  Terrain_Triangulate + Terrain_SetMaterial are the terrain build path (the ren_terrain_*
  cvars of section 76 switch bump mapping, dynamic light, dynamic shadow, single-pass and
  LOD there). Plankton_Set is the ambient particle field (ren_plankton_detail).

=== HUD TEXTURE PATHS (completes section 75) ===
  Right beside Aqua_Renderer::LoadTextures the exe names the radar art:
    vfx\hud\radar_box , vfx\hud\radar_clip , "%s\radar_color"
  So the radar's frame, clipping mask and colour ramp are ordinary textures in
  vfx\hud\ -- recolouring/reskinning the radar is a texture swap on top of the 24
  Radar_Color* config keys (section 75).

=== RESOURCE CACHING ===
  CON_Cache<T> with Allocate/Unlock and the errors "cache overflow" and
  "SetAlternativeExtension() - extension too long". The alternative-extension mechanism
  is how the engine picks .dds vs .tga (and the cache roots dat\animation\, vfx\tex\,
  sfx\sample\, vfx\msh\, vfx\col\ appear next to it).

MODDING TAKEAWAYS
  * Higher-res textures: possible, but keep SQUARE dimensions, mipmaps, the level suffix
    and a supported format; raise dsp_texturecachesize so they actually stay resident.
  * Visual upgrade on modern hardware: turn on ren_terrain_bumpmap, ren_terrain_dlight,
    ren_terrain_dshadow, ren_object_dlight, ren_objectprelit_bumpmap, ren_rays_enable and
    raise ren_plankton_detail / ren_plant_detail / ren_radar_detail; dsp_pixelshader,
    dsp_trilinear, dsp_anisotropyquality, dsp_fsaa (section 76). None of this needs code.
  * Radar reskin = swap vfx\hud\radar_box / radar_clip / radar_color + adjust the
    Radar_Color* keys.

STILL OPEN (low value): the vertex-shader file format/location; the exact D3D device
setup; per-frame draw ordering.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
81. THE TERRAIN SYSTEM + THE SHADER FILES -- how to build a NEW MAP
    (closes two gaps: terrain format, and the vertex-shader files of section 80)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
This is the biggest remaining creative unlock: AN1's terrain is authored from an ORDINARY
IMAGE plus a small .des, so new maps are within reach without any engine work.

=== THE PER-LEVEL TERRAIN FOLDER (map/<level>/terrain/) ===
  terrain.des          the terrain definition (see sections below)
  hmap.tga             *** THE HEIGHTMAP *** -- a 24-bit TGA
  material.des         material/texture-blend definition
  material_0/_1/_3.dds the material texture levels (the _N level scheme of section 80)
  material_s.tga       the material selector/splat map
  lightmap.dds         the PRE-COMPUTED terrain lightmap (this is why the shaders are
                       called "terrainprelit")
  radar_color.dds      the terrain colour ramp used by the radar (section 75/80)
  radar_height_fin.tga the radar height-fin texture
  ground textures      coral_01.tga, fungus_01.tga, grass_01.tga, stone_01.tga (per level)
  (390 terrain files across the game: 175 tga, 154 dds, 61 des; plus 29 map/<lvl>/fog/fog.fog)

=== THE HEIGHTFIELD RULES (from MRT_TileData's own error strings) ===
  * loaded via MRT_TileData::LoadFromTGA_24() or LoadFromBMP_24() -- a 24-BIT TGA or BMP.
    "Format of file for heightfield data ist not supported" [sic] otherwise.
  * "Size of heightfield data must be 2^n + 1"  -> 129, 257, 513, 1025 ...
  * "Width and height of map picture must be equal" -> SQUARE.
  * "Size of heightfield must be > 2".
  => You can literally PAINT a new terrain in an image editor as a square 2^n+1 greyscale
  24-bit TGA. That is the single most accessible content-creation path in the whole game.

=== terrain.des STRUCTURE (from MRT_Terrain::LoadTerrain's errors) ===
  Three required sections, each with its own "no <x> section in terrain file found" error:
    [Parameter]  requires  Scale  and  Offset  ("no scale/offset found in parameter
                 section of terrain file"), plus MaximumD2 and MinimumResolution
    [Grid]       the grid layout
    [Tile]       the tile list
  Texture-name rules: "texturename missing _ character" (names must contain '_', matching
  the coral_01 / grass_01 convention), "type of texturename has to be written in *.des
  (*.tga)", "Texturemap name can have maximal 63 character".
  MaximumD2 / MinimumResolution are the LOD controls: MRT_TileData::CreateD2 /
  PropagateD2 / StoreD2 compute the classic per-node "D2" geometric error metric used by
  ROAM/geo-mipmap terrain, and ren_terrain_lod (section 76) tunes how aggressively it is
  applied at runtime. Other build steps: SetHM (install heightmap), Generate,
  CreateTriangulation, CreateNormalMap (normals are DERIVED, not authored).

=== A NEW MAP, IN PRINCIPLE (all data) ===
  1. Paint hmap.tga (square, 2^n+1, 24-bit).
  2. Copy an existing level's terrain/ folder, replace hmap.tga, adjust terrain.des
     [Parameter] Scale/Offset (world size and depth) and the material/texture names.
  3. Provide the ground textures (square, mipmapped, section 80) and a lightmap
     (or reuse one -- it is prelit data, so a mismatched lightmap only looks wrong).
  4. Write the level .sco (sections 56-62) that calls Terrain_LoadTerrain (id 9003) and
     Game_LoadFog, then places objects.
  5. Register the level: instantfightlist~.des for an arena, or maplist~.des for MP
     (both cooked -- descook round-trips them, section 71).
  Not yet verified in game, but every required piece is now identified.

=== THE SHADER FILES (closes section 80's open question) ===
  The exe names 84 shader programs, loaded as FILES: *.vsa (vertex) and *.psa (pixel),
  each prefixed "FILE" in the string table, e.g. FILErep_terrainprelit_vs_ldlmcs.vsa,
  FILErep_object_vs_cmdmfm0..4.vsa, FILErep_radar_vs_anchor.vsa, FILErep_plant_ps_
  eoafmlm.psa. Families: rep_object / rep_objectprelit (ship rendering, 5 LOD variants
  cm0..cm4), rep_terrainprelit (13 variants), rep_radar (anchor/clear/eoa/fin/msh/msho --
  matching the radar anchors and fins of section 75), rep_plant, rep_billboar2, rep_halo,
  rep_hu2 (HUD), rep_movie, rep_overlay, rep_sprite, rep_standard (eoapsd1..4 / eoapss1..4
  = shadow/specular variants), rep_trail, rep_clear.
  The suffix letters encode the feature set: fm=fog, dm=diffuse, lm=lightmap, cm=colour,
  bm=bump, cs=colour-shift, mm/sm=material/specular maps, eoa=end-of-array//base.
  So the renderer picks a shader permutation per feature combination -- and the ren_*
  cvars (section 76) switch between them. Modding the shaders themselves is possible in
  principle (they are external files) but was not explored.

STILL OPEN: the byte layout inside terrain.des [Grid]/[Tile]; whether hmap.tga encodes
height in one channel or across RGB; the .vsa/.psa assembly format (DirectX 8 shader
assembly, most likely).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
82. THE APPLICATION FRAME -- technology stack, game states, video & audio middleware
    (includes a CORRECTION to section 48's audio-backend claim)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
=== THE TECHNOLOGY STACK (from the exe's DLL import table -- definitive) ===
    binkw32.dll   Bink Video (RAD Game Tools)      -> all cutscene playback
    mss32.dll     Miles Sound System (RAD)         -> ALL audio
    d3d8.dll      Direct3D 8                       -> renderer (section 80)
    DINPUT8.dll   DirectInput 8                    -> keyboard/mouse/joystick (section 72)
    WS2_32.dll    Winsock                          -> networking (section 78)
    WINMM, KERNEL32, USER32, GDI32, SHELL32, ole32  (system)
  That is the whole external dependency set: a 2001 D3D8 title with the two classic RAD
  middlewares. Useful for compatibility work (e.g. d3d8-to-d3d9/11 wrappers like dgVoodoo
  are the standard route to run this on modern hardware).

*** CORRECTION TO SECTION 48 (audio backend) ***
  Section 48 concluded "DirectMusic is the backend" from the DMUS_E_* error strings.
  That is WRONG. The exe imports mss32.dll and uses the Miles API extensively
  (AIL_allocate_sample_handle, AIL_allocate_3D_sample_handle, AIL_enumerate_3D_providers,
  AIL_close_3D_provider, AIL_3D_provider_attribute, AIL_3D_sample_status, AIL_end_sample,
  AIL_file_read / file_seek_callback, AIL_HWND, ...). DirectSound appears only as a Miles
  OUTPUT PROVIDER ("DirectSound_HWND"), and the DMUS_ enum is linked-in boilerplate.
  => AUDIO = MILES SOUND SYSTEM, with 3D providers enumerated at runtime (this is what
  the snd3d_provider / snd3d_speakertype cvars of section 76 select). The .sam descriptor
  + .mp3 model of section 48 is unaffected and still correct; only the "backend" label
  changes. Practical consequence: audio problems on modern systems are Miles/DirectSound
  provider issues, and snd3d_provider is the knob.

=== THE GAME STATE MACHINE (6 states) ===
    Aqua_State_Start                       startup (also holds IPToString for MP)
    Aqua_State_Menu                        the main menu (GetNameByType)
    Aqua_State_Menu_InGame                 the in-game menu (HeartBeat, StartGame)
    Aqua_State_SinglePlayer                SP session: StartGame, HeartBeat,
                                           EquipPlayerToEngine
    Aqua_State_SinglePlayer_InitializeSito  SP init sub-state
    Aqua_State_MultiPlayer                 MP session (ctor, Handle_Not_Initialised)
  EquipPlayerToEngine is the bridge from the STY_ inventory (section 77) to the live
  NOD_Player: it takes InventoryItemEquipped and fits it to the ship before the mission
  starts. So a savegame's equipped items become the in-mission loadout here.

=== THE MOVIE / CUTSCENE SYSTEM ===
  Playback is Bink: BinkOpen, BinkDoFrame, BinkNextFrame, BinkCopyToBuffer, BinkWait,
  BinkService, BinkClose, BinkDX8SurfaceType (renders into a D3D8 surface),
  BinkOpenMiles + BinkSetSoundSystem (audio routed through Miles).
  The movie TABLE is dat/sty/movie_d.des (STY_Base::Movie_LoadTable, section 77) with
  fields MovieName / MovieParameters / Movie_Handle; STY_Player tracks
  NumOfFinishedMovies + MovieKey so each cutscene is shown once (section 77).
  IMPORTANT: the .bik files themselves are NOT in the PAKs -- the only movie assets inside
  are movie_d.des plus the menu artwork (menu_options_movie01..07.tga, hud_movie.msh).
  The videos live as loose files in the game folder (originally on CD). So adding or
  replacing a cutscene = drop a .bik next to the others + a movie_d.des row + an option
  command that unlocks its MovieKey (section 77).

=== INPUT (completes section 72) ===
  DirectInput 8 with INP_Keyboard / INP_Mouse / INP_Joystick device classes and
  AQU_InputConfiguration parsing the control_*~.des binding files. This is the layer that
  turns a key into the action ids of the 108-entry dispatch table -- i.e. where the
  cheat/console bindings of section 72 take effect.

STILL OPEN (low value): the Bink version/parameters used; whether MovieParameters encodes
resolution or subtitle timing.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
83. THE FRAME-UPDATE ARCHITECTURE (HeartBeat) + the menu inventory
    (4th attempt at the physics tick -- honest result recorded)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Every actively-simulated thing in AN1 has a HeartBeat() method = its per-frame update.
46 are named in the exe's error strings; they map the whole runtime.

=== THE HEARTBEAT FAMILIES ===
  APPLICATION: Aqua_State_SinglePlayer::HeartBeat, Aqua_State_Menu_InGame::HeartBeat,
    Aqua_Network::HeartBeat, NOD_Game::HeartBeat, ANT_Client::HeartBeat.
  RULES (MP): ANT_Rules::HeartBeat_CheckFragLimit / _CheckTimeLimit /
    _CheckCaptureLimit / _DroppedFlags  (section 76 -- the limit checks run per frame).
  GAMEPLAY NODES: NOD_Player::HeartBeat (+ _MP_Ghost / _MP_Thing spectator variants),
    NOD_Gun::HeartBeat (cooldown/ammo), NOD_Torpedo::HeartBeat (guidance/lifetime),
    NOD_Camera::HeartBeat, NOD_WayPoint::HeartBeat (radius test -> the take triggers of
    section 56), NOD_ItemBox::HeartBeat, NOD_Device_Repair::HeartBeat (repair-over-time),
    NOD_FX_Rays::HeartBeat, NOD_FX_Spawn::HeartBeat.
  MENUS: 25 GUI_Menu_* screens, each with its own HeartBeat (see inventory below).

=== NOD_ItemBox::HeartBeat -- what an item box does every frame (disassembled) ===
  It reads the global frame time (0x66d100), then builds a rotation from three float
  triples at object offsets +0x68/+0x7c/+0x90 and +0xa0/+0xbc/+0xd8, calls the matrix
  helpers 0x500050 / 0x500130 / 0x5001f0 and applies the result via 0x4ca2c0, finally
  writing a position triple to +0x448 and setting the "moved" flag (or [esi+0xc], 2).
  => This is the TUMBLE ANIMATION: exactly the [tumble] block of section 63
  (amplitudea/b/c, phaseshifta/b/c, frequencya/b/c) -- three axes of amplitude, phase and
  frequency driving the lazy floating rotation of a pickup. Confirms that block's meaning
  from the code side. (The pickup DETECTION itself is not here; it happens in the
  collision path, and Item_Add (section 79) is the delivery.)

=== THE MENU INVENTORY (25 screens, useful for UI mods) ===
  Main, SinglePlayer, Multiplayer, Multiplayer_Choose, MultiplayerTest, StartServer,
  Network, Specify, Password, CDKey, Player, Load, Setup, System, Performance, Sound,
  Controls, Move, Look, Shoot, Camera, Joystick, GameOptions, Cinematics, Misc.
  Each is a GUI_Menu_<Name> class with its own HeartBeat; the artwork lives in
  vfx/menu/picture/*.tga and vfx/menu/mesh/*.msh (e.g. menu_options_movie01..07.tga,
  hud_movie.msh -- section 82). The Controls/Move/Look/Shoot screens are the front end to
  the control_*~.des bindings where the cheat/console actions of section 72 are set.
  Performance is the front end to the ren_*/dsp_* cvars of section 76.

=== THE PHYSICS TICK -- 4TH ATTEMPT, STILL NOT PINNED (honest) ===
  NOD_Body::HeartBeat and NOD_Vessel::HeartBeat do NOT appear among the 46 named
  HeartBeats -- those classes' per-frame updates contain no error strings, so they leave
  no name in the binary. They certainly exist (the vtable slot is called for every
  simulated node), but locating them needs a different approach than string xrefs:
  disassemble the simulation loop that walks the node list and calls vtable[+N] on each,
  then identify which slot is HeartBeat and follow NOD_Vessel's override.
  METHODS TRIED AND FAILED (do not repeat): searching for the [control] field names as
  exe strings (they live inside each .osd, not the exe -- section 73); assuming
  UTL_QuickIntegrator was the integrator (it is a device integrator -- section 76);
  looking for a named HeartBeat (this attempt).
  This remains the single stubborn gap. It is NOT needed for handling mods: the [control]
  data model (section 73) fully determines flight behaviour from the modder's side.

VALUE OF THIS SECTION: it gives the runtime skeleton -- what updates every frame, and
therefore where to look when something moves, ticks, expires or checks a limit. Combined
with the message dispatch (section 66) and the script thread model (section 70), the
engine's control flow is now documented end to end apart from the physics integration.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
84. THE MOT_ MOVEMENT MODELS -- the [control] parser found; CORRECTS section 73
    (analysis session; GOG Aqua.exe; the previously-"unfound" handling parser)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
This closes most of gap-list item 2 and CORRECTS a claim in section 73. Section 73 said
the [control] field names are NOT present as exe strings and are matched "during the
generic DES parse and stored at fixed object offsets". That is wrong: there is a
DEDICATED per-vehicle-class parser family, MOT_*::Parse, and the field names ARE in the
exe as plain strings. This is the parser the four earlier physics-tick attempts were
looking for (section 83 "still not pinned"); it was missed because the search was for the
lowercase cooked-OSD keys, while the exe holds them in CamelCase (MovePV, TurnPV, ...).

THE FIVE MOVEMENT CLASSES (each its own Parse, name string + address):
    MOT_Player::Parse   0x5e7f50..0x5e91a4   name "MOT_Player::Parse()"  @0x660ebc
                        (start corrected in section 85; 0x5e7b55 was a scan artefact)
    MOT_Vessel::Parse   0x5e91a4..0x5ea2c4   name @0x660f5c
    MOT_Vehicle::Parse  0x5ea2c4..0x5eb430   name @0x660f78
    MOT_Turn::Parse     (thin wrapper)       name @0x660f90
    MOT_OldOne::Parse   0x5eb430..0x5ec300   name @0x660fa4  (the EnTrOx/"Old One" boss)
Getter used for every field: 0x507800 (returns 0 on success, else the DES-ERROR path
0x5222c0 logs `(%s): Get "%s" in file "%s" failed`). So a missing key is non-fatal and
logged, same contract as everywhere else.

TWO FIELD VOCABULARIES -- and this is the important correction:
  * MOT_Player uses the LONG, expressive vocabulary (33 fields): a full second-order
    model with SEPARATE sensitivity / sign / stiffness / clip per axis --
      Alpha* (pitch):  AlphaPosVelocity AlphaNegVelocity AlphaSensitivity AlphaSign
                       AlphaStiffness
      Beta*  (yaw):    BetaPosVelocity BetaNegVelocity BetaSensitivity BetaClipPos
                       BetaClipNeg BetaSign BetaStiffness
      Gamma* (roll/thrust/strafe/turn coupling): GammaStrafeSensitivity GammaStrafeSign
                       GammaThrustSensitivity GammaThrustSign GammaTurnSensitivity
                       GammaTurnSign GammaStiffness
      StrafeH*/StrafeV* : PosVelocity NegVelocity Acceleration Deceleration (each)
      Thrust* : ThrustPosVelocity ThrustNegVelocity ThrustAcceleration ThrustDeceleration
      MoveFriction TurnFriction
    => the PLAYER ship has a genuinely richer flight model than the AI ships. This is why
    AquaNox's player handling feels hand-tuned: pitch/yaw have independent stiffness,
    sensitivity and asymmetric pos/neg rates, plus explicit accel AND decel on strafe and
    thrust. None of this exists for the AI classes.
  * MOT_Vessel / MOT_Vehicle / MOT_OldOne use the SHORT vocabulary (10-15 fields) --
    exactly the cooked-OSD [control] keys section 73 already documented:
      MovePV MoveNV MoveT  StrafeHorrVel StrafeHorrT  StrafeVertVel StrafeVertT
      TurnPV TurnNV TurnT TurnR  SwingT SwingA  MoveFriction TurnFriction
    Vehicle drops the strafe pair (ground/tracked movers); OldOne adds
    DamagePropagationRatio (boss hull damage spread). So section 73's [control] table is
    the MOT_Vessel field set -- correctly mapped there, just attributed to the wrong
    mechanism.

STORAGE (verified for MOT_Vessel): fields are written to fixed object offsets via
`lea eax,[edi+OFF]; push eax; push "FieldName"; call 0x507800`. Confirmed offsets:
    MovePV +0x304, StrafeVertVel +0x314, StrafeHorrT +0x318, TurnNV +0x324,
    TurnR +0x32c, SwingA +0x334  (adjacent pos/neg pairs occupy +0/+4 within a call).
So section 73's "stored at fixed object offsets" is TRUE -- but it is a NAME-KEYED read
into those offsets, not a blind positional parse. Practical upshot is unchanged: editing
the cooked-OSD floats is the way to tune handling, and descook round-trips them safely.

RUNTIME SIDE (partial): the per-frame integration that reads these offsets is a
first-order lerp -- the smoothed block at ~0x5e91b0 does `fld target; fsub current;
fdiv (targetmax-current); clamp[0..1]` against constants 0x5f336c / 0x5f3350 / 0x5f33a0,
i.e. eases current velocity toward the target at rate 1/MoveT and clamps. This CONFIRMS
section 73's "smoothed-velocity, first-order time-constant" reading from the code side.
The exact tick that calls it each frame (NOD_Vessel::HeartBeat) is still the one stubborn
unnamed method (section 83) -- but for MODDING it no longer matters: we now have the
parser, the field meanings, the offsets, and proof of the integration shape.

MODDING VALUE:
  * The section-73 recipe stands: clone a ship OSD, edit [control] floats, done -- now
    with the exe-side guarantee of what each field does and that a typo just logs+skips.
  * NEW capability: the PLAYER handling is separately tunable via the Alpha/Beta/Gamma
    fields (in the player-ship OSD's control block). Want a twitchier or heavier feel
    that AI ships can't express? Those fields are the lever. Lower AlphaStiffness /
    BetaStiffness for looser aim; raise Thrust*Acceleration for snappier throttle.
  * For "Nachtschicht" boss ships: MOT_OldOne + DamagePropagationRatio is the EnTrOx/
    Old-One movement+damage model -- relevant to mission 7's boss (design doc).

STILL TODO: dump the full MOT_Player offset table (33 fields) the same way if deep player
re-tuning is wanted; otherwise the data side is complete. The unnamed NOD_Vessel::
HeartBeat that drives the lerp remains the only open physics piece (section 83).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
85. THE PLAYER FLIGHT MODEL IN THE DATA -- pla_/gen_/hi_ OSD variants
    (follow-up to 84, verified against the real OSDs; CORRECTS section 73's table)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Section 84 found that MOT_Player parses a much richer field set than the AI classes.
This section confirms it on the DATA side and turns it into something directly usable --
plus it corrects a mislabelled row in section 73's balance table.

FIRST, A CORRECTION TO SECTION 84 ITSELF: the real start of MOT_Player::Parse is
0x5e7f50 (not the 0x5e7b55 estimated there -- that was a prologue-scan artefact). The
function reads each field into a STACK TEMP via getter 0x507800, then commits the group
to object offsets. Verified commit block at 0x5e80b9 (the Alpha group):
      AlphaPosVelocity  -> obj+0x358      AlphaSensitivity -> obj+0x360
      AlphaNegVelocity  -> obj+0x35c      AlphaStiffness   -> obj+0x364
      AlphaSign         -> NORMALISED to +/-1.0f at obj+0x368 (any negative value
                           becomes -1.0, anything else +1.0 -- so "sign" really is
                           just a direction flag, magnitude is ignored)
      DERIVED: 1.0 / (AlphaStiffness * [obj+0x370]) is precomputed at parse time into
               obj+0x378. The engine caches reciprocals so the per-frame lerp needs no
               division. (Same shape as the section-84 first-order lerp.)
The remaining groups follow the identical pattern into the 0x304..0x43c region; the
Gamma* fields are written DIRECTLY to obj+0x41c..0x43c without a stack temp. Only the
Alpha block above is offset-verified by eye -- the rest is structurally certain but the
individual offsets were not each confirmed, and are not needed for modding (see below).

*** THE KEY DATA FINDING: EVERY SHIP HAS UP TO THREE OSD VARIANTS ***
Same hull, three definitions, and they carry DIFFERENT movement models:
    pla_<ship>~.osd   the PLAYER-flyable definition -- contains BOTH vocabularies:
                      the short [control] set AND the rich alpha*/beta*/gamma*/thrust*
                      set that only MOT_Player reads.
    gen_<ship>~.osd   the AI/generic definition -- ONLY the short set.
    hi_<ship>~.osd    a "high-end" tuned variant (faster; see table below).
Verified on gen_phobocaster (10205 B, no alpha/beta/gamma keys at all) vs
pla_phobocaster (9542 B, all 7 gamma + 7 beta + 5 alpha keys present).
=> The pla_ prefix is the marker for "player-flyable". There are exactly NINE:
   pla_avenger, pla_drowsymaggie, pla_phobocaster, pla_skipjack, pla_succubus2,
   pla_tiburon, pla_toiler, pla_voodoobreath, pla_vortex.

*** CORRECTION TO SECTION 73's BALANCE TABLE ***
Section 73 lists "Phobocaster(pl) movepv 43.75 movenv 21.875 movet 1.5 turnpv 90".
Those are the gen_ (AI) numbers, not the player's. Measured:
    pla_phobocaster  movepv 41.25  movenv 35.0    movet 1.2  turnpv 110
    gen_phobocaster  movepv 43.75  movenv 21.875  movet 1.5  turnpv 90   <- sec.73 row
    hi_phobocaster   movepv 60.0   movenv 21.875  movet 1.5  turnpv 90
A modder following section 73 to "make the player ship faster" would edit the AI variant
and see no change in the cockpit. Edit pla_* for player handling, gen_* for how the AI
flies the same hull.

*** AND THE REAL SURPRISE: THE SHORT BLOCK IS IDENTICAL ON ALL NINE PLAYER SHIPS ***
    ship              movepv movenv movet turnpv | alphastiff betasens thrustaccel
    pla_phobocaster   41.25  35.0   1.2   110    | 7.0        0.4      25
    pla_skipjack      41.25  35.0   1.2   110    | 6.0        0.4      80
    pla_succubus2     41.25  35.0   1.2   110    | 6.5        0.4      24
    pla_voodoobreath  41.25  35.0   1.2   110    | 6.5        0.4      12
    pla_vortex        41.25  35.0   1.2   110    | 6.0        0.4      13
    pla_avenger       41.25  35.0   1.2   110    | 5.5        0.4      13
    pla_tiburon       41.25  35.0   1.2   110    | 5.0        0.4      9
    pla_toiler        41.25  35.0   1.2   110    | 5.0        0.4      8
    pla_drowsymaggie  41.25  35.0   1.2   110    | 5.0        0.4      7
The whole short [control] block -- top speed, reverse, time constant, turn rate -- is the
SAME on every player ship. All differentiation between player ships comes from the rich
model: AlphaStiffness 5.0..7.0 (how crisply the nose responds) and above all
ThrustAcceleration 7..80 (Skipjack 80 = instant throttle; Drowsy Maggie 7 = a barge).
So section 73's advice "lower movet / raise turnpv to make a ship nimble" is correct for
AI SHIPS ONLY. For the player it does nothing to differentiate -- the levers are
ThrustAcceleration / ThrustDeceleration / AlphaStiffness / BetaStiffness.

REFERENCE -- pla_phobocaster's full rich model (the player baseline):
    alphaposvelocity 1.0   alphanegvelocity 1.0   alphasensitivity 0.4
    alphasign 1.0          alphastiffness 7.0
    betaposvelocity 1.0    betanegvelocity 1.0    betasensitivity 0.4
    betaclippos 85.0       betaclipneg -85.0      betasign -1.0  betastiffness 7.0
    gammastrafesensitivity 3.0  gammathrustsensitivity 0.2
    gammaturnsensitivity 0.2    gammastiffness 3.0
    thrustposvelocity 50.0 thrustnegvelocity -35.0
    thrustacceleration 25.0 thrustdeceleration 50.0
Reading: alpha = pitch, beta = yaw (clipped to +/-85 deg, sign inverted -> that is the
"inverted look" default), gamma = the roll/lean coupling when strafing or turning.
*sensitivity = input scaling, *stiffness = how fast it settles (higher = crisper).

MODDING RECIPE (player handling, previously not possible to target):
  1. Take vfx/osd/pla_<ship>~.osd from pak2 (1262 OSDs live there; pak0/1/3/4 have none).
  2. The fields are cooked "name\0" + little-endian float32 -- read/write in place, or
     use descook (round-trips byte-identical).
  3. Snappier feel: raise thrustacceleration and alphastiffness/betastiffness.
     Heavier feel: lower them. Motion-sickness-free: raise betaclip* limits.
     Non-inverted pitch: flip betasign from -1.0 to +1.0.
  4. Ship into a pak that loads after pak2 (the mod pak6 pattern, section 9).

STILL TODO: confirm in game that editing pla_phobocaster's thrustacceleration is felt
(cheapest possible test of this whole section); check what selects hi_ over gen_ at
spawn time (a difficulty or campaign-stage switch is the obvious guess, unverified).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
86. THE MUI_ MENU SYSTEM -- the 3D station UI, and it ships as PLAIN TEXT
    (gap: MUI_ was 12 methods with zero handoff coverage; extends section 83's menu list)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
MUI_ was in the never-analysed list. It turns out to be a SECOND, separate menu system --
and its definition file is the most editable file in the whole game.

*** THERE ARE TWO MENU LAYERS, DO NOT CONFUSE THEM ***
  (a) dat/menu/*~.des  -- COOKED. The FRONTEND menus (Main, SinglePlayer, Multiplayer,
      Setup, Controls...). Driven by the 25 GUI_Menu_* classes of section 83. This is
      where mainmenu~.des, maplist~.des (section 66/60) and control_*~.des (section 72)
      live. Keys: numofitems, heightoftextitem, primarytextdata0 (a text key into
      text~.des), plus seven animation blocks per item (eventopen/eventclose/evententer/
      eventleave/eventbutton{next,prev,release}) each carrying translation, rotation,
      emission, opacity, scale, duration as raw float bytes.
  (b) vfx/menu/menu.des -- UNCOOKED PLAIN TEXT, 150,030 bytes, 7013 lines, 1187 comment
      lines (German + English developer comments!). This is the IN-GAME STATION UI --
      the 3D interface with the visor, rotating circles, shop, equip, dock and dialogue
      screens. Driven by the MUI_* classes. 405 items.
  => Section 83's "the artwork lives in vfx/menu/picture/*.tga and vfx/menu/mesh/*.msh"
     was right about assets but missed that the whole SCENE is described in (b).

WHY IT IS PLAIN TEXT -- and why that matters generally: the exe exports
`DES_File::ParseUncompiled` (@0x639684, alongside `DES_File::Open` @0x639618). The engine
can parse UNCOOKED DES at runtime. menu.des ships uncooked in pak2 and is proof it works.
*** CONSEQUENCE FOR ALL MODDING: cooked ~.des files are an optimisation, not a
requirement. Where an uncooked equivalent is accepted, a mod can ship READABLE text
instead of byte-patching cooked structures. Worth testing for other DES files -- it would
remove the whole descook round-trip risk. (Untested for dat/menu/*; menu.des is the one
confirmed case.) ***
Loading: path is composed by sprintf("%s%s", <dir>, "menu.des") at 0x4115a5 (string
"menu.des" @0x604544), then handed to the DES loader 0x506640.

FILE STRUCTURE of vfx/menu/menu.des:
  [MENU]
    [MOTION]   AlphaSensitivity/AlphaStiffness/AlphaSign, Beta*, Gamma*  (all 0.0/1.0
               in the shipped file)  -- see the architectural note below
    [FONT]     NumOfFont, Font0="font8.tga"
    [COLOR]    Diffuse0..7 as r,g,b floats. Shipped palette: 0 pure white, 1 tint
               (0.5,1,0), 2 frame green (0,1,0.09), 3 frame yellow (1,0.89,0), 4-7 white.
               Items reference these by INDEX (Diffuse = 1), so recolouring the entire
               station UI = editing four lines here.
    [GEOMETRY] named coordinate systems for the menu meshes: Visor_Translation/_Scale,
               Circle01_Translation/_Rotation/_Scale, Circle02_*, ... (the floating
               rings of the station interface, incl. a per-second rotation comment)
    [Item0]..[Item404]

*** ARCHITECTURAL FINDING: THE MENU IS A VEHICLE. *** [MOTION] uses exactly the
AlphaSensitivity / AlphaStiffness / AlphaSign / Beta* / Gamma* vocabulary that
MOT_Player::Parse reads for the PLAYER SHIP (section 84/85). The same first-order
smoothed-motion model that flies the Phobocaster also makes the station menu sway under
the cursor. The shipped values are 0.0 (sway disabled) -- so raising AlphaStiffness /
BetaSensitivity here is an untried, one-line way to give the station UI motion.

ITEM TYPES -- from MUI_Menu::AllocateItem @0x55d430 (switch on type, `cmp esi,7; ja`,
jump table @0x55d570; allocator 0x4279e0):
    type 0  base MUI_Item     0xc0 B   vtable 0x5fa41c      (199 items -- plain meshes)
    type 1  base MUI_Item     0xc0 B   same                 (12)
    type 2  MUI_Item_TEXT     0x388 B  ctor 0x55fb90        (130 -- the big one)
    type 3  MUI_Item_BAR      0xe0 B   ctor 0x560620        (0 used -- gauges/sliders)
    type 4  base MUI_Item     0xc0 B   inline init          (5)
    type 5  MUI_Item_PICTURE  0xc0 B   ctor 0x560aa0        (57)
    type 6  MUI_Item_TURN     0xc4 B   ctor 0x560bc0        (0 used)
    type 7  MUI_Item_OBJECT   0xf0 B   ctor 0x55e7c0        (2)
  Types 3 and 6 exist in code but are UNUSED in the shipped menu -- a BAR (gauge) and a
  TURN (rotating element) widget are available for free to a UI mod.
KEYS PER TYPE (from the Parse functions):
    MUI_Menu::Parse         FONT, NumOfFont, Font<n>, COLOR, Diffuse0..2(+)
    MUI_Item::Parse         Name, Material, Diffuse, Opacity   (+ Mesh, Type)
    MUI_Item_TEXT::Parse    Data, Font, Position, Width, Height, Alignment
    MUI_Item_BAR::Parse     Data, Position, Width, Height
    MUI_Item_OBJECT::Parse  Data, Position1, Position2
  Example (real, from the file):
    [Item0] { Type=0  Mesh="vfx\msh\menu\menu_main.msh"  Name="MAI_BACK_TINT"
              Material=0  Diffuse=1  Opacity=1.0 }        // material is MULTIPLICATIVE
    [Item] { Type=2 Mesh="...menu_shop.msh" Name="SHO_ITEM_DETAIL_NAME" Material=5
             Diffuse=0 Opacity=0.99 Font=0 Position=68.0,90.0 Width=224.0 Height=22.0
             Alignment="right" }

*** THE Name FIELD IS THE BINDING KEY *** -- this is how game logic reaches presentation.
The engine looks an item up by name and fills/toggles it (e.g. the shop code writes the
selected item's name into "SHO_ITEM_DETAIL_NAME"). The prefix identifies the screen; all
405 names are distinct. Screen inventory by prefix:
    MAI 45 station main      EQU 51 equip        EQI 30 equip item     SHO 30 shop
    SHI 29 ship              DOC 21 dock         GAL 35 galaxy/map     LEV 35 level
    MOV 36 movie/briefing    TK1 35 / TK2 19 dialogue     ACK 20 acknowledge
    OPT 16 options           SAB 3
  MAI_ ties to the MAI_Handler class (10 HeartBeats, section 83) and the STY_/MAI_ story
  layer of section 77 -- so section 77's station menu and this file are the same UI seen
  from the data and code sides.
  31 unique meshes under vfx/msh/menu/ (menu_main.msh, menu_shop.msh, menu_equip.msh,
  menu_dock.msh, menu_acknowledge.msh, menu_main_button_01..05, menu_main_button2_01..05,
  menu_equip_circle.msh, menu_equip_item.msh, ...).

MODDING VALUE (high, and cheap):
  * The entire in-game station UI is retextured/repositioned/recoloured by editing ONE
    READABLE TEXT FILE. No cooking, no descook, no byte offsets. Change Diffuse indices
    for a different colour scheme; change Position/Width/Height to move text; swap Mesh
    paths for new geometry.
  * Two unused widget types (BAR, TURN) are available for new UI elements.
  * [MOTION] can give the station UI physical sway (shipped disabled).
  * Renaming a Name key BREAKS the binding -- the logic looks items up by that string.
    Never rename; add or restyle instead.
  * Caution from the file's own comment: Material is MULTIPLICATIVE, so Opacity/Diffuse
    interact; test visually.

STILL TODO: find the code that resolves a Name string to an item (that gives the full
list of names the logic expects, i.e. which items are mandatory); check whether the
FRONTEND dat/menu/*~.des also accept an uncooked form (would remove all cooked-DES risk);
try a BAR (type 3) item to confirm the unused widgets still work.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
87. THE GUI_ FRONTEND MENU SYSTEM -- menuinterface.des is the root; 37 FIXED screens
    (gap: GUI_ had 61 methods and ~6 handoff mentions; completes section 86's layer (a))
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Section 86 split the UI into the cooked FRONTEND (GUI_) and the plaintext in-game station
UI (MUI_). This section does the frontend properly, and answers the question section 83
left open: what actually drives the main menu, and how far can a mod change it.

*** ONLY FOUR MENU PATHS ARE HARDCODED IN THE EXE ***
    dat\menu\menuinterface.des   @0x65c3bc  ref 0x58676b   <- THE ROOT
    dat\menu\maplist.des         @0x65c384  ref 0x587a8d   (section 66)
    dat\menu\instantfightlist.des@0x65c39c  ref 0x587a39   (section 66)
    dat\menu\backgroundpic.des   @0x608368  ref 0x41f63a
Everything else -- mainmenu, controls, sound, setup, ... -- is NOT referenced by path in
the exe. They are listed inside menuinterface.des. That is why grepping the exe for
"mainmenu" finds nothing and why section 83 could only see the classes, not the wiring.

CONTENTS OF dat/menu/menuinterface~.des (cooked, 2520 B; descook round-trips it):
    [HEAD][menu_interface]
        menutextdata = dat\menu\text.des        <- the shared string table
        numofmenus   = 37
        menuname0..36 = dat\menu\<screen>.des
        menutype0..36 = (empty) or 1
  The 37 screens in order (index is meaningful -- see the warning below):
     0 mainmenu           1 singleplayer      2 multiplayer       3 setup
     4 specify            5 controls          6 system            7 cdkey
     8 performance        9 sound            10 network          11 serverlist*
    12 playerlist*       13 infolist*        14 serverlist_headline
    15 playerlist_headline 16 infolist_headline 17 multiplayer_headline
    18 multiplayer_choose 19 startserver     20 player           21 gameoptions
    22 look              23 move             24 weight           25 misc
    26 camera            27 shoot            28 taunts           29 teammessages
    30 furthermove       31 instantfight     32 load             33 password
    34 showmovement      35 cinematics       36 joystick
  (* = menutype 1. Only serverlist/playerlist/infolist carry type 1 = the scrollable
  LIST widget, i.e. the GUI_TextMenu_List class. All others are type 0 = plain screen.)
  Note dat\menu\weight.des and showmovement.des/taunts.des/teammessages.des exist as
  screens -- several are MP-only or unreachable in the shipped UI; worth a look for
  cut UI content in the spirit of sections 44-46/60.

RUNTIME WIRING (verified):
    GUI_MenuInterface holds the screens in an array at THIS+0x3a4.
    GetMenu(idx)   = 0x58d510 :  return [this + idx*4 + 0x3a4]      (thiscall, 1 arg)
    Dispatch       = 0x59ed90 :  5-way jump table @0x59f098 (event 0..4), `ja` -> ignore
    So the ubiquitous idiom `push <event>; ...; call 0x58d510; mov ecx,eax; call 0x59ed90`
    reads as: GetMenu(i)->Dispatch(event, param).
    GUI_Menu_Main::HeartBeat = 0x5749d0 (name string @0x65bc10). It receives an input code
    and branches on it: 0x1b = ESC, 0x6c = 'l', 0x71 = 'q'. On ESC it first tests the
    selected item index (`cmp esi,4`, item 4 = Quit) -- i.e. the CURRENT SELECTION is held
    at menuinterface+0x20 and the screens react to raw key codes, not to abstract actions.

*** THE HARD LIMIT -- AND IT IS A REAL ONE (unlike maplist) ***
    0x58d520 is the screen-switch function. It does `cmp ebx,0x24` (36) / `ja` -> ignore,
    then `jmp [ebx*4 + 0x58d6c0]` into a HARDCODED 37-ENTRY JUMP TABLE, and every case
    writes a DIFFERENT fixed field of the interface object (+0x26c, +0x274, +0x27c,
    +0x29c, +0x2a4, +0x2ac, +0x2b4, +0x2c4, +0x2d4, ...).
    CONSEQUENCES for modding, and they are strict:
      * The screen COUNT is fixed at 37. Raising numofmenus does NOT create a usable
        screen -- index 37 fails the bounds check and is silently ignored. This is the
        OPPOSITE of maplist/instantfightlist (section 66), which have no cap. Do not
        assume the "bump the count" trick generalises; it worked there because the loader
        allocates from the count. Here the count is data but the DISPATCH is code.
      * The screen ORDER must not change. Index N is welded to a specific behaviour in
        the jump table. Swapping menuname5 and menuname6 does not swap two screens, it
        points the Controls behaviour at the System layout.
      * What IS free: editing each individual screen's own .des -- item text keys,
        positions, the seven animation blocks, fonts, colours (section 86 layer (a)).
        And replacing a screen's file wholesale, as long as it keeps its index.

WHAT THIS MEANS FOR "CAN WE EDIT THE MAIN MENU" (refines the earlier answer):
    YES for appearance and wording: mainmenu~.des is index 0; its five items carry
    primarytextdata0 text keys into dat\menu\text.des, plus evententer/eventleave
    animation blocks (emission/scale/translation/opacity/duration) -- restyling the
    highlight or renaming entries is pure data.
    NO for new functional entries: the action behind an item is the key-code branching
    inside that screen's GUI_Menu_* HeartBeat, and screens are index-welded to code.
    An added item can only reuse an action that already exists.

MODDING RECIPES:
    * Rename/restyle menu entries: edit dat\menu\text.des (shared string table, listed as
      menutextdata) or the item's primarytextdata0 key; recook, ship in the mod pak.
    * Reskin the frontend: dat\menu\backgroundpic.des is the only other hardcoded path --
      the background art hook.
    * Hidden screens: 31 instantfight, 34 showmovement, 28 taunts, 29 teammessages,
      24 weight are candidates for UI content that is present but hard to reach.

STILL TODO: dump the 37-entry jump table at 0x58d6c0 and map each index to the field it
sets (that yields the definitive index->behaviour table); check whether menutype has
values beyond 0/1; test whether an uncooked menuinterface.des is accepted (section 86's
ParseUncompiled question -- this file would be the cleanest place to try it).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
88. THE AQU_ INPUT SYSTEM -- the complete action + key-name vocabulary
    (gap: AQU_ had 9 methods and no coverage; CONFIRMS and completes section 72)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Section 72 solved the cheats by finding they are bindable input actions, and listed the
cheat names from the parser side. This section does the AQU_ layer properly and yields
the two things a modder actually needs to write a binding by hand: the FULL action
vocabulary and the EXACT key-name spellings. It also independently re-verifies section 72
-- the cheat action names really are in the exe (0x65cb98..0x65cc18), so section 72 is
CONFIRMED, not corrected.

THE CLASS: AQU_InputConfiguration (+ AQU_Input). Methods with error strings, all verified:
    Parse            @ref 0x403eca/0x403f04   reads the config DES
    GetAction        @ref 0x4047c0 area       name -> action id
    HandleKeyboard   err @0x603838  "illegal keyboard modifier"
    HandleMouse      err @0x6037f8  "illegal Mouse source"
    HandleJoystick   err @0x6036b8  "illegal joystick source"
    GetKeyCode       err @0x6038b8  "illegal key code"
    GetStringByKeyCode err @0x603b88 (the reverse map, used to PRINT a binding in the
                                      controls menu)
    AQU_Input::CollectMouse @0x6034c8
All five error paths are non-fatal logs -- a bad name/key in the config is skipped, not a
crash. Same forgiving contract as the DES getters elsewhere.

DEVICE/AXIS KEYS read by AQU_InputConfiguration::Parse:
    JoyStick, Mouse, DeadZone, Scale,
    AxisX AxisY AxisZ RotationX RotationY RotationZ Slider0 Slider1
  These are the [joystick]/[mouse] blocks at the top of control_*~.des. Shipped
  control_sp values: deadzone axisx/y 0.2, axisz 0.1, rot 0.1-0.2; scale axisx/y -5.433
  (note the NEGATIVE scale = axis inversion is done here, not by a separate invert flag),
  mouse scale axisx -0.3222 / axisy +0.3222.
  Joystick POV names also exist: povforward, povbackward, povleft, povright, button<n>
  (button0..button15).

*** THE FULL ACTION VOCABULARY (89 names) ***
The exe holds them as two plain string clusters:
    0x602e98..0x6034c0   the 83 standard actions (stored in reverse order)
    0x65cb98..0x65cc18   the 6 cheat actions (next to the joystick axis names)
Groups: move_* (4), strafe_* (6), look_* (6), weapon_* (18), camera_* (7), menu_* (12),
voc_taunt_* (15), voc_team_* (13), cheat_* (6), plus inp_joystickenable.

*** 19 ACTIONS EXIST BUT ARE UNBOUND IN THE SHIPPED PROFILE ***
control_sp~.des binds 70 of the 89 (numofcommand = 70). The 19 that ship unbound:
    cheat_indestructable            cheat_invisible
    cheat_terminatetarget           cheat_mission_failed
    cheat_mission_completed_auto    cheat_mission_completed_manual
        -> section 72's six, independently confirmed here from the name table.
    menu_togglestatistics   <- an in-game STATISTICS overlay, never bound. Cheap win:
                               bind it like the console (section 72) and see what it shows.
    weapon_launchtorpedo    <- distinct from the bound weapon_torpedolauncher (select);
                               this is the FIRE action. Possibly a leftover, worth a test.
    look_up look_down look_left look_right  <- DISCRETE look actions. Shipped profile only
                               binds the AXIS forms look_updown/look_leftright, so keyboard
                               head-look is available but unbound.
    menu_up menu_down menu_left menu_right menu_enter menu_escape menu_delete
                            <- menu navigation, bound in the menu profile, not in _sp.

*** EXACT KEY-NAME SPELLINGS (needed to hand-write a binding) ***
From the GetKeyCode table @0x603870..0x603af8, in the engine's own spelling -- note it is
GERMAN-influenced, "strg" not "ctrl":
  modifiers: plain, lshift, rshift, lstrg, rstrg, lalt, ralt, lcontrol, rcontrol,
             lmenu, rmenu
  letters/digits are their own characters; named keys:
    esc tab back return space capital scroll insert delete home end prior next
    up down left right
    f1..f15
    numpad0..numpad9 numpadcomma numpadenter numpadequals decimal divide multiply
    add subtract
    minus equals lbracket rbracket backslash semicolon apostrophe grave comma period
    slash colon underline oem_102
  ("prior"/"next" = PageUp/PageDown; "capital" = CapsLock; "oem_102" = the extra key on
  ISO keyboards, i.e. the German "<>|" key.)

BINDING RECIPE (completes section 72's recipe with verified spellings):
  In dat/menu/control_sp~.des, [user] holds numofcommand = 70 and [command0..69], each:
      action = "<name from the 89>"
      numofevent = N
      [event0] { device = "keyboard" | "mouse" | "joystick"
                 source = "<key name above>"
                 modifier = "plain" | "lshift" | "lstrg" | ... }
  To add god mode: raise numofcommand to 71, append [command70] with
  action="cheat_indestructable", numofevent=1, [event0]{device="keyboard",
  source="f11", modifier="plain"}. Recook with descook (round-trips byte-identical) and
  ship in the mod pak. Unlike the menu array (section 87), numofcommand has no hardcoded
  cap -- it is a plain count-driven loop like maplist (section 66).

STILL TODO: bind menu_togglestatistics and see what the statistics overlay contains (it
may be a dev/debug HUD -- potentially useful for testing waves and spawns); test whether
weapon_launchtorpedo does anything distinct from weapon_torpedolauncher; confirm the 89
count against the parser's own strcmp list (section 72 counted 176 recognised names, so
the parser likely also accepts CVar-style or menu-internal names beyond these 89).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
89. THE REP_ RENDER PASSES -- all 84 SHADERS RECOVERED AS SOURCE from the exe
    (gap: REP_/REB_ had 12 methods, no coverage; CORRECTS section 81's shader claim)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
*** CORRECTION TO SECTION 81. *** Section 81 recorded "84 shader files .vsa/.psa".
There are no such FILES: none of the seven PAKs contains a single .vsa or .psa. The 84
shaders are COMPILED INTO Aqua.exe as D3D8 token streams. What section 81 saw were the
source filenames preserved inside the shaders' own debug comments. The count of 84 is
exactly right -- the location was not.

AND THAT IS GOOD NEWS, because D3D8 keeps debug info in D3DSIO_COMMENT blocks: every
embedded shader carries a `FILE<name>` and `LINE<n>` token block naming its original
source file. So the shaders are RECOVERABLE. This session wrote a small D3D8
disassembler and extracted all 84 with their original names.

  *** DELIVERABLE: aquanox1_shaders.txt -- all 84 shaders disassembled, 1576
      instructions total, each headed by its source filename and exe address. ***
  Regenerate with: scan the mapped image for version tokens 0xFFFE0100 (vs.1.0),
  0xFFFF0101/0xFFFF0104 (ps.1.1/1.4); walk tokens; 0x0000FFFF ends a shader; a token with
  low word 0xFFFE is a COMMENT whose payload starts "FILE"/"LINE". Dest token: writemask
  bits 16-19; source token: swizzle bits 16-23 (0xE4 = .xyzw identity), bit 13 = negate;
  register type = bits 28-30 (0 r, 1 v, 2 c, 3 t, 4 oPos, 5 oD, 6 oT).

INVENTORY -- 79 vertex shaders (all vs.1.0) + 5 pixel shaders (ps.1.1, one ps.1.4):
    family              n   max instr   what it draws
    rep_object         20      62       lit scene objects (ships, stations)
    rep_objectprelit   15      54       objects with baked lightmaps
    rep_terrainprelit  14      11       the seabed (+ all 5 pixel shaders)
    rep_standard        9      86       the general/heaviest path
    rep_clear           6       7       clear/fill passes
    rep_radar           6       8       the radar
    rep_hu2             5       5       the HUD (see section 75)
    rep_plant           3      12       plants/kelp
    rep_sprite 1(26), rep_halo 1(8), rep_trail 1(6), rep_movie 1(6),
    rep_overlay 1(6), rep_billboar2 1(6)
  The suffix encodes the pass combination, and it is a consistent vocabulary:
    cm colour map | dm detail map | fm fog map | lm light map | bm bump map |
    em environment map | wm water map | sm/mm multi/mask map | cs colour shift |
    ld light dynamic | eoa emissive/overlay/alpha | pfm projected fog map |
    rm radar map | pss point-source-shading
  e.g. rep_object_vs_cmdmfm0..4 = colour+detail+fog, five variants (0..4 = light count).
  The four REP_ classes in the exe -- REP_Object_VS, REP_ObjectPrelit_VS, REP_Hud_VS,
  REP_TerrainPrelit -- match the four big families; their methods are named after the
  same suffixes (REP_Object_VS::RenderStatic_CMDM @0x634c14, _CM @0x634c38, _CMDMFM
  @0x634c58, REP_ObjectPrelit_VS::RenderStatic_LMCS/_LMCSA, REP_Hud_VS::RenderStatic_EOA).

THE LIGHTING MODEL (from rep_standard_vs_eoapss4.vsa, the 86-instruction giant):
  Classic per-vertex Blinn-Phong with FOUR point lights, one identical 20-instruction
  block per light. Constant register convention, consistent across the object shaders:
      c0, c1      clamp constants (0.0 / 1.0) -- c0 is the most-used register overall
      c2          eye/camera position          c3..c6   world-view-projection matrix
      c50         material/ambient base        c51      ambient colour accumulator
      c52         specular power
      c53, c54, c55  hemisphere/sky-ground terms (dp3 against normal v1)
      per light i (0..3):  position c59/c63/c67/c71, attenuation c58/c62/c66/c70,
                           diffuse c56/c60/c64/c68, specular c57/c61/c65/c69
      inputs: v0 position, v1 normal, v2 vertex colour, v3 texcoord
      outputs: oPos, oD0 diffuse, oD1 specular, oT0 texcoord
  Per light: normalise L, half-vector H, `dst`+`dp3` attenuation, `lit` for the
  diffuse/specular pair, then MAD into the accumulators. The final line
  `mad oD0, r0, v2, c50` multiplies the accumulated light by the VERTEX COLOUR -- which
  is why vertex-painted meshes tint correctly, and why section 80's texture work and this
  agree.

WHAT THIS MEANS FOR MODDING (be realistic):
  * The shaders CANNOT be replaced by dropping .vsa files -- they are in the exe. Section
    81's implied "84 shader files you could edit" is not achievable without binary
    patching. Editing one means assembling D3D8 tokens and patching them in place; the
    token stream is fixed-length per instruction, so a same-length edit (e.g. swapping a
    constant register index, changing a swizzle, or turning a `mul` into a `mad`) is
    feasible; anything longer is not, without relocating the block.
  * What IS freely tunable is the DATA the constants come from: light colours/positions
    are level data (section 6/aquanox1_levels.json world settings: ambient_light, fog),
    and the CVars ren_* / dsp_* (section 76) switch whole passes on and off -- that is the
    supported way to change how the game looks.
  * The disassembly is still worth having: it documents exactly what each ren_* CVar
    turns on, and it tells you which visual effects exist at all (e.g. there IS a
    silhouette pass, rep_object_vs_silhouette, and an environment-map path _fmem).
  * REB_Mesh::Reconstruct (@errs 0x63c7d4/0x63c804) + REB_TextureCache::RegisterTextureName
    (0x63c740) are the mesh/texture upload side; REB_Map::SetMapToRenderState(RenderMode)
    (0x64497c) is what binds a texture stage per pass. These are the hooks a texture mod
    goes through, but they need no configuration -- they follow the OSD material data.

STILL TODO: map each ren_* CVar (section 76) to the pass/family it enables, by finding
which CVar each RenderStatic_* method tests -- that would turn the CVar list into a
"what does this switch actually do" table; check whether the ps.1.4 shader (only one,
0x56ef58 region) is a fallback or a higher-quality path.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
90. MOST DES FILES SHIP AS PLAIN TEXT -- 170 of 274, fully commented
    (follows from section 86's ParseUncompiled; substantially de-risks sections 71/81)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Section 86 found the engine exports DES_File::ParseUncompiled and that vfx/menu/menu.des
ships uncooked, and asked whether that generalises. It does, far more than expected.

*** CENSUS ACROSS ALL SEVEN PAKS: 274 .des files -- 104 cooked (~.des), 170 PLAIN TEXT. ***
62% of the game's configuration ships as readable, commented text. The handoff has been
treating cooked-DES byte-patching as the normal case; for most of what a modder wants to
touch, it is not needed at all.

WHAT IS PLAIN TEXT (the important ones):
    dat/sty/*_d.des            63 files -- the ENTIRE story/take layer (section 77):
                               mtake_<level>_d.des for every mission, plus briefing_d,
                               mission_d, gun_d, item_d, cargo_d, mood_d, movie_d,
                               difficulty_d, acknowledge_d, mp_gun_d, inst_*_d ...
    map/<level>/fog/fog.des     every level's fog -- 30+ files
    map/<level>/terrain/terrain.des + material.des   every level's terrain spec
    vfx/hud/hud.des            41,810 B -- the HUD/cockpit source (section 75)
    vfx/menu/menu.des         150,030 B -- the station UI (section 86)
    ini/mp_rules.des           the MP rules (section 76's ANT_Rules data side)
    dat/menu/text.des          the menu string table
    dat/animation/*.des        5 boss/character animation defs (belial, crocell,
                               forneus, marax, old_belial)
    dat/version/*.des          7 files incl. locale.des
COOKED-ONLY (the ~.des that really do need descook): dat/menu/* (mainmenu, maplist,
instantfightlist, control_*, performance, menuinterface, backgroundpic, camera, cdkey,
cinematics, serverlist...), the station .sco-adjacent data, and the per-level cooked sets.
So the cooked-DES writer (section 71) is still needed -- but only for the FRONTEND MENU
family and a few others, not for story takes, fog, terrain, HUD or MP rules.

*** PROOF THAT COOKED IS JUST A BUILD ARTEFACT ***
Six files ship in BOTH forms in the same PAK:
    dat/menu/text.des + text~.des          (pak3)
    dat/animation/{belial,crocell,forneus,marax,old_belial}.des + ~.des
The plaintext is the SOURCE, the ~.des is the compiled output, and the shipped game
contains both. Combined with DES_File::ParseUncompiled this strongly suggests a mod can
ship the readable form of a cooked file and let the engine parse it -- worth testing on
text.des first, since we have a known-good pair to compare against. (Still UNTESTED in
game; do not assume it for the frontend menus until tried.)

THE FILES ARE SELF-DOCUMENTING -- the developers left their comments in. Verbatim example,
map/1n1/fog/fog.des (802 B, opens with "// DES file 'fog.des'. // Do not modify."):
    InputFile "fog"        the TGA without extension
    Opacity 0.04           weight multiplied onto each alpha value of the input TGA
    ViewingDistance 500.0  view distance in water covered by the fog
    WaterDepth 1024.0      water depth covered by the fog
    WaterBase 0.0          depth level at which the water begins
    ClosingBarrier 0.70    fraction of view distance at which fog starts going opaque
    MaximalAlpha 0.36      max alpha reached at the closing barrier
  -> This is the complete recipe for the AquaNox "look" per level, in seven numbers, with
  the meaning of each one written down by the people who wrote the engine. Section 6's
  levels JSON records fog VALUES; this is the schema behind them.

TERRAIN AUTHORING (completes section 81, which had to infer the format):
    map/1n1/terrain/terrain.des (515 B):
      [Parameter] Offset -4096,-4096,0 | Scale 4,4,4 | MinimumResolution 30.0
                  MaximumD2 100.0 | RestrictedQuadTree 1 | TriangulationMode 2
      [Tile]      NumOfTriangulationMaps 8 | NumOfTiles 1
                  [Tile0] HeightField "map\1N1\Terrain\hmap.tga"  Type 0  Transition 2
                          Texture "map\1N1\Terrain\tmap_l10.tga"  SED_CanChange 1
      [Grid]      NumOfLines 3, Line0..2 = "0,0,0"
    map/1n1/terrain/material.des (64 B): [Wrapping] W0 64.0 W1 32.0 W2 256.0 W3 8.0
      = the tiling factors of the four terrain texture layers.
  Section 81 established hmap.tga must be 24-bit, 2^n+1, square. This adds the rest:
  Offset/Scale place the heightfield in world space (here 8192x8192 units at scale 4),
  MinimumResolution/MaximumD2 drive the LOD quadtree, and the tile block names the
  heightfield and texture. A NEW MAP is therefore: paint hmap.tga + tmap, write these two
  small TEXT files, done -- no cooking anywhere in the terrain path.

CONSEQUENCES / RECIPES:
  * Rewriting mission dialogue, take keys, briefings: dat/sty/*_d.des, plain text. The
    custom-take work of sections 57/63/69 was operating on this family -- it never needed
    to be binary.
  * Changing a level's atmosphere: edit its fog.des (7 numbers). Cheapest possible visual
    mod, and it is exactly what "Nachtschicht" needs for a night-time look (section 70's
    design doc, mission 1/3) -- lower ViewingDistance, raise MaximalAlpha.
  * Editing the HUD or station UI: hud.des / menu.des, plain text (sections 75/86).
  * MP rules: ini/mp_rules.des, plain text.
  * Only the frontend menu family still needs descook.

STILL TODO: test shipping an uncooked replacement for a cooked frontend file (text.des is
the ideal candidate -- both forms exist, so a diff proves the semantics); diff the six
source/cooked pairs to derive the exact cooking rules, which would let us GENERATE cooked
files from readable text instead of byte-patching them.
